`llamaindex-cli rag --files ./docs` is the one-line "ask questions of a folder" demo that actually works; pick over [`haystack`](clis/haystack/) when reader / vector-store catalog breadth matters more than typed-socket pipeline validation, over [`txtai`](clis/txtai/) when you need integration sprawl not single-binary simplicity, over [`langgraph`](clis/langgraph/) when retrieval is primary and the agent layer is secondary, over [`langroid`](clis/langroid/) when integration depth matters more than explicit `Task` graphs; caveat — package sprawl is real (a real project's `pyproject.toml` carries 10–20 `llama-index-*` sub-packages), the 0.10 → 0.14 series broke imports repeatedly as code moved between `core` and standalone packages so pin tightly and read migration notes, and `LlamaParse` calls hosted LlamaCloud by default — swap in [`docling`](clis/docling/) / [`marker`](clis/marker/) / `pymupdf` for fully-local PDF parsing | [`llama-index`](clis/llama-index/) |
| Want **answers from a corpus of scientific PDFs with verbatim, page-anchored citations as a first-class output, not a bolt-on** — `paper-qa` is purpose-built for one task: answer a question against a folder of papers and return the answer with `(Author Year, page X)` citations back to the exact chunk it came from, and the `PQASession` object exposes the full evidence chain (which papers were searched, which chunks were retrieved, what each chunk's `summary_llm`-generated relevance summary said, which subset survived to the final synthesis prompt); the agent loop (`paper_search` → `gather_evidence` → `gen_answer`) decides when it has enough evidence and stops, optional web fallbacks via Semantic Scholar / Crossref / Google Scholar / arXiv with bring-your-own keys, and the two-tier LLM split (cheap `summary_llm` for per-chunk fan-out + expensive `llm` for final synthesis + medium `agent_llm` for search) makes 1000-paper corpus queries cost dollars instead of tens of dollars; `pqa ask "<question>"` from inside the folder is the two-command "PDFs to first cited answer" loop; pick over [`llama-index`](clis/llama-index/) / [`haystack`](clis/haystack/) / [`txtai`](clis/txtai/) when the citation guarantee is a hard requirement (lit reviews, regulatory filings, due diligence, scientific writing, expert review where "the model said it" is not enough), over a generic RAG pipeline when the search-iteration agent loop pays for itself in retrieval quality, over [`khoj`](clis/khoj/) when the corpus is dense scientific PDFs not personal notes; caveat — defaults are tuned for English scientific PDFs (HTML / scanned / non-Latin scripts will fight `pymupdf` — feed [`marker`](clis/marker/) / [`docling`](clis/docling/) output in instead), the agent loop costs 5–20 LM calls per question (a single direct RAG call is cheaper if you don't need citations), index building is single-process and not blazing fast on 10k+ paper corpora, and Semantic Scholar / Google Scholar / Crossref backends require their own API keys | [`paper-qa`](clis/paper-qa/) |
| Want a **collaborative dataset curation surface where domain experts and AI engineers iterate the *same typed `Dataset`* that your trainer / evaluator consumes** — Argilla declares record schemas (`fields=[TextField, ChatField]`, `questions=[RatingQuestion, RankingQuestion, SpanQuestion]`, `metadata`, `vectors`) once, exposes them as a Vue-3 web UI for multi-annotator review with role-based workspaces + per-user assignment policies (round-robin, overlap-N for inter-annotator agreement, custom Python rules) + per-annotator response attribution (disagreement is queryable, not collapsed), round-trips with HF Hub via `from_hub` / `to_hub` and pairs natively with [`distilabel`](clis/distilabel/) (synthetic-data generator from the same team writes records straight into a `Dataset` for human refinement before training); the **suggestions** mechanic (`record.suggestions = [{"question_name": "rating", "value": 4}]`) pre-fills LLM-judge / model-output scores so annotators only correct disagreements — the lever that turns "hire 5 annotators for a month" into "have 1 senior annotator review 1000 LLM judgments in a day"; pick when the bottleneck is dataset *quality* (gating a fine-tune / DPO run / RAG eval / safety-classifier on the *right* records reviewed by the *right* humans), pair with [`ragas`](clis/ragas/) / [`deepeval`](clis/deepeval/) / [`opik`](clis/opik/) which produce judge scores that Argilla then puts in front of humans for review; caveat — operational footprint is real (Postgres + Elasticsearch / OpenSearch + FastAPI + Redis is the floor, no SQLite mode for a one-person prototype), 2.x SDK rewrite broke 1.x recipes so pin tightly during migration, and default branch is `develop` (not `main`) — minor papercut for drive-by contributors and CI scripts that assume `main` | [`argilla`](clis/argilla/) |
| Want **one-line, dollar-accurate, framework-aware tracing for the agent zoo you already run** — `agentops.init(api_key=...)` above your `crew.kickoff()` / `Runner.run(agent)` / `graph.invoke(...)` auto-instruments [`crewai`](clis/crewai/), [`agno`](clis/agno/), OpenAI Agents SDK, LangChain, LangGraph, AutoGen / AG2, CAMEL, LlamaIndex, [`litellm`](clis/litellm/), Anthropic Computer Use, OpenAI Swarm, MultiOn, [`browser-use`](clis/browser-use/) plus raw OpenAI / Anthropic / Bedrock / Together / Cohere / Mistral / Ollama SDKs (no per-provider instrumentor import) and turns the run into a viewable trace with **per-LLM-event USD spend computed from a maintained provider price table** (~50 providers + ~200 models, including frontier reasoning tiers and provider-specific input / cached-input / output / reasoning-token pricing) — the one number stakeholders actually ask for ("how much does one customer-support session cost?") lands without you writing a token-counter wrapper around every provider's SDK; OTel-native span model under the hood means you can fan the same spans out to Jaeger / Tempo / SigNoz / Datadog / Honeycomb (set `endpoint="http://localhost:4318"`) instead of, or alongside, the AgentOps backend; the session-replay UI (full message tree + tool I/O + token counts + per-step latency waterfall) is the debugger of choice when a CrewAI / Agno run goes off the rails; pick over [`arize-phoenix`](clis/arize-phoenix/) when out-of-the-box per-event pricing + the broad framework auto-instrumentation matrix matter more than OTel-native viewer ergonomics, over [`langfuse`](clis/langfuse/) when "I run a multi-framework agent zoo and want one trace UI" beats "I want prompt management + dataset workflow first", over [`opik`](clis/opik/) when hosted control plane is acceptable; caveat — hosted control plane is the default revenue surface, the self-hosted dashboard is a separately-licensed EE repo (the MIT SDK is genuinely MIT — you can fan to your own OTel collector and get the spans without the AgentOps UI), and auto-instrumentation occasionally lags upstream framework releases (a CrewAI minor that renames a callback hook can break the wrapper for a week or two — pin both versions in production) | [`agentops`](clis/agentops/) |
| Want **self-hostable LLM observability where the spans use the upstream OpenLLMetry semantic conventions, not a vendor-shaped schema** — Langtrace's auto-instrumentors (LLM SDKs: OpenAI / Anthropic / Cohere / Groq / Mistral / Gemini / Vertex / Bedrock / Ollama / Perplexity / xAI / DeepSeek / Together / AWS / Azure OpenAI; frameworks: LangChain / LangGraph / LlamaIndex / DSPy / CrewAI / AutoGen / Vercel AI SDK / Haystack / [`litellm`](clis/litellm/) / [`pydantic-ai`](clis/pydantic-ai/); vector stores: Pinecone / Qdrant / Chroma / Weaviate / Milvus / pgvector) emit `gen_ai.system` / `gen_ai.request.model` / `gen_ai.usage.input_tokens` / `gen_ai.usage.output_tokens` / `db.system` / `db.collection.name` so the *same* spans render in Langtrace's bundled Next.js UI **and** land in Jaeger / Tempo / Honeycomb / Datadog / SigNoz / Grafana via standard OTLP — no parallel observability stack to maintain; bundled dashboard ships prompt management (versioning + deploy labels), evaluations (manual + LLM-as-judge), dataset curation (sample from production traces to build eval sets), and a playground in one self-hostable AGPL service instead of stitching [`langfuse`](clis/langfuse/) + [`promptfoo`](clis/promptfoo/) + [`argilla`](clis/argilla/); pick over [`langfuse`](clis/langfuse/) (MIT) when OTel-conformant `gen_ai.*` span shape matters more than MIT licensing — Langfuse's tracing is great but it's Langfuse-shaped, not OpenLLMetry-shaped, so cross-team correlation in a Grafana / Tempo backend is friction; pick over [`arize-phoenix`](clis/arize-phoenix/) (ELv2) when AGPL is more acceptable to your legal team than ELv2's "you may not provide as a hosted service" clause (different traps, pick the one that fits your business); pick over [`openllmetry`](clis/openllmetry/) when you want a bundled UI on top of the same span schema (OpenLLMetry is the convention library + the instrumentors; Langtrace ships convention + instrumentors + dashboard + eval / prompt / dataset UI all in one); caveat — AGPL on the server is a real trap if your plan is "fork the dashboard, run it as a hosted product for our customers, do not contribute back" (triggers AGPL §13 network copyleft); product is younger and less battle-tested than Langfuse / Phoenix on the "10k req/s production fleet" axis — pin the version and load-test ClickHouse retention before adopting at scale; SDK packages (`langtrace-python-sdk` + `@langtrase/typescript-sdk`) are Apache-2.0 in their own repos, so application code stays under whatever licence you want | [`langtrace`](clis/langtrace/) |
| Want **2× faster, 70%-less-VRAM LoRA / QLoRA / DPO / GRPO fine-tunes on a single consumer GPU** without trading away the standard HF post-training surface — `unsloth` rewrites the hot kernels (RoPE / RMSNorm / cross-entropy / fused MLP / fast LoRA matmul) of `transformers` + `peft` + `trl` in hand-tuned Triton so a 7-13B model fits on one 16-24 GB card, exposes `FastLanguageModel.get_peft_model(...)` as a drop-in input to upstream `SFTTrainer` / `DPOTrainer` / `GRPOTrainer` / `ORPOTrainer` / `KTOTrainer` (you don't learn a new trainer API — you keep the HF one and swap engines underneath), ships day-zero recipe notebooks for every new Llama-3.x / Qwen-3 / Gemma-3 / DeepSeek-R1-distill / gpt-oss release as the *primary* docs surface (100+ versioned Colab notebooks under `unsloth-notebooks/` is the documentation), defaults every knob (4-bit base, rank-16 LoRA, gradient checkpointing on, packing on, fused cross-entropy) for "this has to fit on one 24 GB card" so the framework is opinionated about the single-GPU practitioner's reality; pick over a bare `transformers` + `bitsandbytes` setup when measured 2× speedup + 70% VRAM cut on the same script is worth the kernel-level coupling, over [`axolotl`](clis/axolotl/) / `llama-factory` when you want a Python library + notebook recipes (not a YAML-config trainer wrapper), over hosted fine-tune APIs (OpenAI / Together / Fireworks fine-tune endpoints) when you want to own the weights and the data; caveat — fast path is **NVIDIA + CUDA only** (AMD / Apple Silicon fall back to vanilla HF speed), versioned tightly to specific `transformers` / `trl` / `peft` releases (a `pip install -U unsloth` may pin you to slightly older HF — check `pyproject.toml` of the release tag), beta cadence is fast (multiple `vX.Y.Z-beta` per month — pin a SHA in production training scripts) | [`unsloth`](clis/unsloth/) |
| Want **one CLI that quantises, reshards, and serves an LLM as an OpenAI-shaped HTTP endpoint** instead of stitching three separate quant / convert / serve toolchains — `lmdeploy lite auto_awq` / `lmdeploy lite calibrate` produces W4A16 / W8A8 / FP8 / INT4-KV / INT8-KV variants with bundled calibration loaders (no separate `auto-awq` / `auto-gptq` install), `lmdeploy convert` reshards weights for tensor-parallel deployment, `lmdeploy serve api_server --backend turbomind --tp N` boots the OpenAI-compatible FastAPI on `:23333` with TurboMind's persistent-batch + paged-KV scheduler underneath delivering roughly vLLM-class throughput on Llama-shape models, and the same binary covers `lmdeploy chat` for one-shot local sessions plus `lmdeploy serve gradio` for a quick demo UI; first-class day-one support for the InternLM / InternLM2 / InternLM3 / InternVL families that other inference engines treat as second-class — plus the broad open menu (Llama 2/3/3.x, Qwen 1.5/2/2.5/3, Qwen-VL, DeepSeek-V2/V3/R1, Mistral / Mixtral, Gemma 2/3, Baichuan 2, Yi, ChatGLM 2/3 / GLM-4, MiniCPM / MiniCPM-V, Phi-3, CodeLlama); pick over [`vllm`](clis/vllm/) when "the same CLI also quantises and reshards" matters and the InternLM / InternVL families are first-class, over [`llamafile`](clis/llamafile/) / [`ollama`](clis/ollama/) when you need production-grade tensor-parallel serving rather than single-binary local inference, over [`sglang`](clis/sglang/) when bundled quantisation toolkit is more important than constrained-decoding programs; caveat — TurboMind kernels are **CUDA-first** (non-NVIDIA hardware drops to slower PyTorch-eager backend), server defaults bind `0.0.0.0:23333` (firewall before exposing), vision-model BYO architectures need their own `image_processor` shipped with the HF repo | [`lmdeploy`](clis/lmdeploy/) |
| Want **Google's first-party agent SDK with composition primitives as the API and a real local trace viewer** — `adk-python` ships `LlmAgent` / `SequentialAgent` / `ParallelAgent` / `LoopAgent` (and custom `BaseAgent` subclasses overriding `_run_async_impl`) as plain Python composition primitives so graph-shaped agent topologies are code, not a YAML / JSON DSL; `adk web` boots a per-session timeline viewer that renders every event (model call, tool call, sub-agent transfer, state delta, artifact write) with full input/output payloads from the same process you're editing — closer in spirit to LangGraph Studio than to a print-statement loop; provider matrix runs Gemini-first via `google-genai` and any LiteLLM-routed backend (Anthropic / OpenAI / Mistral / Cohere / Groq / Ollama / vLLM / OpenRouter) via the `LiteLlm(...)` wrapper; deployment is a one-command `adk deploy cloud_run` / `deploy agent_engine` (Vertex managed agent runtime) / `deploy gke` against a project you control, and `adk eval` runs JSON suites of expected tool calls + final-response rubrics with `tool_trajectory_avg_score` + `response_match_score` for repeatable agent regression tests in CI; `RemoteA2aAgent` calls remote agents over the open Agent-to-Agent JSON-RPC protocol so multi-vendor agent meshes don't need custom RPC glue; pick over [`langgraph`](clis/langgraph/) when explicit composition primitives + a Vertex-shaped deploy story beat StateGraph + Postgres checkpoints, over [`crewai`](clis/crewai/) when graph topology beats role-play crews, over [`agno`](clis/agno/) when first-class GCP serving (Cloud Run / Vertex Agent Engine / GKE) matters more than `AgentOS` FastAPI runtime, over [`strands-agents`](clis/strands-agents/) when you want explicit Sequential/Parallel/Loop topology rather than model-driven loop; caveat — optimised for the GCP serving path (deploying to AWS / Azure works via the FastAPI server but you wire the runtime yourself), default `SessionService` / `MemoryService` are in-memory (production needs Vertex / DB-backed implementations), and `adk web` is localhost-only with no built-in auth (front it with a reverse proxy before exposing) | [`adk-python`](clis/adk-python/) |
| Want a **model-driven agent SDK that picks its own trajectory** — declare tools with `@tool`, hand them to `Agent`, the model decides which tool to call, when to stop, and what to return (no hand-coded state machine, no orchestration graph required for the common case); Strands Agents is closer in spirit to how `claude-code` / `codex` work internally than to LangGraph's explicit `StateGraph`s, with multi-agent primitives (`Graph` for DAG, `Swarm` for peer-to-peer with handoff, `Workflow` for declarative pipeline, `Agent2Agent` for the open A2A JSON-RPC protocol) shipping in-tree only for when single-`Agent` outgrows you; provider abstraction is an honest `Model` interface — Bedrock / Anthropic / OpenAI / LiteLLM / Ollama / llama.cpp / Mistral / Writer / SageMaker / OpenAI-compatible (vLLM / LM Studio / TGI), all subclasses of one `Model` rather than config-dict adapters; `strands-agents[otel]` exports every model + tool + agent step as OTel spans into Logfire / Langfuse / Phoenix / Honeycomb / Jaeger via OTLP without vendor-specific adapters; the published AWS Q CLI / Q Developer case studies make it the rare agent SDK with battle-tested high-throughput examples in-tree (timeouts, retries, streaming, conversation managers, sliding-window context all have answers); pick over [`langgraph`](clis/langgraph/) when "the model picks the trajectory" beats "I author the StateGraph", over [`crewai`](clis/crewai/) when single-`Agent` simplicity beats role-play crews and you graduate to `Graph` / `Swarm` only when you actually need multi-agent, over [`adk-python`](clis/adk-python/) when "model-driven loop" beats "explicit Sequential/Parallel/Loop primitives" and AWS-friendly defaults beat GCP-friendly defaults, over [`agno`](clis/agno/) when you don't want a bundled `AgentOS` FastAPI runtime; caveat — defaults assume Bedrock + AWS credentials (non-AWS users must pass `model=` explicitly or set the provider extra), the multi-agent `Graph` / `Swarm` / `Workflow` surface is newer than the single-`Agent` core (APIs in this surface have churned across recent minor versions, pin a version in production), and the tool-library extras (`strands-agents-tools` / `strands-agents-builder` / `strands-agents-cli`) ship as separate PyPI packages on independent release cadences | [`strands-agents`](clis/strands-agents/) |
| Want a **self-hostable production RAG stack with hybrid search + knowledge-graph + multi-tenant auth, brought up by one Docker command** — `r2r serve --docker --full` provisions Postgres+pgvector + Hatchet workflow engine + Unstructured parsers + the R2R FastAPI on `:7272` wired together (no day-2 Helm-chart archaeology to get something running on a single machine); `use_hybrid_search=True` flips reciprocal-rank fusion of pgvector dense + Postgres `tsvector` sparse without a re-architecture; `create_graph` + `enrich_graph` runs LLM-based entity / relation extraction across the same corpus and stores the KG in the same Postgres so subsequent `rag()` calls can fuse vector hits with KG-walked neighbors (multi-hop questions like "who reports to the CFO" get real answers, not vector-only approximations); `users` / `collections` / `groups` make multi-tenant document scoping a first-class API surface so SaaS-shaped RAG apps don't bolt a permission model on top; the `agent` endpoint runs an LLM loop with `search` and `kg_search` as tools and returns the full reasoning trace + citations (Perplexity-shaped UX, not single-shot retrieve→stuff→generate); pick over [`llama-index`](clis/llama-index/) when "production RAG server with auth + KG + hybrid search out of the box" beats "richest catalog of primitives I assemble myself", over [`paper-qa`](clis/paper-qa/) when general-purpose multi-tenant document QA beats verbatim-citation scientific PDF QA, over [`haystack`](clis/haystack/) when bundled Postgres+pgvector+Hatchet+Unstructured deploy beats typed-pipeline composition, over a hand-rolled LangChain RAG stack when "I don't want to maintain my own auth + ingestion + KG pipeline" is the value; caveat — latest tagged release `v3.6.5` lags `main` (upstream now publishes most changes via the Docker image tag — pin to a SHA or image digest in production rather than the tag), the "one command" full deploy assumes Docker (bare-metal install needs you to provision Postgres+pgvector and the Hatchet workflow engine yourself), KG enrichment is LLM-token-heavy on large corpora (budget for it explicitly and gate per collection), and the upstream also offers a hosted SaaS / enterprise tier (read `LICENSE.md` before assuming everything in the dashboard is bundled OSS) | [`r2r`](clis/r2r/) |
| Want **OpenTelemetry-native LLM observability where the SDK speaks structured-log Python and auto-instruments the Pydantic-shaped agent stack** — Logfire is OTel under the hood (dual-export to the hosted backend *and* any Jaeger / Tempo / Honeycomb / Datadog / Grafana Cloud collector via `OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_ENDPOINT` so walking away from the SaaS keeps your instrumentation), Pydantic-team-built so `pydantic-ai` Agents + `BaseModel` validation errors + `TypeAdapter` calls land with the offending field path on the span (closing the "model returned bad JSON" → "validator rejected this field" loop), `logfire.instrument_openai()` / `instrument_anthropic()` / `instrument_pydantic_ai()` / `instrument_httpx()` capture prompt + completion + tool_calls + token counts + model name + cost + latency as first-class span attributes (so trace search like "all spans where model = gpt-4o and prompt_tokens > 8000" works without manual wrapping), and the f-string-style structured logging API (`logfire.info("got {n} hits", n=len(hits))`) compiles to a templated message + typed attributes in one call site (no separate `extra={}` dict — human-readable lines and queryable structured fields fall out of the same call); the `logfire` CLI (`auth`, `projects new/use`, `whoami`, `info`, `inspect`, `run`, `clean`) handles auth + project management without a web-only flow; pick over [`langfuse`](clis/langfuse/) when OTel-conformant `gen_ai.*` semantic-convention spans + dual-export to your existing OTel collector beat a Langfuse-shaped trace schema, over [`langtrace`](clis/langtrace/) when MIT SDK on the application side + paid hosted backend beats AGPL on the server, over [`agentops`](clis/agentops/) when Pydantic-team Pydantic-AI / `BaseModel` validation tracing is the differentiator (per-event USD pricing matters less than typed-validation observability), over [`openllmetry`](clis/openllmetry/) when you want a bundled hosted product UI on top of the same OTel spans rather than just the convention library + instrumentors; caveat — hosted backend is paid SaaS beyond a generous free tier (configure a self-hosted OTel collector endpoint for pure-OSS), auto-instrumentation captures prompts and completions by default (review `capture_args` / scrubbing config before pointing at PII workloads), SDK is **Python-only** today (for Node / Go / Rust services use any standard OTel SDK and ship to the same collector) | [`logfire`](clis/logfire/) |
| Want **the database you already run (MongoDB / Postgres / MySQL / SQLite / DuckDB / Snowflake) to *be* the AI runtime** instead of standing up a parallel vector store + ETL pipeline — Superduper's `Listener` primitive declares "when this query's result set changes, apply this model and write the output here", so embeddings, classifier outputs, and LLM completions land in the same row as the source data in the same transaction (`db.apply(Listener(model=embedder, select=mongo_query, key="text", identifier="doc_embeddings"))` auto-embeds every insert and backfills existing rows once, no glue ETL); same `db.apply()` / `db["table"].like(...)` / `db.execute()` API works whether the underlying store is MongoDB or SQL, useful when prototyping on SQLite and shipping on Postgres; model plugins are thin Apache-2.0 packages (`superduper-openai`, `superduper-anthropic`, `superduper-vllm`, `superduper-llamacpp`, `superduper-transformers`, `superduper-sentence-transformers`, `superduper-jina`) so adding a backend is one subclass of `Model`, not a fork; pick over [`r2r`](clis/r2r/) when "the DB I already operate is the vector store" beats "spin up a new Postgres+pgvector+Hatchet+Unstructured stack", over [`llama-index`](clis/llama-index/) / [`haystack`](clis/haystack/) when the value is *no separate index to keep in sync* (not the richest reader / pipeline catalog), over [`chroma`](clis/chroma/) / [`qdrant`](clis/qdrant/) when the constraint is "no new database in production"; caveat — the "DB is your vector store" pitch only pays off if your DB has a usable ANN index (pgvector, Mongo Atlas Search, SQLite-VSS); plain MySQL / DuckDB falls back to brute-force search, the 0.x API has churned across recent minor versions so pin a version, and multi-tenant / RBAC is the application's job — Superduper does not impose a permission model on top of your database's | [`superduper`](clis/superduper/) |
| Want **declarative incremental compute over multimodal data — text + image + audio + video + documents — where computed columns *are* the pipeline** — Pixeltable is a Python-native table store that treats any media type as a first-class column and lets you declare `t.add_computed_column(caption=openai_chat([...]))` whose values are LLM / embedding / vision-model outputs; the runtime handles incremental recomputation, automatic caching, and on-disk versioning, so re-running on a new model = swap the function and the rows recompute incrementally without a full rebuild; `Image` / `Video` / `Audio` / `Document` are real column types with bytewise dedup, lazy decoding, and frame iterators (not "VARCHAR with a path string") — extract video frames at 1 fps as a virtual column, embed each frame with CLIP, then `where(similarity(t.frame, "person riding a bicycle") > 0.5)` searches "person riding a bicycle" across hours of video in one query; embedded Postgres + on-disk media means the whole stack ships with `pip install` (no Docker, no managed vector DB to provision before you can index); model integrations are import-paths not extras (`pixeltable.functions.openai` / `anthropic` / `huggingface` / `whisperx` / `yolox` / `sentence_transformers` / `fireworks` / `mistralai` / `together` / `replicate` / `gemini` / `groq` / `deepseek` / `bedrock`) and custom UDFs via `@pxt.udf` wrap any Python callable into a typed computed-column function; pick over [`llama-index`](clis/llama-index/) / [`haystack`](clis/haystack/) when the data is *multimodal* (video frames, image regions, audio segments) not "documents → chunks → vectors", over [`chroma`](clis/chroma/) / [`lancedb`](clis/lancedb/) when "computed-column auto-recompute on new rows" beats "I write the indexer", over [`r2r`](clis/r2r/) when single-node embedded simplicity beats Postgres+pgvector+Hatchet+Unstructured, over a notebook + DataFrames + manual caching when the bookkeeping is the actual cost; caveat — embedded-Postgres deployment is great for laptops and single-node prototypes (multi-node sharding is not yet a supported topology), the API is still 0.x and column-decorator signatures have changed across recent minor versions so pin a version, and computed-column re-runs on a new model can be expensive on large media tables — use `if_exists="ignore"` patterns when iterating model choices | [`pixeltable`](clis/pixeltable/) |
| Want **multi-LoRA inference for thousands of fine-tuned variants of one base model on a single NVIDIA GPU** — Lorax loads one base (Llama / Mistral / Qwen / Gemma / Phi) plus N rank-16 adapters that fit in MB each, the SGMV kernel applies the right adapter per request *in the same batch step* (no per-tenant deployment, no warmup), `adapter_id` in the OpenAI-compatible request body picks the variant at request time, adapter sources are HF Hub / S3 / GCS / Azure / Predibase / local with LRU caching; pick over [`vllm`](clis/vllm/) / [`sglang`](clis/sglang/) / [`lmdeploy`](clis/lmdeploy/) when serving N per-customer / per-task LoRA fine-tunes from one base (collapses serving cost from O(tenants) to O(1)), pair upstream with [`unsloth`](clis/unsloth/) for the fine-tune side (PEFT-shaped LoRA checkpoints drop in with no conversion); caveat — NVIDIA-only, Ampere+, no full-weight fine-tunes (those need one [`vllm`](clis/vllm/) deployment per variant), throughput advantage shrinks as adapter heterogeneity per batch grows, project velocity has slowed (rolling `:main` Docker tag is the canonical install — pin a SHA in production) | [`lorax`](clis/lorax/) |
| Want a **self-hostable MIT AI gateway with header-driven routing primitives** — `npx @portkey-ai/gateway` boots a Hono/TS server fronting ~200 providers (OpenAI / Anthropic / Bedrock / Vertex / Gemini / Cohere / Mistral / Groq / Together / Fireworks / DeepSeek / xAI / Perplexity / Ollama / vLLM / any OpenAI-compatible) behind one OpenAI-shaped surface; failover / load-balance / conditional routing / retries / timeouts / semantic + simple caching / virtual-key budget caps / per-provider RPM-TPM limits / per-request guardrails (PII / toxicity / schema / regex / model-graded rubric) declared per-request in headers or per-deployment in a config JSON; same binary boots on Node / Workers / Deno / Bun for sub-50 ms global edge; pick over [`litellm`](clis/litellm/) when "header-driven config + Workers-grade edge runtime" beats "Python SDK + FastAPI proxy", over [`helicone`](clis/helicone/) when self-hostable + MIT data plane beats hosted control-plane bundling, over OpenRouter when you want to own provider keys and not pay a managed margin; caveat — OSS gateway is the *data plane only* (prompt management, dataset curation, eval runs, log search, cost dashboards, virtual-key admin UI all live in the hosted commercial Portkey backend; OSS records nothing on its own — fan to [`langfuse`](clis/langfuse/) / [`arize-phoenix`](clis/arize-phoenix/) / [`openllmetry`](clis/openllmetry/) via log hooks for trace UI), provider adapter parity drifts (pin the version), semantic cache is pre-1.0 quality in OSS — review threshold + TTL before enabling on outputs where near-miss is dangerous | [`portkey-gateway`](clis/portkey-gateway/) |
| Want a **provider-agnostic guardrail library you run inside your own process** — `llm-guard` sits between your app and any LLM provider (OpenAI / Anthropic / Gemini / Bedrock / Vertex / Mistral / Cohere / Groq / Ollama / vLLM / any) and runs a configurable pipeline of 20+ input scanners (prompt injection via `deberta-v3-base-injection`, jailbreak patterns, Presidio-backed PII anonymize-with-restore, secrets, ban-substrings, ban-topics, ban-code, language detection, token-limit, toxicity, regex, gibberish) plus 15+ output scanners (bias, toxicity, malicious-URL, sensitive-info, no-refusal, factual-consistency, JSON-schema, regex, language, code-detection, relevance, reading-time) over a `(text, is_valid, risk_score)` tuple shape; `Anonymize` → call LLM → `Deanonymize` pair lets the provider never see the SSN / credit-card / email while the user still gets a coherent answer; full-local execution (HF transformer detectors, no third-party guardrail service in your data path); pick over [`deepteam`](clis/deepteam/) when the goal is *runtime defense* (every request) not *adversarial testing* (red-team campaign — the two compose: use deepteam to test your llm-guard config), over a managed product (NeMo Guardrails / Guardrails AI hosted / AWS Bedrock Guardrails) when self-hosted + MIT + no vendor in the data path matters, over [`portkey-gateway`](clis/portkey-gateway/) when you want guardrails inside your Python process not at a gateway hop; caveat — detector quality is bounded by small underlying models (novel multi-turn / obfuscated jailbreaks slip past — adversarially test your config), sequential scanner execution adds 5–50 ms per scanner on CPU (use ONNX-quantized variants + the FastAPI worker pool), `Toxicity` / `Bias` scanners encode American-English social norms and produce culturally-specific false positives in non-English contexts | [`llm-guard`](clis/llm-guard/) |
| Want **adversarial red-teaming for an LLM / RAG / agent system, with OWASP LLM Top 10 + NIST AI RMF compliance shape out of the box** — deepteam is the adversarial-evaluation sibling of [`deepeval`](clis/deepeval/): instead of asserting that the system behaves *correctly* on a known test set, it generates and runs *attacks* against the system and asserts that the system *resists* them; 50+ pre-built vulnerabilities (prompt injection, jailbreaks, PII leakage, toxicity, bias, hallucination, excessive agency, RBAC bypass, unauthorized tool use, BFLA / BOLA, …) cross-product with attack *enhancements* (linear / tree-of-attacks-with-pruning jailbreak, multi-turn crescendo, ROT-13 / Base64 / leetspeak obfuscation, prompt probing) so adding a new attack covers every vulnerability and vice versa with no glue; an LLM-as-judge metric per vulnerability returns pass/fail with a structured reason, and `vulnerability.owasp_id` / `vulnerability.nist_id` properties make compliance reports a one-liner instead of a manual mapping spreadsheet; the *target* is any callable (`def model_callback(prompt: str) -> str`) — wrap your chat endpoint, your LangChain chain, your CrewAI crew, your agent runner, or a remote HTTP API; tree-of-attacks-with-pruning multi-turn jailbreaks branch and prune adversarial paths against the target with the attacker LLM choosing which node to expand (not a hand-rolled loop); pick over [`deepeval`](clis/deepeval/) when the eval surface is *resistance to attacks*, not *correctness on a test set* (the two compose — gate the same target on both), over [`promptfoo`](clis/promptfoo/)'s `redteam` mode when an OWASP / NIST mapping + Python-callable target shape + deeper attack catalog matter more than YAML config, over [`inspect-ai`](clis/inspect-ai/) when adversarial probing of any callable beats sandboxed agent capability evals; caveat — quality of the red-team is bounded by the *attacker* model (use frontier-tier Claude Sonnet / GPT-4o / Gemini 2.x; Ollama-only attackers produce shallow attacks), token cost scales as `vulnerabilities × attacks × attacks_per_type × multi_turn_depth` (keep `attacks_per_vulnerability_type` low while iterating), LLM-as-judge metrics inherit judge bias (pair with manual spot review on borderline failures), and use only against systems you operate or have written authorization to test | [`deepteam`](clis/deepteam/) |
| Want a **minimal Python framework for building custom AI inference servers — full control over predict logic, batching, and scaling without reinventing FastAPI** — `LitServe` exposes a `LitAPI` subclass with `setup` / `decode_request` / `predict` / `encode_response` hooks; pass it to `LitServer(api, accelerator="auto", max_batch_size=16, batch_timeout=0.05, workers_per_device=2).run(port=8000)` and you get dynamic batching, GPU streaming (`yield` from `predict` for SSE token streaming), multi-worker autoscaling, OpenAI-compatible spec via the `OpenAISpec` mixin, and OpenTelemetry traces for free; pure-Python (~2 MB pip install, no Docker required), same surface serves a Llama-3 chat endpoint, a Whisper transcription endpoint, an embedding endpoint, and a classical sklearn classifier behind one process; pick over [`vllm`](clis/vllm/) / [`lmdeploy`](clis/lmdeploy/) / [`sglang`](clis/sglang/) when the endpoint is a *compound* pipeline (RAG with a re-ranker, Whisper → translate → TTS, vision → caption → embed) — one Python class instead of a service mesh — not when raw single-model tokens/sec is the target metric, over [`bentoml`](clis/bentoml/) when you want a thinner framework without `bentoml.Service` / packaging / yatai control-plane assumptions, over hand-rolled FastAPI + custom batching when "dynamic batching + multi-worker autoscaling + OpenAI-compat in 50 LOC" beats reinventing the queue; caveat — LitServe is the *framework*, not a model registry (you write the Python and bring the weights, no `lit serve llama3` shortcut), no built-in auth / rate-limiting (front it with Caddy / nginx / an API gateway before exposing publicly), `python server.py` + your own process manager is the deployment shape (no `litserve start` daemon) | [`litserve`](clis/litserve/) |
| Want **a YAML-driven fine-tuning workhorse covering the full open-weight LLM training menu under one CLI** — `axolotl` declares base model + dataset(s) + chat template + tuning method (full SFT / LoRA / QLoRA / ReLoRA / GPTQ-LoRA / DPO / KTO / ORPO / SimPO / IPO / RLOO / multi-modal SFT) + precision (bf16 / fp16 / fp8) + distribution strategy (DeepSpeed ZeRO-1/2/3, FSDP, FSDP+QLoRA, sequence parallel, multi-node) in one diffable YAML; `axolotl preprocess` caches tokenized data, `axolotl train` runs the pipeline end-to-end on the HF `transformers` + `trl` + `peft` + `accelerate` stack with sample packing / Liger / Flash-Attention-2 / xformers / paged-optimizer / gradient-checkpointing / fused cross-entropy as YAML toggles, `axolotl merge-lora` merges adapters, `axolotl evaluate` and `axolotl lm-eval` run the harness; same config scales from a single 24 GB consumer card to a multi-node H100 cluster by flipping `deepspeed:` or `fsdp_config:` blocks; covers Llama / Mistral / Qwen / Gemma / Phi / DeepSeek / Yi / Falcon / Cohere Command-R / Granite / OLMo / Pixtral / LLaVA / Idefics2 plus any HF causal-LM, with multi-modal vision+text fine-tunes first-class via `mm_plugin` configs; pick over [`unsloth`](clis/unsloth/) when "config-as-experiment + DeepSpeed/FSDP multi-node + the broadest tuning-method menu (DPO/KTO/ORPO/SimPO in-tree, not just SFT/LoRA)" beats "fastest single-GPU LoRA via custom Triton kernels" (the two are complementary on different hardware shapes — Unsloth on one consumer card, Axolotl when you have a cluster), over `llama-factory` when you prefer YAML configs over a Gradio launcher, over hosted fine-tune APIs (OpenAI / Together / Fireworks fine-tune endpoints) when you want to own the weights and the hyperparameters; caveat — **CUDA-only in practice** (flash-attn / bitsandbytes / deepspeed presume NVIDIA; ROCm experimental, Apple Silicon training unsupported), **heavy install** with sharp version pinning between torch / CUDA / flash-attn / bitsandbytes / deepspeed (use the official `axolotlai/axolotl:main-latest` Docker image to avoid a half-day of dep-pinning), the YAML surface has hundreds of keys (read `axolotl fetch examples` output before authoring a config from scratch), Axolotl trains and merges weights but does *not* serve them — pair with [`vllm`](clis/vllm/) / [`lmdeploy`](clis/lmdeploy/) / [`sglang`](clis/sglang/) / [`litserve`](clis/litserve/) for the deploy step | [`axolotl`](clis/axolotl/) |
| Want an **opinionated, API-first GenAI runtime where stack distributions (preset combinations of providers under fixed REST APIs) are the deployable unit** — `ogx` (the rebranded successor to Meta's `llama-stack`) defines `/v1/inference/chat-completion`, `/v1/agents`, `/v1/safety/run-shield`, `/v1/memory/insert`, `/v1/eval`, `/v1/post-training`, `/v1/tool-runtime` as the contract, then lets you mount **provider implementations** under each: Inference (Meta Reference, Llama API, Ollama, vLLM, llama.cpp, MLX, Together, Fireworks, Cerebras, Bedrock, Groq, SambaNova, NVIDIA NIM, Watsonx, any OpenAI-compatible), Safety (Llama Guard 3/4, Prompt Guard), Memory (FAISS, Chroma, Weaviate, Qdrant, PGVector, Milvus, SQLite-Vec), Tool Runtime (built-in Brave / Tavily / Bing search + code interpreter + MCP-server tool sources); `ogx build --distribution ollama --image-type venv` then `ogx run ~/.ogx/distributions/ollama/ollama-run.yaml` boots the whole REST surface in two commands; same client code targets local-laptop (`distribution=ollama`), single-GPU (`meta-reference-gpu`), and multi-tenant cloud (`together` / `fireworks` / `bedrock`); pick over [`bentoml`](clis/bentoml/) / [`litserve`](clis/litserve/) when "the API surface is fixed across providers so swapping Ollama for Bedrock is a YAML edit" beats "I write the FastAPI", over [`r2r`](clis/r2r/) when Safety + Agents + Memory + Eval are part of the *same* stack (not a RAG-first server), over rolling your own multi-provider router when the agent loop in `/v1/agents` already calls safety shields + memory + tools by contract; caveat — **active rebrand**: package + repo recently migrated from `llama-stack` to `ogx-ai/ogx` (legacy URLs still redirect, pip alias still installs the same package — but pin a version when citing APIs because some docs / blog posts still reference old names), the API spec is opinionated (if your serving setup already speaks raw OpenAI / Anthropic / Bedrock, ogx adds a layer rather than removes one — the win shows up at 3+ providers or 3+ APIs), provider coverage is uneven (Inference is densest; Post-Training and Eval still filling in providers — check the matrix before betting on a non-obvious combination), local heavy distributions (`meta-reference-gpu`) need the matching CUDA + torch toolchain (the `ollama` / `together` distros are the friendlier on-ramps) | [`ogx`](clis/ogx/) |
| Want a **fast, headless-browser-free URL → clean Markdown + metadata extractor for LLM data prep** — `trafilatura -u <url> --output-format markdown` strips nav / footer / ads / share widgets using a tuned algorithm (with optional `readability-lxml` and `justext` fallbacks via `--fallback`), emits text / markdown / json / csv / xml / TEI-XML with metadata (title, author, `htmldate`-extracted publication date, hostname, language, fingerprint), pairs a sitemap / RSS / Atom discovery layer with a focused in-domain crawler so `--sitemap https://example.com/sitemap.xml --list \| xargs -P 8 trafilatura -u` builds a deduplicated, language-filtered Markdown corpus of an entire site without `scrapy`; pure-`lxml` install (no Playwright, ~30 MB) benchmarks substantially faster than browser-backed extractors on static / server-rendered content; pick over [`crawl4ai`](clis/crawl4ai/) when inputs are static or server-rendered HTML and you want shell-pipeable speed without ~300 MB of browser binaries (use crawl4ai for SPAs / JS-rendered pages / MCP server publishing), over `curl \| html2text` when nav-bar pollution would inflate the token bill 3–10× and you want metadata (title / author / date / language) per document, over [`docling`](clis/docling/) / [`marker`](clis/marker/) when the input is HTML not PDF / DOCX (the two compose: trafilatura for web, docling/marker for binaries), over `scrapy` + middleware when the goal is "main content + metadata", not stealth crawling with rotating proxies; caveat — **zero JavaScript execution** (silent on SPAs that render client-side; promote to crawl4ai the moment you hit one), no schema-driven structured extraction (body text + standard metadata only — layer an LLM extractor on top for typed pulls), no MCP server surface | [`trafilatura`](clis/trafilatura/) |
| Want a **production-shaped, framework-agnostic Python serving layer that turns any ML / LLM function into one reproducible OCI image deployable from laptop to multi-pod K8s without rewriting the serving config** — `bentoml` decorates a Python class with `@bentoml.service` + `@bentoml.api`, declares Pydantic-typed inputs / outputs, and the framework generates the FastAPI / Starlette / gRPC server, adaptive micro-batching tuned by latency target (`batchable=True`), request-level GPU scheduling, OpenAPI docs, Prometheus metrics, OTel tracing, multi-replica runner topology via `bentoml.depends()`, and a Dockerfile / OCI image; the same `service.py` runs `bentoml serve` for dev, `bentoml build` packages a versioned **bento** (pinning Python + OS + CUDA + deps + service code + model refs), `bentoml containerize` produces the OCI image, `bentoml deploy` ships it to BentoCloud or any K8s cluster — the *one* artefact runs unchanged across dev / staging / prod; ships streaming SSE + OpenAI-compatible chat-completion shim for LLM endpoints, and is the substrate underneath [`openllm`](clis/openllm/); pick over [`litserve`](clis/litserve/) when reproducible build + cloud deploy + multi-model topology beats single-file simplicity (use litserve for the one-box demo, bentoml when you need versioned bentos and adaptive batching across replicas), over [`openllm`](clis/openllm/) when the workload is *not* a stock OSS LLM (custom business logic, vision + LLM pipeline, classical ML beside the LLM), over `vllm` / `sglang` / `lmdeploy` raw when you need an HTTP layer with metrics / batching / streaming around the engine (those become the inference *runner inside* a BentoML service), over [`modal`](clis/modal/) when BYO-cluster + open-source platform matter more than hosted simplicity, over hand-rolled FastAPI + Dockerfile + Helm chart when "one Python class + `bentoml build` + `bentoml deploy`" beats writing the same boilerplate per service; caveat — Python-only, non-trivial concept count (services / runners / bentos / models / IO descriptors), `enterprise/` carve-out applies only to BentoCloud not the OSS framework, BentoCloud is the funded SaaS path (you can deploy bentos to your own K8s with `bentoctl` instead) | [`bentoml`](clis/bentoml/) |
| Want a **Node-first visual canvas for LLM apps and agents — drag-drop builder + embeddable chat widget + HTTP API + MCP server publishing in one binary** — `flowise` boots a React Flow front end + Express / TypeORM back end at `localhost:3000` (`npx flowise start` or `docker run flowiseai/flowise`), persists flows as JSON in SQLite / MySQL / Postgres, and runs them on LangChain.js / LlamaIndex.js with ~200 prebuilt nodes covering chat models (OpenAI / Anthropic / Gemini / Bedrock / Azure / Mistral / Cohere / Groq / Together / Fireworks / OpenRouter / Ollama / vLLM / LM Studio / llama.cpp), embedders, vector stores (Pinecone / Chroma / Qdrant / Weaviate / Milvus / pgvector / Mongo Atlas / Redis / Astra / Upstash), document loaders (PDF / DOCX / Notion / Confluence / GitHub / Drive / S3), agents (ReAct / OpenAI-Functions / Plan&Execute / Conversational Retrieval / Hierarchical Multi-Agent Supervisor / AutoGPT-style), tools (built-in calculator / web search via Serper / Brave / Tavily / Exa, requests, SQL, Python sandbox, Playwright browser, code interpreter, OpenAPI-imported tools, MCP-server tools), and the same flow runs as a `<flowise-chatbot>` web component embed for a marketing site, an HTTP endpoint (`POST /api/v1/prediction/<flow-id>`), and an MCP server publishing the flow as a callable tool to MCP-aware agent CLIs ([`opencode`](clis/opencode/) / [`claude-code`](clis/claude-code/) / [`crush`](clis/crush/) / [`fast-agent`](clis/fast-agent/)); pick over [`langflow`](clis/langflow/) when the host stack is JavaScript / TypeScript not Python (same shape, different runtime — by language affinity), over [`mastra`](clis/mastra/) when non-engineers (PMs, prompt engineers, ops staff) need to author flows by drag-drop instead of typed TS code, over [`fast-agent`](clis/fast-agent/) when the visual canvas IS the value proposition (handing flows to non-Python / non-engineer authors) rather than MCP-purist code-first crews, over [`chainlit`](clis/chainlit/) when the deliverable is the flow editor + embeddable widget rather than a code-first Python chat UI, over hand-rolling LangChain.js + Express when "PM tweaks the flow without filing a PR" is a real requirement; caveat — **`packages/server/src/enterprise/` is under a separate commercial licence** (the rest is Apache-2.0; consult counsel or strip those files at build time before redistributing as a commercial product), node config shapes drift across releases so pin the Flowise version in production, diffing flow JSON in PRs is painful once the flow is non-trivial (the visual layer becomes a source-control liability — gate flow edits behind a review of the rendered diagram, not the JSON) | [`flowise`](clis/flowise/) |
| Want a **production-grade browser-RPA agent that hardens vision + DOM-hybrid web automation behind a real service surface** — `skyvern` ships a FastAPI server + Postgres + Helm chart + React UI + AGPL-3.0 licence; submit a goal in plain English plus a starting URL (`skyvern tasks create --url ... --goal "..."`) and the agent drives a real Chromium session (Playwright underneath) to completion: every step the model sees an annotated screenshot (numbered overlays on every interactive element) **and** the matching DOM string with the same numbering, so vision and structure agree on what "element 23" means and the agent recovers from late-loading modals / A/B variants / locale shifts that break XPath-only RPA scripts; `Workflow` YAML chains tasks, loops over CSV inputs, branches on extracted JSON, feeds outputs forward (downloaded file → next task's upload field) so "open 200 supplier portals and download last month's invoice" is one workflow, not 200 scripts; provider matrix is anything LiteLLM speaks (OpenAI / Anthropic / Gemini / Bedrock / Groq / DeepSeek / Ollama / vLLM, vision-capable strongly recommended); every step persists screenshot + DOM + action + LLM messages to the artifact store so failed runs are reproducible without re-driving the browser; multi-tenant org scoping + webhook callbacks + S3-compatible artifacts shape the deploy story closer to "VPC install" than "notebook demo"; pick over [`browser-use`](clis/browser-use/) when "self-hosted service with Helm + Postgres + multi-tenant scoping + workflow chaining" beats "code-first Python library for one-off tasks", over a hand-rolled Selenium / Playwright + LLM loop when the artifact-store + workflow-YAML + multi-step browser-RPA value proposition is the actual deliverable, over hosted RPA tools (UiPath / Automation Anywhere / Zapier) when self-hosted + AGPL + LLM-driven (not selector-recorded) wins; caveat — **AGPL-3.0** triggers network-use copyleft if you embed the service in a hosted product (use Skyvern's own cloud or self-host with non-network-use products), vision-model spend dominates the bill (a 30-step checkout = ~30 image-conditioned chat calls — budget vs. cheaper RPA on stable sites), strict bot-detection (Cloudflare Turnstile / hCaptcha / advanced fingerprinting) still blocks despite stealth defaults, and the dependency surface (Playwright + Postgres + FastAPI + React) is heavier than a one-shot agent — pick browser-use for single-page tasks | [`skyvern`](clis/skyvern/) |
| Want **OAuth-as-a-service for AI agents — one SDK that returns ready-to-call tool schemas for 1000+ SaaS toolkits to any agent runtime** — `composio` runs a tool-execution layer (hosted control plane plus self-hostable Docker / Helm) that solves the "every framework re-implements GitHub / Slack / Gmail / Jira / Notion / Linear / Stripe / GoogleDrive integrations + re-solves OAuth" problem: `composio toolkits add github` walks a per-end-user OAuth flow, then `composio.tools.get(user_id="alice@example.com", toolkits=["GITHUB","LINEAR","SLACK"])` returns the same `GITHUB_CREATE_ISSUE` action as an OpenAI tool / Anthropic tool block / Gemini function declaration / LangChain `StructuredTool` / CrewAI `BaseTool` / Vercel AI SDK `tool()` / MCP tool with consistent input/output schemas — framework hops do not require rewriting the tool layer; `composio mcp create my-stack --toolkits github,slack,linear --transport http` packages selected toolkits as a hosted MCP endpoint that any MCP-aware agent ([`opencode`](clis/opencode/) / [`claude-code`](clis/claude-code/) / [`crush`](clis/crush/) / [`fast-agent`](clis/fast-agent/) / [`pydantic-ai`](clis/pydantic-ai/)) mounts with a single URL; toolkits expose both pull-style **actions** (call this API) and push-style **triggers** (Slack message, GitHub PR opened, calendar event) so event-driven agents ("when a new PR is opened, run a code-review pipeline") use the same SDK as request/response agents; tool calls run in a per-user sandbox with rate limiting + audit logging — bad LLM output cannot exfiltrate by hitting `evil.com` from your laptop; pick over hand-rolling OAuth-per-integration when "multi-tenant agent product, do not want to build a Connect-Your-Slack flow per toolkit" is the value, over [`mcp-use`](https://github.com/) / a hand-curated MCP server when 1000+ pre-integrated toolkits beats writing each adapter, over [`langchain`](clis/langchain/)-bundled tools when per-end-user OAuth + sandboxed proxy execution + audit logging matter more than in-process function calls; caveat — hosted control plane is the default surface (fully air-gapped requires the self-hosted images plus your own Postgres + Redis), 1000+ toolkit coverage is a long tail (top SaaS APIs are excellent, obscure verticals lag), pricing meters tool calls in the hosted tier (high-volume agentic workloads should benchmark self-hosted before committing), and the `next` branch is the default — long-lived forks against `main` will drift | [`composio`](clis/composio/) |
| Want a **workspace-shaped agent platform with managed connectors that respect source-system ACLs** — `dust` is a TypeScript monorepo (Node + Next.js + Rust core, MIT, no EE carve-out) that ships the *product* shape: an admin / builder / user team workspace, managed connectors (Slack / Notion / Google Drive / GitHub / Confluence / Intercom / Salesforce / Zendesk / Snowflake) that sync incrementally via Temporal workers, capture per-document ACLs, and filter retrieval at query time so a user only sees chunks from documents they could open in the source system, hybrid (BM25 + dense) retrieval, and a typed `Dust Apps` JSON DSL (`llm` / `data_source` / `code` / `chat` / `curl` / `database` blocks) executed by a Rust core for reusable assistants you publish to channels / Slack / API; per-assistant model assignment lets the planner be Claude Opus and the worker be Mistral Small without rewriting the assistant; pick over [`flowise`](clis/flowise/) / [`langflow`](clis/langflow/) when "team workspace + ACL-respecting connectors + RBAC" beats "graph IDE for one-off agents", over [`composio`](clis/composio/) when you want the *product* (workspace + assistants + conversations + connector sync) not just OAuth-wrapped tool schemas, over hand-rolling LangChain + a doc loader matrix when ACL inheritance is a hard requirement (most "GPT over my docs" stacks ignore source-system ACLs); caveat — operational footprint is real (Postgres + Qdrant + Temporal + Redis + ElasticSearch all required for self-host), the SDK / CLI is for *embedding* and ops not "build me an agent from scratch in code", and connector coverage is a long tail (top SaaS sources are excellent, obscure verticals lag the hosted product) | [`dust`](clis/dust/) |
| Want a **self-hostable prompt CMS with environment-label deploys plus a thin observability layer** — `pezzo` is a self-hostable Nx monorepo (Node + NestJS server, Next.js console, TS + Python SDKs, Apache-2.0 across the whole repo, no EE carve-out) that treats prompts as deployable artifacts: edit in the console, publish to an environment label (`Development` / `Staging` / `Production`), and the next `pezzo.getPrompt("Foo")` invocation in your running service picks up the new version without a code deploy; settings (model + temperature + top-p + max_tokens + response_format) version with the prompt text so decoding params can't drift independently from the prompt; the `PezzoOpenAI` wrapped client records every call to Clickhouse with prompt-version + cost + latency + token counts, filterable in the console for "which prompt version regressed cost overnight"; bundled docker-compose stack (Postgres + Redis + Clickhouse + Supertokens) brings up the whole platform on one host; pick over [`langfuse`](clis/langfuse/) when prompt-deploy-label workflow is the primary value (not bundled traces + evals + datasets + playground, with a heavier ops footprint), over [`helicone`](clis/helicone/) when prompt-CMS beats one-line base-URL-swap polyglot tracing, over [`langtrace`](clis/langtrace/) when Apache-2.0 across the whole repo beats AGPL on the server, over building a "prompt deploy" pipeline on top of a config repo + custom CI when "non-engineer publishes a prompt by clicking a button" is a real product requirement; caveat — only the OpenAI provider has a first-class wrapped SDK (`PezzoOpenAI`); other providers are manually instrumented or paired with a third-party tracer, project velocity has slowed since v0.9.2 (2024-05) — pin the image SHA in production and treat as a stable mature LLMOps stack rather than a fast-moving one, observability surface is intentionally thin compared to Langfuse / Phoenix / Langtrace (no eval harness, no dataset versioning, no playground — pair with one of those when the prompt CMS is the load-bearing piece) | [`pezzo`](clis/pezzo/) |
| Want a **cross-engine, cross-instance KV-cache layer that lets vLLM / SGLang share precomputed attention state instead of recomputing it on every request** — `lmcache` is a Python + C++/CUDA library (Apache-2.0) plus an out-of-process storage backend (CPU RAM, local NVMe, optional remote Mooncake / Redis / NIXL distributed stores) that intercepts an inference engine's KV-cache reads/writes and serves cache hits for *any* shared token prefix, not just the head of a prompt — the `CacheBlend` primitive lets you cache the KV for a RAG chunk *once* and stitch it into any future request that contains that chunk regardless of position (3-10× TTFT reduction on multi-turn QA + RAG benchmarks vs vanilla prefix-cache); first-class connector for [`vllm`](clis/vllm/) (`LMCacheConnectorV1`, drop-in via `--kv-transfer-config`) and [`sglang`](clis/sglang/), so no fork of the engine is required; multi-tier storage configured in YAML with explicit per-tier capacity caps + LRU/LFU policy beats trusting the engine's opaque default; first-class production deployments via the sibling `vllm-production-stack` Helm chart for fleet-wide KV sharing across replicas (a request that lands on replica B can pull cache that was warmed by replica A) and disaggregated-prefill setups where prefill GPUs and decode GPUs are physically separate processes sharing KV via the connector; pick over relying on [`vllm`](clis/vllm/)'s built-in PagedAttention prefix cache when the workload is *agentic* / *RAG-heavy* (same chunks re-injected at variable positions across hundreds of independent requests) — built-in prefix cache only catches token-prefix matches and is per-process RAM-bound, over [`sglang`](clis/sglang/)'s RadixAttention when cross-instance + multi-tier storage matter (RadixAttention is single-engine, single-process), over hand-rolling a Redis cache around the inference layer when KV-page-aware storage + position-independent CacheBlend stitching are the actual win; caveat — connector-shaped integration means the host engine version + the LMCache version must move together (pin both in production), Mooncake / NIXL remote tiers add operational surface (run them only when fleet-wide sharing is the explicit goal), `dev` is the default branch and the release cadence is fast (latest `v0.4.4` on 2026-04-22 — pin a tag, do not track `dev` in production) | [`lmcache`](clis/lmcache/) |
| Want a **multi-agent framework whose differentiator is real-time observability via a bundled local Studio web UI** — `agentscope` pairs a typed message-passing core (`Agent` ↔ `Msg` ↔ `Pipeline`) with **AgentScope Studio** (`as_studio` boots `:5000`) where every agent's inbox, every tool call, every model prompt, every state transition streams as it runs into a navigable timeline + agent-graph + chat-per-agent view with per-step replay — multi-agent runs that would be opaque in stdout become a debuggable surface; typed message blocks (`Msg(content=[TextBlock(...), ImageBlock(url=...), AudioBlock(...)])`) carry the same shape across vision / audio / text so a multi-modal pipeline is the same `Pipeline` API as a text pipeline; the distributed runtime is built in (`RpcAgent` wraps any agent as a gRPC service so an agent society fans out across hosts — one big LLM serves judges on a GPU box, lightweight workers on CPU laptops — without rewriting the orchestrator); workflow JSON ↔ Studio drag-drop ↔ Python code triangle (a workflow drafted in the Studio canvas exports to JSON, runs via `as_workflow`, and the same JSON regenerates from a Python `Pipeline`) lets PMs and engineers meet in the middle without forking the source of truth; lifecycle hooks (`pre_reply` / `post_reply` / `pre_observe` / `post_observe`) attach guardrails / telemetry / redaction / rate limiting as decorators on the agent class instead of a separate middleware service; provider matrix covers OpenAI + Anthropic + Gemini + DashScope (Qwen / Qwen-VL / Qwen-Omni) + ZhipuAI (GLM-4 / GLM-4V) + Yi + DeepSeek + Ollama + vLLM + LiteLLM + any OpenAI-compatible base URL; pick over [`crewai`](clis/crewai/) when "live trace UI of every inter-agent message" beats "role-play crews", over [`langgraph`](clis/langgraph/) when bundled Studio + distributed gRPC runtime + multi-modal `Msg` blocks beat StateGraph + Postgres checkpoints, over [`pydantic-ai`](clis/pydantic-ai/) / [`smolagents`](clis/smolagents/) when multi-agent observability is the load-bearing concern (not single-agent typed validation or minimal LOC), over [`autogen`](https://github.com/) when first-class DashScope / ZhipuAI / Yi / DeepSeek Chinese-model defaults matter; caveat — running headless without Studio loses the strongest reason to pick it (use a thinner framework instead), the explicit model + formatter pairing (DashScope ↔ DashScopeChatFormatter, OpenAI ↔ OpenAIChatFormatter) means model swaps need a formatter swap (a learning-curve speed bump versus frameworks that hide tool-call dialect behind a single `model=` arg), distributed-runtime gRPC + asyncio debug scenarios are still less ergonomic than a single-process trace, and the Chinese docs lead the English docs by a release or two on the latest Studio features | [`agentscope`](clis/agentscope/) |
| Want a **small, hackable agentic Python coding CLI with JIT system-prompt injection and per-session model switching** — `sidekick-cli` (`pip install sidekick-cli`, MIT, v0.5.1) is a beta single-loop coding agent for Anthropic / OpenAI / Gemini that re-injects the system prompt at strategic points instead of trusting the model to remember it past 80k tokens, MCP-client config in `~/.config/sidekick.json`, in-session model switching, per-session cost/token tracking, `/yolo` to skip per-tool confirmations once you trust the run; pick over [`aider`](clis/aider/) / [`opencode`](clis/opencode/) / [`crush`](clis/crush/) when "small enough to read in an afternoon and fork" beats production polish; caveat — explicitly beta with thin tests, no sub-agents, no planner/executor split, no sandboxed exec | [`sidekick-cli`](clis/sidekick-cli/) |
| Want a **local-first voice + text AI command suite — autocorrect, transcribe, TTS, voice-edit, RAG proxy, parallel git-worktree dev — bound to system-wide hotkeys** — `agent-cli` (`uv tool install "agent-cli" -p 3.13`, MIT, v0.90.0) is a toolbox of small composable commands sharing a local LLM/ASR/TTS backend (Whisper via MLX on Apple Silicon or Faster Whisper on Linux/CUDA, Kokoro/Piper for TTS) that runs 100% offline by default with optional OpenAI/Gemini fallback; cataloged here as `agent-cli-local` to avoid naming collision; bundled `server` exposes both Wyoming and OpenAI-compatible protocols; pick over chaining a separate transcription tool + TTS tool + chat CLI + RAG server when one project that bundles ASR + TTS + chat + RAG + memory + parallel-worktree dev wins, over cloud transcription APIs when audio must never leave the machine; caveat — center of gravity is voice/text productivity not repo-coding, requires a non-trivial Python 3.13 environment with local model assets, scriptable CLIs not a TUI | [`agent-cli-local`](clis/agent-cli-local/) |
| Want a **coding-agent CLI with a 14-role virtual R&D team and a closed-loop "experience capsule" learning system across sessions** — `clawcode` (GPL-3.0, V0.1.2) fronts 200+ models via OpenAI-compatible APIs (Anthropic / OpenAI / Gemini / DeepSeek / GLM / Kimi / Ollama / GitHub Models / any OpenAI-compatible) and ships `/clawteam` (multi-role specialist orchestration — PM / architect / dev / reviewer / QA, up to 14 roles), `/designteam` (research / IxD / UI / product / visual specialists producing structured design specs), and ECAP / TECAP — Experience / Team-Experience Capsules — a portable, feedback-scored, privacy-controlled persistent memory that distills completed tasks into reusable knowledge written back automatically by `/clawteam --deep_loop`; three modes: `clawcode` (TUI), `clawcode -p "..."` (non-interactive), `clawcode -p "..." -f json` (structured output); pick over [`crewai`](clis/crewai/) / [`metagpt`](clis/metagpt/) when you want multi-role orchestration wired into a coding CLI not a framework, over single-loop coding CLIs when persistent cross-session learning matters; caveat — **GPL-3.0** copyleft restricts derivative work, large surface area (multi-role teams + capsule store + dual Python+Rust build), early-stage v0.1.x with rapid surface changes | [`clawcode`](clis/clawcode/) |
| Want **the supported successor to OpenAI's experimental Swarm — a 200-line readable multi-agent core with handoffs, agents-as-tools, guardrails, sessions, and per-step traces in one box** — `openai-agents-python` exposes four primitives (`Agent` = LLM + instructions + tools, `Runner` = the loop, `Handoff` = transfer control, `Guardrail` = input/output validators, `Session` = memory) and stops; `agent_a.handoffs=[agent_b]` exposes "transfer to agent_b" as a tool that swaps the active agent in the same conversation, `agent.as_tool(...)` exposes a peer as a callable function for triage-fan-out without transferring control; first-class MCP client (`MCPServerStdio` / `MCPServerSse` / `MCPServerStreamableHttp` with cached tool listings — one of the cleanest in the catalog); litellm extra unlocks Anthropic / Gemini / Bedrock / 100+ as a one-line model swap; pick over [`crewai`](clis/crewai/) when "two primitives I can read in 200 lines" beats "role-play crews + an evaluation CLI", over [`agency-swarm`](clis/agency-swarm/) when you don't want the agency-chart concept on top of handoffs, over [`langgraph`](clis/langgraph/) when handoffs + agents-as-tools are enough and you don't need a `StateGraph` runtime, over [`pydantic-ai`](clis/pydantic-ai/) when handoffs are the primary primitive (not typed Pydantic outputs); caveat — **tracing is on by default and ships to OpenAI's dashboard** unless you call `set_tracing_disabled(True)` or wire a non-OpenAI processor (Logfire / Langfuse / Phoenix / AgentOps / OTel) — explicitly disable before your first run if you route non-OpenAI models and don't want their inputs/outputs visible to OpenAI; opinionated toward the Responses API (non-OpenAI works but the defaults push you to OpenAI infra), Python-only (the JS / TS sibling lives in a separate repo with different ergonomics), no checkpoint / resume out of the box | [`openai-agents-python`](clis/openai-agents-python/) |
| Want **the same agent framework primitives in Python and TypeScript, plus MCP both ways (client *and* server)** — `beeai-framework` (LF AI & Data sandbox, Apache-2.0) ships `Agent` / `RequirementAgent` / `Workflow` / `Memory` in two languages with the same `ChatModel.from_name("openai:gpt-4.1")` URI scheme so a Python prototype ports line-for-line to TS for an edge runtime; `MCPTool` adapter wraps any MCP server as agent tools (client side) and `agent.serve(mcp=True)` exposes a BeeAI agent as an MCP server other clients can consume (server side) — bidirectional MCP is rare in this catalog; `Workflow` declarative composition (sequential / parallel / conditional) plus handoffs via a built-in handoff tool; first-class IBM watsonx adapter alongside OpenAI / Anthropic / Gemini / Bedrock / Groq / xAI / Ollama; OTel-native spans with no vendor dashboard wired in by default (opt-in exporters for Arize Phoenix / Langfuse / Logfire); pick over [`pydantic-ai`](clis/pydantic-ai/) when the stack is mixed Python + TS and you want one mental model, over [`langgraph`](clis/langgraph/) when bidirectional MCP + LF foundation governance beat StateGraph + Postgres checkpoints, over [`mastra`](clis/mastra/) when the Python half of your stack also needs the same framework (Mastra is TS-only), over [`adk-python`](clis/adk-python/) when watsonx + LF governance + dual-language matter more than GCP-shaped deploy story; caveat — smaller ecosystem than LangChain / LlamaIndex / agno (fewer pre-built tools, you write more adapters), TS and Python APIs are aligned in spirit but not 1:1 identical (porting requires light translation), docs improving but trail the bigger players | [`beeai-framework`](clis/beeai-framework/) |
| Want **a named role-based team of agents (CEO / CTO / engineers / copywriter) with declarative communication edges enforced by the runtime** — `agency-swarm` (MIT, v1.x on top of `openai-agents`) treats the *agency chart* as a first-class object: `agency_chart=[ceo, [ceo, cto], [ceo, cmo], [cto, eng_a]]` declares directed communication edges, the framework injects per-agent `SendMessage` tools per allowed edge and enforces them at runtime — agent A literally cannot message agent C unless an edge exists, which is the legibility win when you have many agents and want the topology visualisable; v1 line inherits openai-agents' MCP client (`MCPServerStdio` / `MCPServerSse` / `MCPServerStreamableHttp`) so any agent in the agency mounts MCP servers natively; bundled `genesis` agent helps author new agencies interactively, `create-agent-template` scaffolder bootstraps new agents with `instructions.md` + `tools/` + a stub class, and `agency.run_demo()` (Gradio) / `agency.terminal_demo()` (REPL) cover the demo surface; pick over [`crewai`](clis/crewai/) when explicit communication-edge enforcement beats role-play with implicit any-to-any messaging, over [`openai-agents-python`](clis/openai-agents-python/) when the multi-peer communication chart matters more than two-primitive minimalism (handoffs alone get unwieldy past 4-5 agents), over [`metagpt`](clis/metagpt/) when general role-based teams beat the SDLC-shaped opinionation, over [`swarms`](clis/swarms/) when one declarative chart beats picking from a catalog of named topologies; caveat — strongly OpenAI-flavoured (v1 made non-OpenAI more practical via the `litellm` extra but defaults / docs / examples assume OpenAI), legacy (pre-v1) Assistants-API line tied state to OpenAI's hosted threads, smaller community than crewai / autogen / langgraph (fewer ready-made example agencies), agency-chart is one more concept to learn on top of "what is an agent", and v1 inherits openai-agents' default-on tracing to the OpenAI dashboard — disable or redirect explicitly | [`agency-swarm`](clis/agency-swarm/) |
| Want a **wire-compatible OpenAI-shape proxy that runs prompt-injection / PII / secrets / jailbreak / hallucination detectors on every request *and* response, MIT-licensed and fully self-hostable** — `superagent` (formerly an agent framework, pivoted in 2025 to a focused security-and-compliance gateway; npm `superagent-ai`, Docker `superagentai/superagent`, MIT, `node-v0.0.9`) exposes `http://localhost:8080/v1/chat/completions`; point your existing `OpenAI` / `Anthropic` / `Bedrock` SDK at it, your application code does not change, every request is inspected against a YAML-declared detector pipeline (`prompt_injection`, `pii` with field-level redact, `ban_topics`, `secrets`, `ban_substrings`, `hallucination`, `factual_consistency`) before it reaches the upstream model, every response is inspected before it reaches the user, blocked requests return a 4xx with structured reason, flagged requests pass through with a webhook + OTel span; three deployment shapes share one policy file (sidecar proxy / in-process middleware / MCP server), the in-process detectors run locally with no third-party guardrail-as-a-service in the data path; pick over [`llm-guard`](clis/llm-guard/) when "wire-level proxy, OpenAI-shape clients unchanged" beats "in-process Python library you call yourself" (the proxy lets non-Python services use the same guard layer for free), over managed Bedrock Guardrails / NeMo Guardrails when self-hosted + MIT + zero data egress matter, over [`portkey-gateway`](clis/portkey-gateway/) when guardrails are the load-bearing concern (not multi-provider routing), over building per-detector middleware when "flip one base URL" beats writing a Django middleware per detector; caveat — the 2025 pivot from agent framework to guard means older blog posts / tutorials / starred examples (the repo has 6.5k stars largely from the framework era) describe a product that no longer exists (anchor on the current README, not search results), detector quality is bounded by the underlying classifiers (novel multi-turn jailbreaks slip past — pair with [`deepteam`](clis/deepteam/) for adversarial testing), hallucination / factual-consistency detectors require an LLM judge and add a full extra round-trip per request (enable selectively), and **do not confuse with the unrelated `superagent` HTTP client npm package** (this one is `superagent-ai`) | [`superagent`](clis/superagent/) |
| Want **long-term agent memory shaped as a temporal knowledge graph, where superseded facts retire cleanly and "what was true at time T" is a first-class query, not a re-ranking heuristic** — `zep` (Apache-2.0, Go server + Python/TS SDKs; Community Edition self-hostable via `docker compose up`, hosted Zep Cloud as an opt-in SaaS) extracts typed `(subject, predicate, object, valid_from)` triples from each `client.memory.add(session_id, messages=[...])` call into a graph layered alongside a vector index over messages and rolling LLM summaries, deduplicates against existing nodes, and marks superseded edges as `invalid_at` so a chat history of "I'm in Berlin" → "I moved to Tokyo" returns Tokyo as the current location and keeps Berlin queryable as a historical fact (instead of averaging the two by semantic similarity the way a flat vector store does); `client.memory.search(session_id, text="...", search_scope="messages|facts|summary")` answers all three retrieval shapes from one endpoint, the `memory.get()` call returns a small `(facts, summary, recent_messages)` bundle that an agent prepends to its system prompt — long-running agents (customer support, personal assistant, ops copilot) keep working at session ages of weeks or months without blowing the context window; first-class adapters for `langchain` / `llama-index` / `crewai` / `autogen` / `openai-agents` plus a community MCP server that exposes `add_memory` / `search_memory` / `get_session_facts` to any MCP-aware agent CLI ([`opencode`](clis/opencode/) / [`claude-code`](clis/claude-code/) / [`crush`](clis/crush/) / [`fast-agent`](clis/fast-agent/)); pick over [`mem0`](clis/mem0/) when temporal correctness (facts have lifetimes) beats "vector store with extraction on top" (Mem0 is the lighter sibling — pick it when one-file simplicity and no graph DB to operate matter more than the temporal model), over [`letta`](clis/letta/) when the agent has *many* sessions across many users (Letta's tiered memory blocks are tuned for one long agent run; Zep's per-session graph + multi-tenant model fits a multi-user product), over a pure vector store ([`qdrant`](clis/qdrant/) / [`chroma`](clis/chroma/) / [`weaviate`](clis/weaviate/) / [`lancedb`](clis/lancedb/)) when "memory" actually needs entity resolution, fact retirement, and temporal queries; caveat — operationally heavier than a pure vector store (Postgres required, Neo4j/Graphiti recommended for the full graph experience, the extraction pipeline is **asynchronous** so "I just wrote this fact, why doesn't search return it?" is a real foot-gun — eventual consistency, typically seconds), the repo layout is confusing (the headline `getzep/zep` repo is mostly examples and integrations; the server lives in a `community-edition/` subtree; core graph logic is in the sibling `getzep/graphiti` repo — budget time to learn the topology before deploying), and the extractor LLM call sees conversation contents (point it at a local model via the OpenAI-compatible base URL setting for sensitive workloads) | [`zep`](clis/zep/) |
| Want **open-source observability + evaluation purpose-built for AI agents, with a curate-from-traces loop in one tool**: production spans → tagged dataset → eval → CI regression gate as a single workflow, not stitched across an observability product and an eval product — `laminar` (lmnr-ai, YC S24, Apache-2.0 across the whole repo with no EE carve-out, `v0.1.43` on 2026-04-14) ships a self-hostable Postgres + Clickhouse + Rust ingestion + Next.js console stack at `localhost:5667`; one decorator (`@observe`) emits OTel spans, auto-instrumentation hooks `openai` / `anthropic` / `litellm` / `langchain` / `llamaindex` / `crewai` / `openai-agents` / `pydantic-ai` / `fast-agent` / a dozen others (install SDK + set env var, traces appear without code changes), `lmnr eval run my_eval.py` executes a Python file declaring a dataset + executor + evaluator with diff vs previous run for PR-time regression checks, the "curate from traces" workflow filters spans by tag/score/date and one-clicks them into a versioned dataset that becomes the input to the next eval (closing the loop production → eval → gate), the prompt playground compares prompts side-by-side across providers with cost + latency + token counts and "promote to production" publishes a versioned prompt the SDK fetches at runtime, and an optional browser-agent recorder stores Playwright + screenshots + DOM snapshots so a failing [`browser-use`](clis/browser-use/) / [`skyvern`](clis/skyvern/) run replays inside the trace UI; auto-instrumentation captures MCP client traffic as first-class spans alongside HTTP/LLM spans; pick over [`langfuse`](clis/langfuse/) when "Apache-2.0 across the whole repo (no EE carve-out)" + the curate-from-traces loop as one workflow beat "the most mature OSS LLM observability with the biggest ecosystem and a server EE carve-out", over [`arize-phoenix`](clis/arize-phoenix/) when self-hostable Apache-2.0 beats Elastic-2.0, over [`logfire`](clis/logfire/) when self-hosted with no per-span pricing beats hosted-only with a one-line setup, over [`openllmetry`](clis/openllmetry/) when a UI you operate beats "just OTel SDKs into your existing backend", over [`helicone`](clis/helicone/) when traces + evals + datasets in one tool beat trace + cache at the gateway hop, over [`langtrace`](clis/langtrace/) when Apache-2.0 server beats AGPL-3.0 server, over [`pezzo`](clis/pezzo/) when observability + eval is the load-bearing piece (not prompt CMS); caveat — Clickhouse in the stack is a real ops dependency (self-hosting is "spin up Postgres + Clickhouse + Rust service + Next.js console" not a single binary, so the bar to "I just want to look at one trace" is higher than `logfire` or `openllmetry`), the browser-agent recorder is the newest surface and docs lag the code, smaller community than Langfuse / Phoenix means fewer pre-built integrations for niche frameworks (write your own auto-instrumentation if your stack is exotic), and the SDK can mask span attributes by key pattern before egress (`mask_input_keys=["password","ssn"]`) so "trace everything except secrets" is a one-line config rather than a custom span processor — set it before your first run | [`laminar`](clis/laminar/) |
