| Want **one DSL that spans "POC against an OpenAI key" and "self-hosted multi-GPU fine-tune cluster" without rewriting the application** — `lazyllm` (LazyAGI, Apache-2.0, v0.7.6 on 2026-03-04) treats every model as a `Module` (`OnlineChatModule(source='openai')` for hosted APIs, `TrainableModule('internlm2-chat-7b')` for local weights LazyLLM auto-routes onto **lightllm** or **vllm**, embedding / reranker / vector-store / web-UI modules in the same shape) and composes them with a tiny flow DSL (`pipeline`, `parallel`, `switch`, `ifs`, `loop`); switching from "POC against GPT-4o" to "self-hosted InternLM on 8×A100 served by vllm + fine-tuned with LLaMA-Factory" is a one-line module swap, LazyLLM handles the launcher (`SlurmLauncher` / `K8sLauncher` / `EmpyrealLauncher`), framework selection, and a lightweight gateway that turns the multi-agent graph into one HTTP endpoint; ships `lazyllm run chatbot --model=internlm2-chat-7b` for a one-command web UI; pick over [`langgraph`](clis/langgraph/) + [`vllm`](clis/vllm/) chained separately when you genuinely need the same tool to own orchestration AND serving AND fine-tune (most frameworks punt one of those), over [`crewai`](clis/crewai/) when local-model fine-tune + Slurm/K8s launchers matter more than role-play crews, over [`agno`](clis/agno/) when one-line online↔local swap on every module beats agno's perf-first single-process focus; caveat — opinionated module taxonomy you have to live inside, China-origin online providers (Kimi / GLM / Qwen / DeepSeek / Doubao / SenseNova) are first-class while Anthropic / Bedrock / Vertex are second-class, docs assume comfort jumping between English and Chinese editions, and `TrainableModule` requires either lightllm or vllm installed | [`lazyllm`](clis/lazyllm/) |
| Want **a self-hostable visual-DAG editor for AI agents where test cases live next to the workflow itself, not in a separate test suite** — `pyspur` (PySpur-Dev, Apache-2.0, v0.1.18 on 2025-03-25) is a FastAPI + Next.js stack (`pyspur init && pyspur serve --sqlite` boots `:6080`) that lets you drag-drop LLM / tool / branch / loop / RAG / human-in-the-loop nodes — or write the same workflow as Python via `from pyspur import …` — and attaches a **test-cases panel** to every workflow: define `(input → expected output)` pairs once, every iteration runs against the whole set with diff-style comparisons in the same UI; per-node evaluation column remembers last-N runs with inputs / outputs / latency / cost, the human-in-the-loop node pauses execution waiting for an approval webhook, and `pyspur deploy` ships any workflow as a versioned REST endpoint; LiteLLM under the hood for the LLM nodes (OpenAI / Anthropic / Gemini / Groq / Mistral / Together / OpenRouter / Bedrock / any OpenAI-compatible Ollama / vLLM / LiteLLM / llama.cpp), pgvector / Chroma / Pinecone / Qdrant for the RAG nodes, generic MCP-client tool node for any third-party MCP server; pick over [`langflow`](clis/langflow/) / [`flowise`](clis/flowise/) when the regression-suite-attached-to-every-workflow pattern is the actual job-to-be-done (those leave you eyeballing "Run" output), over [`promptfoo`](clis/promptfoo/) when you need the same regression model lifted up to multi-step agent graphs not single prompts, over [`langgraph`](clis/langgraph/) when a visual editor + non-engineer authoring beats living entirely in Python; caveat — smaller community than Langflow / Flowise (release cadence infrequent — v0.1.18 from 2025-03 was still current at snapshot), Python SDK is younger than the visual editor, deploy assumes self-hosted Postgres for production | [`pyspur`](clis/pyspur/) |
| Want a **fully open-weight computer-using agent stack — terminal-native multimodal CLI plus a desktop GUI agent driving a vendor-trained vision-grounding model** — `ui-tars-desktop` (ByteDance Seed-TARS, Apache-2.0, Agent TARS CLI v0.3.0 on 2025-11-04) ships two halves of the same stack: **Agent TARS CLI** (`npm install -g @agent-tars/cli`) is a terminal-first multimodal agent that wires a planner LLM (OpenAI / Anthropic / Gemini / vLLM / SGLang / OpenRouter / Ollama / LiteLLM) + Vision + a third-party MCP tool host + an optional Web UI event-stream viewer + the **AIO Sandbox** (one Docker image bundling shell + browser + filesystem as a single MCP endpoint, so tool calls never touch the host); **UI-TARS Desktop** is the Electron app that drives the local computer or a remote VM / browser using the open-weight **UI-TARS-1.5-7B** vision-language grounder (trained specifically to translate "click the blue Submit button near the bottom" into pixel coordinates, runs on a single 24 GB GPU served by vLLM / SGLang / HF Inference / ModelScope); pick over [`browser-use`](clis/browser-use/) / [`stagehand`](clis/stagehand/) / [`skyvern`](clis/skyvern/) when DOM scraping is not an option (native macOS / Windows apps, iframes, Canvas-rendered UIs, native installers — pixel-grounded clicks reach what DOM-grounded agents cannot), over [`agent-s`](clis/agent-s/) when you want the vendor-shipped CLI + desktop app + AIO Sandbox combo (Agent-S is the academic baseline with OSWorld eval harness; UI-TARS is the productized stack with a real terminal UI and container sandbox), over OpenAI Operator / Anthropic Computer Use when "fully open weights end-to-end, no closed-frontier API on the grounding hop" matters; caveat — pixel-grounded agents are slower per step than DOM-grounded ones (Agent TARS shines for native apps + Canvas-rendered UIs, prefer DOM agents inside the browser when possible), release cadence is fast and breaking, AIO Sandbox bundle is recommended for safe execution but adds a Docker dependency, and the planner provider matrix assumes a vision-capable model on the planner hop unless you serve the open grounder separately | [`ui-tars-desktop`](clis/ui-tars-desktop/) |
| Want **a programmable container engine where the same Functions run in CI and in the agent loop, with content-addressed caching of LLM-driven steps** — `dagger` (Apache-2.0, v0.20.6) ships the `dagger` Go binary + a containerized engine; Functions in Python / TS / Go / Java / PHP are exposed both as CLI verbs (`dagger call <fn>`) and as MCP tools (`dagger mcp`), and the new `LLM` core type is a chainable graph node that runs a tool-use loop over those Functions with caching — when prompt + tools + inputs are unchanged the LLM call short-circuits on a cache hit (a property no Python-only agent framework in this catalog has); pick over [`pydantic-ai`](clis/pydantic-ai/) / [`smolagents`](clis/smolagents/) when the agent loop must share caching + secrets + container builds with an existing CI pipeline (they're the same Functions), over [`container-use`](clis/container-use/) when you need to *write* Functions yourself (container-use sits on top of Dagger but is a server, not a Function authoring surface), over [`e2b`](clis/e2b/) / [`modal`](clis/modal/) when self-hosted + caching across runs matters more than serverless cold-start time; caveat — engine pulls a multi-hundred-MB image on first use, the `LLM` surface is the newest part of the product (shape moved between 0.15 and 0.20), pure agent-framework work without a CI / build story is better served by [`pydantic-ai`](clis/pydantic-ai/) | [`dagger`](clis/dagger/) |
| Want a **genuinely local AI meeting notetaker — system + mic audio capture, on-device Whisper transcription, on-device or optional remote summarization, no SaaS bot joining the call** — `anarlog` (formerly Hyprnote, fastrepl, MIT, desktop_v1.0.24) ships a Tauri desktop app (macOS / Windows / Linux) plus a CLI escape hatch that captures system + mic audio, runs Whisper-family GGML weights locally for STT, runs a local llama.cpp GGUF for summarization (or any optional remote provider for the summarization step only — audio never leaves the device under any configuration), and stores transcripts on your disk with templates that chain summary → action items → follow-up email; ~120 MB installed, first launch downloads the chosen Whisper weights with an explicit consent prompt; pick over SaaS notetakers (Otter / Fireflies / Granola) when the requirement is "audio must never leave the device", over a hand-rolled `ffmpeg | whisper.cpp` script when you want a real GUI your team will use, over a browser extension when reliability across Zoom updates matters; caveat — quality ceiling is the local model you pick (Whisper-large + 70B local LLM is great on a Mac with enough RAM, marginal otherwise), the 2026 Hyprnote→Anarlog rename means older docs / package indexes still point at the old name, system-audio capture on Linux relies on PipeWire / PulseAudio loopback the app cannot fully automate, no multi-device sync (by design — there is no backend) | [`anarlog`](clis/anarlog/) |
| Want **the de-facto image / video generation execution substrate for LLM agents — node-graph runtime where workflows are portable JSON artefacts you can POST to an HTTP API** — `comfyui` (comfyanonymous / Comfy-Org, GPL-3.0, v0.19.3) is the reference node-graph runtime for diffusion models (SD 1.5 / SDXL / SD3 / SD3.5 / Flux / HunyuanVideo / LTX-Video / Wan / HiDream and the long tail of open image + video models); a user (or an LLM agent) designs a graph in the web UI, exports `workflow.json`, and any other ComfyUI install can re-run it bit-exactly given the same model files — agent frameworks ([`smolagents`](clis/smolagents/), [`crewai`](clis/crewai/), [`pydantic-ai`](clis/pydantic-ai/)) routinely ship a `ComfyUITool` that emits workflow JSON and POSTs to `/prompt`, polling `/history/<id>` for the result; pick over AUTOMATIC1111 / Forge / Fooocus / SD.Next when you need maximum control over the diffusion pipeline (custom samplers, multi-stage refinement, region prompting, controlnets, IP-adapter, LoRA stacks, video) or when an LLM agent will be driving generation programmatically, over hosted APIs (Replicate, Fal, Runway) when you have a GPU and want full control + no per-image cost; caveat — **GPL-3.0** rules out direct embedding in a closed-source commercial product without legal review, custom-node ecosystem is also the supply-chain attack surface (treat any custom node as untrusted code, ComfyUI-Manager's security-level toggle gates installs by trust tier), workflows are not portable across installs unless the exact custom nodes + model files are present (resolving "missing nodes" is its own minor career), and the spatial-graph UI is powerful but not learnable in 10 minutes — AUTOMATIC1111's slider-driven UI is friendlier for casual use | [`comfyui`](clis/comfyui/) |
| Want **a real-time voice-agent orchestration framework where end-to-end latency, interruption handling, and turn detection are first-class concerns** — `pipecat` (pipecat-ai, BSD-2-Clause, v1.0.0) models a voice agent as an asynchronous pipeline of `FrameProcessor` nodes that pass typed `Frame` objects (audio / text / transcription / function-call / image / control) — `Transport.input → VAD → STT → LLM → TTS → Transport.output` — so partial STT streams into partial LLM into partial TTS and the agent starts speaking before the user finishes the next word; `InterruptionFrame` propagation cleanly stops mid-sentence TTS and logs partial-spoken text back into LLM context (the failure mode that kills naïve voice agents); optional smart-turn ML model distinguishes "user finished" from "user paused" so the agent stops talking over commas; broadest provider matrix in any voice framework — Deepgram / AssemblyAI / Whisper / Azure / Google / Gladia for STT, OpenAI / Anthropic / Gemini / Groq / Together / OpenRouter / Ollama / vLLM for LLM, Cartesia / ElevenLabs / Rime / PlayHT / Piper for TTS, Daily / LiveKit / Twilio / FastAPI WebSocket for transport, all swappable as one-line `Service` class swaps; pick over [`livekit-agents`](clis/livekit-agents/) when provider portability beyond LiveKit's stack matters, over text-first frameworks ([`pydantic-ai`](clis/pydantic-ai/) / [`openai-agents-python`](clis/openai-agents-python/) / [`langgraph`](clis/langgraph/)) when the product is genuinely real-time voice / video (phone bot, in-game NPC, video call assistant, drive-through kiosk, in-car UX), over managed voice-bot platforms (Vapi / Retell / Bland) when self-hosted + open source matters; caveat — voice-first means more boilerplate for "text chatbot with optional voice" than text-first frameworks, the pipeline-of-frames mental model takes a session to internalize (everything async, everything a frame type, processors must respect frame direction), provider sprawl becomes config sprawl (typical production stack juggles credentials for transport + STT + LLM + TTS + VAD across 4-5 vendors), MCP client adapter is on the roadmap but not yet shipped in v1.0 | [`pipecat`](clis/pipecat/) |
| Want **prompts as versioned JSON artefacts that ship in your application repo, with a notebook-style local editor non-engineers can use, and an SDK that loads the same config identically from Python and TypeScript** — `aiconfig` (lastmile-ai, MIT, v1.1.32) treats the `aiconfig.json` document as the source of truth: prompts, model identity, decoding parameters, and prompt-graph chains live in the JSON (not hardcoded in source), `AIConfigRuntime.load("demo.aiconfig.json").run("translate", params={...})` resolves `{{summarize.output}}` placeholders by running upstream prompts first, the `aiconfig edit` command boots a local Next.js notebook on `:8080` where each prompt renders as a Jupyter-style cell with prompt text + model dropdown + parameters panel + Run button so non-engineers iterate without touching code, prompt edits show up as JSON diffs in PRs (legible review), and prompt regressions roll back via `git revert`; pick over [`pezzo`](clis/pezzo/) when "prompts ship in the application repo" beats "prompts ship from a server-side CMS with environment-label deploys" (no extra service to operate), over [`promptfoo`](clis/promptfoo/) when prompt *authoring + runtime* is the workflow not just *evaluation*, over hardcoded prompt strings + `git blame` archaeology when prompt iteration is a daily activity for a non-engineer collaborator; caveat — smaller community than Pezzo / Langfuse / Promptfoo, release cadence has slowed since v1.1.x stabilized, exotic providers (local llama.cpp / Bedrock / Vertex) require writing a `ModelParser` extension if not in the shipped registry, no built-in eval harness (pair with [`promptfoo`](clis/promptfoo/) or [`deepeval`](clis/deepeval/) for regression checks), and the prompt-graph model is intentionally simple — agentic behavior (tool calls, conditional branching, retries on validation failure) lives in your application code, not the config | [`aiconfig`](clis/aiconfig/) |
| Want **one MLOps + LLMOps framework that runs the same pipeline locally / on K8s / on Vertex / on SageMaker by swapping a stack abstraction, with automatic artefact versioning and lineage across both classical-ML training and LLM pipelines** — `zenml` (zenml-io, Apache-2.0, 0.94.3) wraps Python functions as `@step` and composes them into `@pipeline` DAGs; each step's typed return value materializes to the configured artefact store (local fs / S3 / GCS / Azure Blob / MinIO) with a content-addressable URI, the DAG dispatches to the configured orchestrator (local / Docker / Kubernetes / Kubeflow / Vertex / SageMaker / Airflow / Tekton / Skypilot / Databricks), and `zenml stack set <name>` swaps the entire orchestrator + artefact store + container registry + secrets backend without changing pipeline code (laptop dev → K8s staging → Vertex prod is a stack swap, not a code change); the 2024+ LLMOps surface adds `Prompt` artefact versioning, RAG-pipeline templates, `EvaluationRun` for LLM evals tracked alongside classical training runs, and integrations for [`langchain`](clis/langchain/) / [`llama-index`](clis/llama-index/) / [`litellm`](clis/litellm/) / `openai` so prompts + retrieved contexts + responses are first-class artefacts in the same lineage graph as training data + model weights; pick over hosted MLOps platforms (Vertex Pipelines / SageMaker Pipelines / Databricks Workflows) when "runs in our own cloud, lineage in our own database" is a hard requirement, over [`mlflow`](clis/mlflow/) when you need orchestration + stack swapping (not just experiment tracking — ZenML overlaps but does more), over LLM-only observability ([`langfuse`](clis/langfuse/) / [`laminar`](clis/laminar/)) when classical ML training and LLM pipelines coexist and you want one dashboard for both; caveat — non-trivial onboarding hump (typed steps + materialized artefacts + stack components versus "just write a script"), LLMOps surface is younger than the classical-ML surface and the prompt / eval / RAG primitives are still settling, ZenML Pro (hosted control plane with RBAC + Teams + multi-tenancy + audit logs) is how the project monetizes — OSS server has core pipelines + dashboard but lacks org-grade access control, stack-component sprawl means production setups carry a meaningful integration dependency surface | [`zenml`](clis/zenml/) |
| Want **a vendor-shipped headless control plane for LM Studio that boots an OpenAI-compatible local-model endpoint from a CLI** — `lms` (lmstudio-ai, MIT, 0.3.43) is the official command-line companion to the LM Studio desktop app: `lms get qwen/qwen3-coder-30b` pulls weights from the HuggingFace mirror into `~/.lmstudio/models/`, `lms load qwen3-coder-30b` allocates KV cache in the bundled llama.cpp / MLX / ONNX engine (with optional `--draft small-model` for speculative decoding), `lms server start` exposes `/v1/chat/completions` + `/v1/completions` + `/v1/embeddings` on `127.0.0.1:1234` so any catalog CLI that takes `OPENAI_BASE_URL` ([`aichat`](clis/aichat/) / [`aider`](clis/aider/) / [`shell-gpt`](clis/shell-gpt/) / [`gptme`](clis/gptme/) / [`oterm`](clis/oterm/) / [`continue`](clis/continue/)) can target it, and `lms ls` / `lms ps` / `lms log stream` make CI scripts and SSH sessions first-class peers of the GUI; pick over [`ollama`](clis/ollama/) when the team is already on LM Studio and the GUI-curated model library + Metal / CUDA / ROCm engine selection is doing real work, over [`llamafile`](clis/llamafile/) when you want a managed model directory rather than a single executable per model, over [`vllm`](clis/vllm/) / [`sglang`](clis/sglang/) when "a developer laptop running a few models" beats "a production inference server with batched throughput"; caveat — couples to the LM Studio desktop app (the CLI is a remote control, not a standalone runtime; uninstalling the app breaks `lms`), closed-source desktop bundle + open-source CLI is an unusual split that license-strict environments must audit, no first-party MCP subcommand (the served endpoint inherits the desktop app's MCP via HTTP), Linux support lags macOS / Windows in some engine builds | [`lms`](clis/lms/) |
| Want **a content-aware filesystem agent that organizes a messy directory by reading file contents, with a host-side approval step so the worst-case failure is a misnamed file rather than a `rm -rf`** — `llama-fs` (iyaja, MIT, HEAD `0a69371`) walks a target tree (the `~/Downloads` graveyard, an unsorted scan dump, an AirDrop staging area), runs mime-aware extraction (raw text → string, PDF → `pypdf2`, DOCX → `python-docx`, image → vision model or OCR fallback), batches up to N files into one LLM prompt of the form *"propose new names and a folder hierarchy that groups related files"*, and returns a JSON list of `{src, dst}` moves — the human (or `POST /commit`) approves before anything moves; `POST /watch` flips the same agent into a `watchdog`-driven daemon that classifies new files in isolation as they land (downloaded receipts auto-file under `2026/04/receipts/`, AirDropped photos under `2026/04/photos/`); the LLM never executes `mv` directly — it returns text that deterministic host code parses and applies, classic LLM-as-planner / host-as-actuator separation; pick over a general coding agent ("organize my Downloads folder" prompted into [`open-interpreter`](clis/open-interpreter/) / [`claude-code`](clis/claude-code/)) when scope-limiting the action surface to one verb (`mv`) is the safety property that matters, over commercial alternatives (Hazel) when "Apache-style permissive license" + "swap to local Ollama" + "self-hostable agent" beats polish; caveat — unmaintained-ish (no tagged releases, no PyPI package, last meaningful commit several months stale, Groq / Ollama wiring may need a small patch as model APIs drift), default Groq backend ships filenames + truncated file contents off-machine (manual swap to Ollama for sensitive directories), no conflict resolution if two files map to the same proposed name (second move overwrites silently), no built-in audit log / undo store beyond reading the JSON before calling commit | [`llama-fs`](clis/llama-fs/) |
| Want **a deliberately tiny multi-provider LLM client whose explicit goal is to "replace LangChain and LangGraph for most common use cases" — three top-level functions, native Pydantic structured output, no chain DSL, source you can read in one sitting** — `simplemind` (kennethreitz, Apache-2.0, v0.3.3) collapses the LLM-call surface to `sm.generate_text(prompt, llm_provider="anthropic", llm_model="claude-3-5-sonnet-latest")` for one round-trip, `sm.create_conversation(...)` for stateful chat (the `Conversation` is a Pydantic `BaseModel` you serialise / load to persist sessions), and `sm.generate_data(prompt, response_model=Recipe)` for native structured output via each provider's first-party schema mode (OpenAI `response_format`, Anthropic `tool_use`, Gemini `response_schema`); per-provider extras (`pip install 'simplemind[anthropic]'` / `[openai]` / `[gemini]` / `[groq]` / `[ollama]` / `[xai]`) keep the venv small, plugin hooks let you inject a system prompt or log every turn without growing the core surface, and the Reitz design tax (readable code, no clever metaclasses, every public symbol does what its name says) is the actual product; pick over [`langchain`](../) when the workload is "talk to an LLM with a Pydantic schema, multi-provider, maybe stateful" and the rest of LangChain feels like overkill, over [`instructor`](clis/instructor/) when you do not need retry-on-validation-failure (provider-native structured output is enough), over [`mirascope`](clis/mirascope/) / [`ell`](clis/ell/) when "smallest possible mental model" beats "decorator-driven typing"; caveat — slow release cadence (last tag early 2025, model id list drifts under the library), no tool / function-calling abstraction at all (any agent loop is bring-your-own), no streaming token API in the public surface (provider SDKs stream, but `sm.generate_text` is non-streaming), no async API in v0.3.3 (every call is blocking `httpx.Client`), test coverage is light — production use should treat this as "vendored code I can read and patch", not "managed dependency I trust upstream to maintain for me" | [`simplemind`](clis/simplemind/) |
| Want **a Python-superset DSL for LLM programming where token-level constraints (regex, type, vocabulary subset, length bound) are a first-class `where` clause that masks logits on local models, not a post-hoc JSON-schema retry loop** — `lmql` (eth-sri, Apache-2.0, v0.7.3, last commit 2025-05) is a query-language-shaped wrapper around HuggingFace transformers / llama.cpp / OpenAI / Anthropic / Replicate where `argmax "Q: [QUESTION] A: [ANSWER]" where len(TOKENS(ANSWER)) < 100 and ANSWER in ["yes","no","maybe"]` literally constrains decoding at the logits layer for local backends (rejection-sampling fallback for hosted chat APIs); ships `lmql run script.lmql`, `lmql playground` (browser UI on `:3000`), `lmql serve-model` (KV-cache-sharing model server); pick over [`outlines`](clis/outlines/) / [`guidance`](clis/guidance/) when "single declarative file that reads top-to-bottom" beats "Python decorators + object-mode API calls", over [`instructor`](clis/instructor/) when you have local-model logits access and want guarantees not retries; caveat — maintenance has slowed (last tag 2024-04, last commit 2025-05), rejection-sampling on hosted chat models is expensive for tight constraints, the DSL is its own grammar (not Python), no MCP, no tooling LSP / formatter for `.lmql` files | [`lmql`](clis/lmql/) |
| Want **an `aider`-class autonomous pair-programmer with a richer per-step approval TUI where every proposed file edit is a unified diff you can hand-edit before apply** — `devon` (entropy-research, AGPL-3.0, `devon-agent` 0.1.25, last commit 2025-05) splits into a Python backend (`pip install devon-agent`, exposes an HTTP server) and an Ink/React terminal UI (`npm install -g devon-tui`); the agent walks an explicit Plan / Code / Test loop, surfacing each proposed shell command and file edit to the TUI for `Ctrl-A` (approve), `Ctrl-D` (reject with free-form reason that goes back into the planner), or `Ctrl-E` (open the proposed diff in `$EDITOR` and mutate it before apply — no other catalog pair-programmer offers diff hand-editing on unapplied changes); supports Anthropic / OpenAI / Groq / Ollama / OpenAI-compatible endpoints with planner prompts tuned for Claude 3.5 Sonnet; pick over [`aider`](clis/aider/) when per-step approval + diff-mutation UX matters more than `aider`'s lighter footprint, over [`plandex`](clis/plandex/) when you want approve-each-step over long-horizon plan branching, over [`openhands`](clis/openhands/) when you want a terminal-first approval flow not a sandboxed multi-agent runtime; caveat — **AGPL-3.0** on the agent process (does not infect edited code, but matters for hosted-fork scenarios), maintenance is slow (last commit 2025-05), the Python+Node two-process split is fragile (port `:10000` collisions, version skew between `pip` and `npm` halves causes silent feature drops), planner tuning trails Claude 3.5 Sonnet so o-series / GPT-5 work but underperform, no MCP | [`devon`](clis/devon/) |
| Want **a deterministic, file-first code generator where every generated source file has a `.prompt.md` sibling committed alongside it in git, re-runnable from the prompt + Handlebars template + included-files snapshot** — `codespin` (codespin-ai, MIT, npm `codespin` 0.0.276, last commit 2025-01) is a Node CLI installed via `npm install -g codespin`; `codespin gen path/to/foo.ts.prompt.md` expands the prompt file (with `{{include path}}` / `{{includeMany glob}}` / `{{spec path}}` directives + per-file front-matter `model:` / `temperature:`) into `path/to/foo.ts`, `codespin diff` previews without writing, `codespin commit` writes + git-commits naming the prompt as the source, `codespin batch <glob>` parallelizes; supports OpenAI / Anthropic / Gemini / any OpenAI-compatible backend, no daemon, no session — each invocation is a one-shot LLM call that re-reads the prompt file; pick over [`aider`](clis/aider/) / [`devon`](clis/devon/) when "the prompt file lives in git, the generation is reproducible, no chat history to replay" matters more than autonomous multi-file edits, over [`smol-developer`](clis/smol-developer/) when you want incremental file-by-file generation (not "one prompt → whole repo" greenfield), over a chat REPL (`aichat` / `mods`) when generated files should outlive a chat session; caveat — stalled (last commit 2025-01, npm `0.0.x` versioning has never crossed an API-stability boundary so flag surface still drifts), no agent loop / test-and-iterate (re-prompt by hand if generated code does not compile), one prompt → one file (multi-file refactor is N prompts authored separately), no MCP, no TUI | [`codespin`](clis/codespin/) |
| Want **a small, readable GraphRAG implementation in one Python package — typed entity / relation extraction, personalised-PageRank traversal across the entity graph at query time, local pickled stores + `igraph`, no Neo4j** — `fast-graphrag` (circlemind-ai, MIT, HEAD `23b3a1b`) is the catalog's reference for "GraphRAG, on this laptop, against this 50-document corpus, by Friday": `instructor`-driven extraction emits `(entity, type, description)` and `(source, target, description)` tuples per chunk, dedup updates an `igraph` graph + per-node / per-edge embeddings, and `query` runs personalised PageRank from vector-seeded entities to materialise a context bundle of entities + relations + chunks scored by `entity_range_func` / `relation_range_func` / `chunk_range_func`; configurable `domain` + `example_queries` + `entity_types` knobs are the one-knob spec for the extractor prompts, and any OpenAI-compatible base URL (Ollama, vLLM, [`litellm`](clis/litellm/)) plugs in as the LLM + embedder; pick over heavyweight RAG platforms ([`r2r`](clis/r2r/), [`ragflow`](clis/ragflow/), [`docsgpt`](clis/docsgpt/)) when you want the graph layer without the platform, over plain vector RAG when entity / relation structure makes the graph walk reach further per token, over [`graphiti`](clis/graphiti/) / [`cognee`](clis/cognee/) when the workload is corpus-RAG (not agent-memory); caveat — no GitHub releases (ships from `main`), no Neo4j / property-graph back-end, no incremental delete, no UI / multi-tenant story — past a few thousand documents the right move is to keep the prompts and port storage to a real graph database | [`fast-graphrag`](clis/fast-graphrag/) |
| Want **a LangGraph-shaped multi-agent deep-research / SuperAgent harness with role-specialised subagents (planner / researcher / coder / reporter / background-investigator), sandboxed tool-use, shared memory, human-in-the-loop interrupts, and a graph-level time-travel debugger — all in one MIT repo** — `deer-flow` (bytedance, MIT, HEAD `9dc2598`) is the first open release that ships the full multi-agent split most "deep research" demos approximate as a one-shot prompt chain: a coordinator drives a typed LangGraph topology where each role has its own model selection (planner can be a frontier model while the coder runs locally), tool whitelist, and recovery policy, the React / Next.js front-end streams the graph state in real time so a human can interrupt + edit a step + resume, the FastAPI back-end exposes the run as JSON HTTP, `litellm`-routed providers (OpenAI / Anthropic / Gemini / DeepSeek / Qwen / Doubao / Ollama / vLLM / [`sglang`](clis/sglang/)) are per-role config, and a Docker-Compose / E2B sandbox tier handles the coder steps; pick over single-pass deep-research scripts ([`gpt-researcher`](clis/gpt-researcher/), [`local-deep-research`](clis/local-deep-research/)) when the workload is multi-hour, multi-tool, multi-source research that needs role specialisation + sandboxed tool-use + shared memory + human-in-the-loop, over [`langgraph`](clis/langgraph/) / [`crewai`](clis/crewai/) / [`autogen`](clis/ag2/) / [`metagpt`](clis/metagpt/) when you want a pre-built role split rather than assembling primitives, over coding-agent harnesses ([`openhands`](clis/openhands/), [`devon`](clis/devon/)) when the goal is research not interactive coding; caveat — heavy footprint (Docker Compose + FastAPI + Next.js + sandbox runtime + LangGraph dev server), running coder steps without the sandbox is unsupported and a security risk, no GitHub releases (ships from `main`) | [`deer-flow`](clis/deer-flow/) |

| Want **a notebook-to-pipeline Python framework that imposes a project template + typed `DataCatalog` + node/pipeline DAG so analytics code graduates into reproducible runs without bolting on bespoke glue** — `kedro` (kedro-org / LF AI & Data, Apache-2.0, v1.3.1) ships `kedro new --starter=spaceflights-pandas` for opinionated scaffolding, `conf/base/catalog.yml` declares typed datasets (`pandas.CSVDataset` / `spark.SparkDataset` / `partitions.PartitionedDataset` / custom `AbstractDataset`) so the same `@node` reads from local files in dev, S3 in staging, a warehouse table in prod via config-only swaps, `kedro run --pipeline=ingest --from-nodes=embed` executes / partial-reruns DAGs against pluggable runners (Sequential / Parallel / Spark), `kedro viz run` boots a `:4141` lineage UI, and plugins (`kedro-airflow` / `kedro-mlflow` / `kedro-databricks` / `kedro-azureml` / `kedro-vertexai` / `kedro-sagemaker`) translate the project into the target platform's native job spec; pick over [`dagster`](clis/dagster/) when "structure my Python project + typed catalog" beats "asset-graph orchestrator with sensors + partitioned backfills", over [`zenml`](clis/zenml/) when you want the project template + data catalog without the LLMOps dashboard, over [`mlflow`](clis/mlflow/) when pipeline structure (not just experiment tracking) is the actual gap; caveat — opt-in `kedro-telemetry` plugin prompts on first invoke (decline writes `consent: false`, `DO_NOT_TRACK=1` honoured), narrower than dagster on scheduling / event-driven sensors (it is not the production scheduler — pair with Airflow / Argo via plugin), the `conf/base/` + `conf/local/` + `parameters.yml` + `catalog.yml` layout is opinionated and cuts both ways | [`kedro`](clis/kedro/) |
| Want **a flow-shaped, decorator-driven Python ML framework with first-class GPU + HPC dispatch (`@batch` / `@kubernetes` / `@nvidia`) and automatic artifact-by-default versioning + `resume` from the failed step** — `metaflow` (Netflix, Apache-2.0, v2.19.22) models a workflow as a `FlowSpec` subclass whose `@step` methods are wired by `self.next(...)`, every `self.x` assignment in any step automatically materialises to the configured artifact store (local fs / S3 / Azure Blob / GCS) keyed by run-id + step-name (queryable later as `Run('TrainFlow/123').data.x` for time-travel debugging), `python myflow.py run` dispatches the DAG to a local subprocess pool / AWS Batch / Kubernetes / Argo Workflows / Step Functions / Airflow with the same source, `@retry(times=2)` + `@batch(gpu=1, memory=60000, image='pytorch/pytorch:2.5.1-cuda12.4')` + `foreach='shards'` fans a fine-tune across hundreds of GPUs with one decorator change, `python myflow.py resume` re-executes from the failed step using cached upstream artifacts, and `@card` / `@checkpoint` / `@huggingface_hub` / `@pypi` / `@conda` are the in-tree extension points; pick over [`dagster`](clis/dagster/) / [`zenml`](clis/zenml/) when GPU / HPC dispatch + "every variable is a versioned artifact for free" matters more than asset-graph orchestration or LLMOps dashboards, over [`kedro`](clis/kedro/) when you want decorator-driven flow code (not project-template + YAML catalog), over [`skypilot`](clis/skypilot/) when you want a workflow framework around the GPU dispatch (not a bare cluster launcher); caveat — OSS package emits no telemetry but cloud mode requires standing up an artifact store + metadata service + compute backend (the hosted Outerbounds offering is the managed path), `foreach` width is determined at runtime not statically, no asset-shaped sensor / partitioned-backfill UI, no first-party MCP — agent / LLM use happens inside `@step` containers | [`metaflow`](clis/metaflow/) |

caveat — anonymous usage telemetry on by default in OSS (Sentry-shaped runtime errors + counts of pipeline runs / destinations / sources, no row content / no schemas / no credentials; opt-out via `[runtime] dlthub_telemetry = false` in `config.toml` or `DLT_TELEMETRY=false`), batch-oriented (minute / hour cadence; sub-second CDC is [Debezium](https://debezium.io/) + Kafka territory), default branch is `devel` not `main` | [`dlt`](clis/dlt/) |
| Want **a pandas / polars-shaped Python library + CLI for building, versioning, and querying multimodal datasets (images / video / audio / PDFs / text) backed by a SQLite (or Postgres / ClickHouse) catalog while the bytes stay in S3 / GCS / Azure / local FS by content-addressed manifest** — `datachain` (datachain-ai, Apache-2.0, v0.54.0) is the data-engineering peer to [`dvc`](https://dvc.org) for the unstructured-data + LLM-annotation era: a `Chain` is a lazy DAG of `gen` (1→N) / `map` (1→1) / `filter` / `agg` / `save` operators that compile to one query plan, so adding "caption every image with GPT-4o, then keep only the captions mentioning a dog" is a 10-line script that fans out across a thread pool, checkpoints to the catalog, and survives an OOM mid-run by resuming from the last persisted version; UDFs declare input columns with type hints, Pydantic models become row schemas, the result is one diffable `my_dataset.py` per dataset version that a coding-agent CLI can author and re-run, and a 10 TB image corpus enriched with CLIP embeddings + GPT-4o captions versions in seconds without copying bytes; pick over [`fiftyone`](clis/fiftyone/) when headless / SQL-shaped data engineering beats App-in-the-loop visual exploration, over hand-rolling `pandas.read_parquet` + boto3 + `tqdm` when schema typing + version checkpoints + content-addressed manifests are the hidden costs, over heavyweight ETL platforms when a `pip install` and a Python file is the desired ergonomic; caveat — Iterative-style anonymous usage telemetry on by default (opt-out via `STUDIO_TOKEN` config or `DATACHAIN_NO_ANALYTICS=1`), the catalog is single-process SQLite by default (Postgres / ClickHouse for team scale), and concurrent writers to the same dataset version need an external lock | [`datachain`](clis/datachain/) |

| Want **a Python-native workflow orchestrator where the same `@flow` you call interactively in a notebook is what the scheduler runs in production, with retries / caching / concurrency as decorator kwargs and a local-SQLite → managed-Postgres upgrade path that is a connection-string swap** — `prefect` (PrefectHQ, Apache-2.0, v3.6.28) is the decorator-shaped peer to [`dagster`](clis/dagster/) for AI / data pipelines that don't justify Airflow: `prefect server start` boots a local SQLite-backed UI / API at `127.0.0.1:4200`, `prefect deploy ./my_flow.py:my_flow -n hourly --cron "0 * * * *" --pool default` registers a deployment with a schedule + parameters, `prefect worker start --pool default` polls and runs it, `prefect flow-run logs <id> --tail` streams logs back; retries / timeouts / caching / concurrency are decorator kwargs (`@task(retries=3, retry_delay_seconds=10, cache_key_fn=…)`), sub-flows are nested `@flow` calls, and `task.submit()` against `ConcurrentTaskRunner` / `DaskTaskRunner` / `RayTaskRunner` is the parallelism story; pick over [`dagster`](clis/dagster/) when your team thinks in functions not assets, over [`metaflow`](clis/metaflow/) when decorator + async beats class + `step` methods, over [`modal`](clis/modal/) when you orchestrate wherever you already run (laptop / ECS / k8s / Modal itself — a `@flow` whose tasks call `modal.Function.lookup(...).remote(...)` is the compose path); caveat — v3.x rewrote the engine and replaced `agent` with pull-based `worker` pools (any tutorial that says `prefect agent start` is v2 and will not work), the OSS server's default SQLite is fine for one operator but move to Postgres before sharing the URL, deployments without a running worker in their pool sit in `Late` forever, and `prefect cloud login` opts into Prefect's SaaS metadata plane so pick deliberately | [`prefect`](clis/prefect/) |

`Ctrl+Enter` runs the current statement, `F2` renames a buffer tab, `Ctrl+E` exports the result grid, `F1` is the keymap, `Ctrl+Q` quits; pick over [`datasette`](clis/datasette/) when you need a *write-capable* terminal IDE for the operator (datasette is read-only HTTP for sharing data with a browser audience), over raw `duckdb` / `psql` REPLs when you want a schema tree + multi-buffer editor + results pagination + cross-engine parity, over a JetBrains DataGrip licence when 80×24 over SSH is the only surface available; pairs naturally with the LLM SQL tools — let [`vanna`](clis/vanna/) / [`wrenai`](clis/wrenai/) draft the query, paste into `harlequin` to inspect the plan and the rows; caveat — adapters are extras (`pip install harlequin` alone gets you DuckDB only; "no Postgres driver" on first connect almost always means you skipped `pip install 'harlequin[postgres]'`), the TUI assumes a terminal with at least 80 columns and true-color (`xterm-256color` works, schema tree gets cramped under ~100 cols), and BigQuery / Snowflake adapters use those vendors' Python clients so auth is whatever those clients expect (ADC, `~/.snowflake/config`) — `harlequin` itself stores no credentials | [`harlequin`](clis/harlequin/) |
| Want **the shortest path from "I have a file" to "I have a queryable database" — a Swiss-army CLI for SQLite that ingests CSV / JSON / NDJSON / Parquet into typed tables in one command, transforms schemas in place, runs queries with JSON output, and ships the resulting `.db` anywhere** — `sqlite-utils` (simonw, Apache-2.0, v3.39) is the upstream loader and schema-mutator that produces the `.db` other tools open: `sqlite-utils insert mydata.db events events.csv --csv --detect-types` creates the table, infers column types, and bulk-inserts in one call; `sqlite-utils memory access.csv "select status, count(*) from t group by 1"` runs SQL against a CSV without ever materializing a file (each arg becomes a table named `t`, `t1`, `t2`, …); `sqlite-utils transform logs.db requests --add latency_ms integer` mutates schemas in place (rebuilding under the hood since SQLite has no full `ALTER`); `sqlite-utils enable-fts logs.db requests url user_agent --create-triggers` adds an FTS5 index that stays in sync on insert / update / delete; output of any query defaults to JSON (`--csv`, `--tsv`, `--nl`, `--table` available) so it composes with `jq` and any LLM CLI; pick over [`datasette`](clis/datasette/) for the *write* side (same author, same data model — they compose: `sqlite-utils insert ... && datasette serve ...`), over [`harlequin`](clis/harlequin/) for non-interactive scripts and CI (harlequin is for exploration), over hand-rolled Python for the canonical `sqlite-utils insert prompts.db runs out.json` pattern that lands an LLM CLI's JSON output (e.g. [`llm`](clis/llm/)'s `~/.config/io.datasette.llm/logs.db`) into a queryable log without writing any code; caveat — type inference on `--csv` is best-effort and picks `TEXT` when in doubt (mixed columns need `--detect-types` and a `sqlite-utils schema` verify), `transform` rebuilds the table so on a multi-GB table it's slow and needs free disk equal to the table size, `enable-fts` with `--create-triggers` makes writes ~2× slower (rebuild periodically with `populate-fts` for write-heavy workloads), and the CLI talks to SQLite only — the sister project `db-to-sqlite` handles Postgres / MySQL ingestion | [`sqlite-utils`](clis/sqlite-utils/) |
| Want **the in-process columnar OLAP engine as a single ~30 MB static binary that scans CSV / Parquet / JSON / Arrow / Iceberg / Delta directly from local disk, S3, GCS, Azure Blob, or HTTPS with predicate pushdown and projection pruning — and answers SQL over billions of rows on a laptop in seconds, no server, no daemon, no driver matrix** — `duckdb` (duckdb, MIT, v1.5.2) is the analytics substrate other entries in this catalog ([`harlequin`](clis/harlequin/), [`dlt`](clis/dlt/), [`datachain`](clis/datachain/)) read and write directly: `duckdb -c "select count(*) from 's3://bucket/*.parquet'"` runs a one-shot query against remote Parquet without ingesting a single row first, `duckdb -json -c "select * from read_csv_auto('events.csv') limit 10"` is the streaming-CSV-to-JSON Unix filter that makes it shell-composable, `duckdb my.db` opens (or creates) a database file and drops you into a vectorized REPL, the bundled `httpfs` / `aws` / `azure` / `iceberg` / `delta` extensions let SQL touch object storage natively, and the same engine binary backs the Python / Node / Rust / R / Java / JDBC clients so the file moves between languages without conversion; pick over [`sqlite-utils`](clis/sqlite-utils/) when the workload is OLAP aggregation (column-store, vectorized) not OLTP key-value (row-store), over [`harlequin`](clis/harlequin/) for non-interactive scripts and pipelines (harlequin is the TUI front-end *for* this engine and seven others), over [`datasette`](clis/datasette/) when you are the analyst doing the computation (datasette is for read-only HTTP sharing with a browser audience), over warehouse CLIs (Snowflake / BigQuery `bq`) for the dev / preview / local tier where you do not want to pay per query; caveat — embedded single-process (no concurrent writers, do not share a `.duckdb` file over NFS), `httpfs` / `aws` / `azure` extensions are lazy-loaded on first use so CI environments that wipe `~/.duckdb/extensions` pay the download every run (pre-bake an image), `read_csv_auto` infers types from the first ~20K rows and casts mixed columns to `VARCHAR` (use explicit `read_csv(columns = {...})` for canonical schemas), and on-disk format is stable within a major version line but not guaranteed across major versions (use `EXPORT DATABASE` / `IMPORT DATABASE` for archives, or just keep the source Parquet) | [`duckdb`](clis/duckdb/) |
| Want **the dialect-aware SQL linter and auto-formatter for the modern data stack — a `prettier` / `ruff` for SQL that parses each file into a real per-dialect syntax tree, applies a configurable rule set, and rewrites the file in place** — `sqlfluff` (sqlfluff, MIT, v4.1.0) covers Postgres / BigQuery / Snowflake / Redshift / Databricks / DuckDB / Trino / Athena / Spark / T-SQL / Teradata / MySQL / SQLite / Hive / Exasol / Materialize / ClickHouse from one binary: `sqlfluff lint queries/` walks a tree and emits one parseable finding per line for CI (`L010` keyword case, `L036` SELECT-target newline, `L042` subquery-inside-JOIN, …), `sqlfluff fix queries/ --force` rewrites in place with the same rule set so the diff is reviewable in a PR, `sqlfluff parse query.sql` dumps the parse tree for debugging, the dialect is per-project in `.sqlfluff`, and **templater** plugins (`jinja`, `dbt`, `python`, `placeholder`) render the file before parsing so it lints `dbt` models with `{{ ref('foo') }}` and parameterized queries with `:user_id` correctly, not as syntax errors; pick over an LLM-based reviewer ([`code-review-gpt`](clis/code-review-gpt/)) for the deterministic dialect-aware surface (LLM catches the semantic bugs, sqlfluff catches the policy + syntax — run both), over [`harlequin`](clis/harlequin/) when the goal is *grading* SQL in CI not running it interactively, over [`sqlite-utils`](clis/sqlite-utils/) when you are checking SQL text against a style guide not manipulating a SQLite database, paired with LLM SQL generators ([`vanna`](clis/vanna/), [`wrenai`](clis/wrenai/)) it catches the dialect-specific footguns the model does not know about; caveat — rules are opinionated (expect to disable 5–10 of the default set or restrict to `rules = core`), the Jinja / dbt templaters render before parsing (rendering failures show as templating errors not SQL errors — use `sqlfluff render` to debug), `fix` rewrites files in place (run inside a clean git tree or use `--check` first to preview), and the per-dialect LL parser is slower than `flake8`-class linters on large trees (~1s per 100 medium files — cache via `pre-commit` rather than re-linting the whole tree on every save) | [`sqlfluff`](clis/sqlfluff/) |
| Want **operational BI as code — a single Go binary that takes Parquet / CSV / DuckDB / ClickHouse / Druid / Pinot / BigQuery / Snowflake / Postgres sources, declarative YAML metrics-view + dashboard definitions, and serves a sub-second OLAP UI on `localhost:9009` with no warehouse, no JS build, and no separate semantic layer** — `rill` (rilldata, Apache-2.0, v0.85.3) is the dashboard layer for the AI / data pipelines in this catalog ([`dlt`](clis/dlt/), [`prefect`](clis/prefect/), [`dagster`](clis/dagster/), [`metaflow`](clis/metaflow/)) that land their output as Parquet / DuckDB / warehouse tables: `rill start my-project` reads `sources/*.yaml`, `models/*.sql`, and `dashboards/*.yaml`, wires them through a bundled DuckDB engine (default) or a remote OLAP target, and boots a fast operational dashboard with time-series, dimension drill-downs, leaderboards, and time-comparison derived from the metrics view; definitions are *files in git* — a **source** is a YAML connector spec, a **model** is a `.sql` file in the connector's dialect, a metrics view declares dimensions / measures / time grain — and edits hot-reload the running UI; `rill deploy` ships the same project to managed Rill Cloud where the dashboard is shareable while the OSS binary works fully offline; pick over [`harlequin`](clis/harlequin/) when the deliverable is a persistent reviewable dashboard not ad-hoc SQL exploration (use harlequin to prototype the SQL, then promote into a `rill` model), over [`datasette`](clis/datasette/) when the data shape is OLAP (time-series + dimensions + measures) not document / transactional, over [`evidently`](clis/evidently/) / [`trulens`](clis/trulens/) when you author the metrics yourself (those ship built-in ML / LLM observability metrics; `rill` is generic BI), over Superset / Metabase when single-binary code-first sub-second-on-a-laptop beats multi-user permissioned UI-authored dashboards; caveat — OSS binary stores no auth (UI binds on localhost by default; exposing it requires a reverse proxy with auth, or promotion to Rill Cloud), anonymous telemetry on by default (`rill telemetry disable` writes opt-out to `~/.rill/config.yaml`, or `RILL_TELEMETRY=0`), metrics views inherit the SQL dialect of the underlying connector (DuckDB SQL on a DuckDB source, ClickHouse SQL on ClickHouse — keep models per-connector), and hot-reload watches the project tree (network mounts with weak FS event support won't propagate edits without restarting `rill start`) | [`rill`](clis/rill/) |
| Want **the keyboard-first API client that lives in your terminal — a Textual TUI for HTTP / REST / GraphQL exploration that runs inside `tmux` / SSH and stores collections as plain YAML in git instead of locked behind a SaaS sync product (Postman Cloud, Insomnia sync, vendor-specific `.bru`)** — `posting` (darrenburns, Apache-2.0, v2.10.0) opens a multi-pane Textual UI (collection tree + request editor with method / URL / params / headers / body / auth / scripts + response viewer with status + headers + syntax-highlighted body + cookies); `Ctrl+J` sends, `Ctrl+T` opens a new tab, `Ctrl+P` is a fuzzy command palette over every action, `Ctrl+S` saves the request as a `.posting.yaml` file that diffs cleanly in PRs, `--collection ./api --env ./api/staging.env` loads a directory of YAML requests with a typed env overlay so `{{API_KEY}}` and `{{BASE_URL}}` interpolate correctly across staging / prod without copying secrets into the request body, `pre_request` / `post_response` Python script hooks per request let you sign requests or assert on the response inline; pick over raw `curl` / `httpie` for the persistent multi-tab editor + history + environments + response pretty-printing (use `curl` once the request is baked into a script), over Postman / Insomnia when you are already inside a terminal session debugging a service and the desktop-app context-switch is friction, over [`elia`](clis/elia/) / [`oterm`](clis/oterm/) when the goal is debugging an HTTP API not chatting against an LLM (those are TUI chat clients for models, this is a TUI HTTP client for any API); caveat — TUI-first with no headless one-shot mode for CI (pair with `httpie` / `curl` against the same env vars for CI runs), the YAML format is `posting`-specific and Postman / Insomnia collections do not import directly (budget time for a one-time migration), `pre_request` / `post_response` hooks execute arbitrary Python from the YAML so treat collections as code for review purposes, and the TUI assumes a true-color terminal with at least 80 columns (request editor pane gets cramped under ~100 cols) | [`posting`](clis/posting/) |
| Want **the "import-linter that you actually adopt" for Python — a CLI that parses your codebase into a module graph, lets you bootstrap the *current* dependency edges as the baseline in one command, and fails CI on any new undeclared cross-module import** — `tach` (gauge-sh, MIT, v0.34.1) gives a growing Python codebase the layered-architecture hygiene Java / Go / Rust have by default: `tach mod` walks the project and writes a `tach.toml` listing every top-level module, `tach sync` infers the current dependency edges and records them as the allowed baseline, `tach check` parses every Python file via the PyO3-bound Rust core (tens of thousands of files per second), resolves every `import`, and exits non-zero with one finding per offending line if a module imports another module not on its `depends_on` list — edges are declared in TOML so a "module X may now depend on module Y" change is one line for review in a PR; the same binary supports `tach show --format mermaid` (graph export for documentation), `tach report` (per-module incoming / outgoing dependency tables), and `tach test --base origin/main` (run only the tests downstream of the diff — the Python answer to `bazel query`); pick over `import-linter` when the friction of writing a contract DSL before seeing any value is what has stopped your team adopting it (`tach sync` infers the rule from current behaviour, you accept-or-edit), over [`ast-grep`](clis/ast-grep/) when the goal is enforcing module boundaries not finding / rewriting code patterns, over [`grep-ast`](clis/grep-ast/) / [`symbex`](clis/symbex/) when the question is *which symbols may import which* not *find this symbol*; caveat — static analysis only (does not see `importlib.import_module(name)` with runtime-built names, `__import__(...)` inside a function, monkey-patched re-exports — pair with runtime monitoring if dynamic imports are load-bearing), default `tach mod` granularity is top-level modules (enforcing submodule boundaries needs explicit nested entries in `tach.toml`), `tach test --base <ref>` requires git history available in CI (shallow clones with `git clone --depth=1` defeat it — fetch the merge base), and the project is pre-1.0 so pin the version in `pyproject.toml` and review release notes before bumping minors | [`tach`](clis/tach/) |
| Want **the Rust-rewrite-of-the-JS-toolchain story — a near-drop-in `eslint` replacement that runs 50–100× faster across ~500 ported rules, with the same parser / semantic / resolver / formatter / minifier / transformer crates available as building blocks for downstream Rust-native bundlers** — `oxc` (oxc-project, MIT, apps_v1.61.0) ships `oxlint` as the flagship: `npx oxlint .` walks the repo, runs the ported `eslint` / `eslint-plugin-{import, jsdoc, jest, react, react-hooks, typescript, unicorn, jsx-a11y, n, oxc, vitest, promise}` rules in parallel across all CPU cores against the Rust-parsed AST, and finishes in seconds where `eslint` takes minutes (a 50-file PR that cost 8 s with `eslint` costs 0.1 s with `oxlint`, so the linter runs on every save instead of every CI push); `npx oxlint . --fix` auto-fixes ~40% of rules safely, `npx oxlint . --format json --deny-warnings > oxlint.json` is the CI-shaped invocation, and the broader `oxc_*` crate set (`oxc_parser`, `oxc_ast`, `oxc_semantic`, `oxc_resolver`, `oxc_minifier`, `oxc_transformer`) is the building blocks Rolldown / Rspack / Tsdown / Rstest / the Void Zero stack consume rather than wrapping `@babel/parser` / `typescript`; pick over [`ast-grep`](clis/ast-grep/) when the goal is the standard eslint-shaped lint pass at speed not custom codemod-shaped queries (use `ast-grep` for the latter), over LLM-driven reviewers ([`code-review-gpt`](clis/code-review-gpt/), [`pr-agent`](clis/pr-agent/)) for the deterministic fast gate (run `oxlint` first as the cheap gate, LLM reviewer second on what survives), over `eslint` itself for the ~50–100× throughput on the rules `oxlint` has ported (and run `oxlint` *alongside* `eslint` for the unported tail — different config files, no clash); caveat — rule coverage gap (the long tail of `eslint` plugins, especially internal company rule packages, is not portable — audit your `eslint` config before assuming a full swap), autofix coverage is partial (~40% of rules — plan a manual cleanup pass on first adoption), formatter `oxfmt` is pre-1.0 (targets `prettier`-output compatibility but the format-stability guarantee `prettier` users expect is not yet there), type-aware lint rules that need type information (`no-floating-promises`, `no-misused-promises`) still need `tsc --noEmit` or the full `typescript-eslint` chain (the syntactic `oxlint` does not have type info — `oxc_type_synthesis` is roadmap), and monorepo config discovery is per-file (no global `extends:` cascading from a workspace root by default — pass `-c <root>/.oxlintrc.json` explicitly) | [`oxc`](clis/oxc/) |
| Want **the `psql`-shaped REPL for the polyglot-database era — one Go binary that connects to ~50 different SQL engines (Postgres, MySQL, SQLite, ClickHouse, DuckDB, Snowflake, BigQuery, Trino, CockroachDB, Vertica, Cassandra, MongoDB, Redis, Oracle, SQL Server, and ~30 more) via `driver://user:pass@host/db` URLs and gives you a single REPL with the `psql` backslash-command vocabulary as the lingua franca** — `usql` (xo, MIT, v0.21.4) ports the `psql` muscle memory (`\dt` lists tables, `\d users` describes, `\copy` streams CSV in / out without server-side perms, `\watch 5` reruns the last query every 5 s, `\g file.csv` writes results to a file, `\i script.sql` includes a script, `\set` defines variables, `\if` / `\elif` / `\else` / `\endif` branch) across every engine in one binary, with output formats switchable per-session (`\pset format aligned|unaligned|csv|json|html|markdown|asciidoc|vertical`) so the same query becomes a Markdown table for a doc, JSON for a script, or CSV for downstream tooling without leaving the REPL; one-shot mode (`usql -c "select ..." -J pg://...`) makes it equally good in pipes (`usql ... | jq`); pick over [`duckdb`](clis/duckdb/) / [`sqlite-utils`](clis/sqlite-utils/) when your day touches Postgres + DuckDB + Snowflake in the same hour (those are engine-specific; this is cross-engine), over [`harlequin`](clis/harlequin/) when you want the one-line REPL not the multi-pane TUI (use `harlequin` for exploratory schema browsing, `usql` for the `psql`-style one-liner from a script or `tmux` pane), over `psql` / `mysql` / `sqlite3` themselves when you would otherwise install four engine-specific CLIs with four different sets of meta-commands; caveat — "universal" is the *interface* (URL + backslash commands + output formats), not the SQL dialect (a Postgres `RETURNING` clause does not magically work against MySQL), CGO-required drivers (Oracle, DB2, Informix, Tibero) need the right native client libs and a non-default build tag (the `most` build tag covers the ~40 pure-Go drivers without any compile step), backslash-command coverage is broad but not 100% `psql`-parity against non-Postgres engines (run `\?` in a session to see what is supported on the current connection), and connection-string secrets in shell history is the usual hazard (prefer env vars / `~/.usqlrc` / a password helper rather than baking creds into the URL) | [`usql`](clis/usql/) |
| Want **`psql` you actually want to type into — same `\` backslash vocabulary, same libpq URI / `.pgpass` / `PG*` env-var auth, same multi-line semicolon submit semantics, but with live schema-aware autocomplete (table names, column names, function names, aliases, `JOIN` conditions all completed from the *currently connected* schema, refreshed in the background so a freshly created table appears without reconnecting), syntax highlighting in the buffer, named-query favourites stored in `~/.config/pgcli/config`, switchable result rendering via `\T markdown` / `\T csv` / `\T json` for paste-ready output, and per-host history with `Ctrl+R` fuzzy reverse search across past sessions** — `pgcli` (dbcli, BSD-3-Clause, v4.4.0) is the `prompt_toolkit`-based interactive client for Postgres / Greenplum / Redshift that keeps `psql`'s contract (every `\d`, `\dt`, `\dn`, `\df`, `\timing`, `\copy`, `\watch` works the way the muscle memory expects, every `\i script.sql` includes a script, every libpq env var is honoured) and adds the schema-aware completion that turns the "open another tab and `\d users` to remember the column name" detour into `SELECT ` + `Tab`; same author / same UX as the `mycli` (MySQL) and `litecli` (SQLite) siblings so the keymap is portable across all three engines, and the `--help-with -e "<sql>"` non-interactive one-shot path returns results on stdout in whichever `\T` format is set (CSV / TSV / markdown / JSON) for piping into [`jq`](clis/jq/) / [`miller`](clis/miller/) downstream; pick over stock `psql` when the live-schema completion + syntax highlighting + named-query favourites are worth the extra dependency (you already have `psql` for scripts and CI; `pgcli` is for the interactive session), over [`usql`](clis/usql/) when 95% of your day is Postgres and the per-engine completion + syntax highlighting matter more than the cross-engine universality (use `usql` when you bounce between Postgres + MySQL + Snowflake hourly; `pgcli` when Postgres is home), over [`harlequin`](clis/harlequin/) when the one-line `psql`-style REPL beats the multi-pane TUI (use `harlequin` for exploratory schema browsing across a dozen connections; `pgcli` for the daily query session against one Postgres), over a JetBrains DataGrip / pgAdmin GUI when 80×24 over SSH is the surface; caveat — Postgres-only (the engine is in the name; for MySQL / SQLite use the sibling `mycli` / `litecli`), the background catalog-refresh worker keeps autocomplete fresh but on a 50-table multi-schema database the first connect takes a few hundred ms before completion is fully populated (visible only on cold connect), `prompt_toolkit` rendering needs a terminal with ≥80 columns and reasonable Unicode support (true-color preferred for the syntax highlighting; degrades gracefully to 16-color), and per-host history is plain text in `~/.config/pgcli/history` so `\set HISTORY_IGNORE` patterns or moving to [`atuin`](clis/atuin/) is the move when shared shells could read each other's queries | [`pgcli`](clis/pgcli/) |
| Want **shell history as a real local database, not a flat tail-only log — every command captured into SQLite along with cwd, exit code, wall-clock duration, hostname, and session id, fronted by a full-screen fuzzy `Ctrl+R` TUI, optionally end-to-end encrypted and synced across machines via a self-hostable server** — `atuin` (atuinsh, MIT, v18.15.2) replaces the flat `~/.zsh_history` / `~/.bash_history` line-buffer with `~/.local/share/atuin/history.db`, rebinds `Ctrl+R` to a Textual-style TUI with fuzzy filtering and scope toggles (`session` → `directory` → `host` → `global`), exposes the same store from scripts (`atuin search --cmd "kubectl" --cwd ~/code/myrepo --limit 20`, `atuin stats`, `atuin history list --cwd ~/code/myrepo --format json | jq '...'` to count failed commands in this repo) — for an operator who is constantly re-running variations of the same command across many directories and machines (and an LLM-CLI workflow that lives by re-running prompt invocations), this turns shell history from "scrollback" into "personal command database"; the optional sync layer client-side-encrypts the history with a passphrase-derived `XChaCha20-Poly1305` key (libsodium) and pushes ciphertext to the free hosted instance or a `cargo install atuin-server` you run yourself, so all your machines see the same history without ever trusting the server with the plaintext; pick over [`zoxide`](clis/zoxide/) when the question is *which command* not *which directory* (they compose — both go in the same `eval` block in your rc file), over [`fzf`](clis/fzf/) when you want history-specific UI with cwd / exit / duration context (use `fzf` for generic picker UIs), over `mcfly` when you use more than one machine and want E2EE multi-host sync (the killer differentiator), over [`lnav`](clis/lnav/) when the target is *your shell* not log files (different layers, no overlap); caveat — the `Ctrl+R` rebinding is the point but feels unfamiliar versus stock `bck-i-search` for the first hour (use `atuin init zsh --disable-up-arrow` if Up-arrow rebinding is too aggressive for muscle memory), the recovery key (`atuin key`) is the *only* way to decrypt your history on a new machine after enabling sync (save it to your password manager the moment you enable sync), capture happens at `preexec` / `precmd` hook boundaries so a shell framework that overwrites the same hook (`direnv` / custom prompt setups / some plugin managers) can collide (the README has a troubleshooting section), and the local SQLite store grows ~unbounded (`atuin search --before '1y ago' --delete` or a periodic `atuin store rebuild` keeps it tidy) | [`atuin`](clis/atuin/) |
| Want **the framework-agnostic schema-migration runner for teams that don't want migrations to be a programming-language commitment — plain `.sql` files in `db/migrations/` named `YYYYMMDDHHMMSS_description.sql` with two fence-delimited sections (`-- migrate:up` and `-- migrate:down`), applied transactionally in order against whatever engine `DATABASE_URL` points at, run from one ~10 MB Go binary you `COPY` into a Docker image** — `dbmate` (amacneil, MIT, v2.32.0) is the `migrate`-style runner for the polyglot service: `dbmate new add_users_table` scaffolds the file, `dbmate up` applies pending migrations and writes a checksum + timestamp into a `schema_migrations` table, `dbmate down` rolls back the most recent, `dbmate dump` regenerates a `db/schema.sql` snapshot of the current schema for code review (so reviewers see the *full* post-migration schema in the PR diff, not just the delta), `dbmate status` lists applied + pending; engines: Postgres / MySQL / SQLite / ClickHouse, addressed by the engine's standard URL (`postgres://user:pass@host:5432/db?sslmode=require`, `mysql://...`, `sqlite:./local.db`, `clickhouse://...`); pick over [`goose`](clis/goose/) when you want the simpler "SQL files + CLI only" posture without Go-function migrations (use `goose` if you want to embed the migrator into a Go binary or write migrations as Go funcs), over Rails `ActiveRecord::Migration` / Django `manage.py migrate` / Sequelize / TypeORM / Alembic / Knex when you want migrations to run from a CI image that has none of the app's runtime or when multiple services share one database, over [`sqlfluff`](clis/sqlfluff/) which lints / formats the SQL inside a migration but does not run it (pair them in a pre-commit hook — `sqlfluff lint db/migrations/ && dbmate status`), over [`harlequin`](clis/harlequin/) / [`usql`](clis/usql/) which are interactive REPLs for ad-hoc SQL not deploy-shaped migration runners; caveat — engine matrix is intentionally narrow (Postgres / MySQL / SQLite / ClickHouse — no SQL Server, no Oracle, no Snowflake, no BigQuery; if your warehouse is Snowflake this is the wrong tool), migrations are append-only timestamps with no squash / merge / rebase command (long-lived branches that both add migrations conflict on `db/schema.sql` mechanically; adopt a "rebase before merge" rule), rollbacks are best-effort and a `down` that does `drop column` on Postgres is destructive (production posture is "roll forward with a new migration", not "trust `dbmate down` against prod"), `dbmate dump` shells out to the engine's native dumper (`pg_dump`, `mysqldump`, `sqlite3 .schema`, `clickhouse-client`) so those binaries must be on `$PATH` of whichever host runs the dump, and transactional DDL is engine-dependent (Postgres wraps each migration in a transaction by default; MySQL silently commits between DDL statements so a half-applied migration is a real failure mode — author MySQL migrations as one atomic-able statement where possible) | [`dbmate`](clis/dbmate/) |
| Want **a five-panel ncurses-style git TUI where every common operation (stage hunk, amend, interactive rebase, cherry-pick, conflict-resolve, bisect, push, force-push-with-lease) is one or two keystrokes — including interactive rebase as a *live editable TUI* (`s` squash, `f` fixup, `d` drop, `Ctrl+J/K` reorder, no `EDITOR=vim` rebase TODO file) and three-way merge conflict resolution with picker UI instead of `<<<<<<<` markers in your editor** — `lazygit` (jesseduffield, MIT, v0.61.1) opens a status / files / branches / commits / stash layout on top of any git repo, where `enter` on a modified file opens per-hunk + per-line staging without `git add -p`'s modal-keystroke prompts, `e` on a commit starts an interactive rebase whose TODO list is the commits panel itself (move with `Ctrl+J/K`, `s` to squash, `f` to fixup, `d` to drop — all live, all undoable with `z` the recovery command-log), conflicted files in the files panel render as a three-way picker (`space` picks the highlighted hunk, `b` keeps both, `c` continues the rebase / merge), and the `customCommands:` YAML DSL in `~/.config/lazygit/config.yml` lets you bind project-specific actions (a `r` keystroke that resets to `origin/main` after a confirm prompt, a `D` that deploys to staging, …); pick over [`gitui`](clis/gitui/) when you want the richer interactive-rebase + custom-commands + GitHub-integration story (`o` on a branch shells out to `gh pr create` if `gh` is on `$PATH`) over `gitui`'s raw-speed-on-monorepos story, over [`jj`](clis/jj/) when the team is on git and you want a faster keymap not a different VCS (`jj` changes the underlying commit semantics, `lazygit` is a UI on unmodified git), over the bare `git` CLI for the per-hunk staging + live-editable rebase + recovery-undo combination that pays back the learning cost in the first hour; pairs with [`delta`](clis/delta/) (set `git config --global core.pager delta` and `lazygit`'s diff modals on `D` automatically render through it) and is orthogonal to [`gh`](https://cli.github.com/) (local-git only, but composes via the `o` keybind); caveat — TUI assumes a true-color terminal with ≥80 columns (under ~100 cols the five-panel layout gets cramped — works fine in `tmux` / `iTerm2` / `kitty` / `wezterm` / modern Windows Terminal, breaks in old `cmd.exe`), `Shift+P` is force-push-with-lease in one keystroke and the confirm modal is small (pair with branch protection on `main` if you share repos), the recovery `z` command-log only undoes operations `lazygit` performed itself this session (a `git reset --hard` typed in another pane is *not* in the log — treat it as session-local convenience, not a substitute for `git reflog`), the custom-commands YAML DSL is YAML with its own `prompts:` syntax quirks (read [docs/Custom_Commands.md](https://github.com/jesseduffield/lazygit/blob/master/docs/Custom_Commands.md) before hand-rolling complex ones), and there is no first-class git-LFS UI (LFS pointers show as normal files; LFS-specific ops like `git lfs migrate` go through the `:` command palette) | [`lazygit`](clis/lazygit/) |
| Want **the diff pager you wire into `core.pager` once and never think about again — re-renders every `git diff` / `git log -p` / `git show` / `git blame` / `git stash show -p` (and bare `diff -u` / `rg --json` / merge-conflict markers in the working tree) with side-by-side or unified layouts, real per-language syntax highlighting via `syntect` against any Sublime `.tmTheme`, per-hunk file headers, line numbers, and navigable hunk-by-hunk jumps** — `delta` (dandavison, MIT, 0.19.2) is the diff renderer the rest of the catalog's git tools shell out to: `git config --global core.pager delta` + `git config --global delta.side-by-side true` + `git config --global delta.line-numbers true` + `git config --global delta.navigate true` + `git config --global delta.syntax-theme "Monokai Extended"` is the one-time setup, after which a 50-line moved function shows as side-by-side columns instead of "50 lines of `-` then 50 lines of `+`", every changed line gets real tokenisation (Rust lifetimes, Python f-strings, TSX, Markdown all colored correctly), conflict markers (`<<<<<<<` / `||||||| common` / `=======` / `>>>>>>>`) render as three columns (ours / base / theirs) with the diff between each pair highlighted instead of the "stare at conflict markers in the editor and try to remember which side was which" failure mode, and `n` / `N` jump between hunks inside the `less`-driven pager; pick over [`bat`](clis/bat/) when the input is *diff structure* not *source files* (bat highlights diff syntax — the `+` / `-` markers — but not the underlying language on each changed line; delta parses diff structure and applies the underlying language's grammar to each side), over `git diff --color-words` / `--word-diff` for code (those modes are useful for prose, noisy on code — delta's character-level intra-line diff is sharper without the noise), over `diff-so-fancy` for the syntax-highlighting + side-by-side + Sublime-theme bonuses (delta has a `--features=diff-so-fancy` preset that emulates the visual style for teams migrating); composes with [`lazygit`](clis/lazygit/) / [`gitui`](clis/gitui/) (set `core.pager = delta` and their internal-diff modals automatically render through it) and is orthogonal to those *clients* (delta is the *renderer*); caveat — `core.pager = delta` runs delta on every paginated git command including `git log` (no `-p`) where it has nothing diff-shaped and just forwards to `less` (correct, no perf hit, but if you scripted around the exact `less` behavior of `git log | head` you may see different terminal sequences — set `delta.paging = never` for those scripts), `syntect` highlighting is heuristic (files without a recognised extension fall back to plain text — force with `--default-language=Rust` in scripts), `--side-by-side` needs ~160 columns to be comfortable (narrower terminals get cramped two-column layout that wraps — set `delta.side-by-side = false` per-machine on a laptop and `true` on a wide monitor by including a per-machine config from `~/.gitconfig`), performance on huge diffs (10k+ lines) is fine for interactive review but slower than raw `less` because every line is tokenized (for `git log -p` over thousands of commits, prefer `git log --stat` or scope with `git log -p -- <path>`), and delta does *not* replace `git diff` — it consumes git's diff output, so settings that conflict with what delta expects (e.g. a script forcing `--color=never`) make output look broken (let the `core.pager` wiring handle it) | [`delta`](clis/delta/) |
| Want **a git-compatible VCS that fixes git's commit model — every working-tree edit is automatically a commit (no `add` / `commit` cycle), the operation log is a queryable history (`jj op restore` rolls back any aborted rebase including the working copy and bookmarks atomically, the universal undo), conflicts are first-class commit content (a `jj rebase` over a conflicting change *succeeds* — the resulting "conflicted commit" can be rebased / split / abandoned without resolving first), and change IDs (stable across rewrites) are separate from commit IDs (which change on every rewrite) so `jj rebase` / `jj squash` / `jj split` don't break references** — `jj` (jj-vcs, Apache-2.0, v0.40.0) is a Rust-implemented VCS that keeps git's storage compatibility but replaces git's commit model with one that removes most of the cliff edges (detached HEAD, "oops I committed to the wrong branch", "the rebase is now broken and I lost my work", "I rewrote a commit and now my collaborator's branch points to a non-existent SHA"); the core bet — every working-copy state is *already* a commit, every operation is recorded in `jj op log` and rollback-able with `jj op restore <op-id>` (atomically including working copy + bookmark pointers, where `git reflog` only shows commit-level moves and doesn't restore branch pointers atomically), conflicts are *content* of a commit (not interruption), branches are explicit pointers ("bookmarks") you move with `jj bookmark set my-feature -r @-` not auto-following symrefs, and `jj git init --colocate` makes the same directory *both* a git repo and a jj repo (`.git/` + `.jj/` side-by-side) so teammates on plain git clone / pull / push / review PRs from your branches without knowing `jj` exists; pick over plain `git` when you want the operation-log undo + conflicts-as-content + change-ID-stability combination (the "I broke a rebase 30 commits in" failure mode stops happening), over [`lazygit`](clis/lazygit/) / [`gitui`](clis/gitui/) when you want to change the *underlying VCS semantics* not just the UI on top (those are TUIs over unmodified git and don't understand jj's change-ID model or operation log), over `sapling` (Meta's git replacement) for stronger conflict-as-content semantics + a more developed operation log (sapling is the closest peer with stronger stacked-PR tooling — picking is mostly community / docs preference), over `mercurial` / `fossil` / other non-git-compatible VCSes when interop with the git-using world is a hard requirement (`--colocate` is the unique selling point); for an LLM-CLI workflow that produces a long stack of small related commits per agent turn that need frequent reorder / squash / split / rebase, removes most of the friction; caveat — the vocabulary is the cost (`jj squash` ≠ `git commit --amend`, `jj new` ≠ `git checkout -b`, bookmarks ≠ branches — budget a week of read-the-docs-while-using-it before muscle memory catches up; do the [tutorial](https://jj-vcs.github.io/jj/latest/tutorial/) once end-to-end), working-copy auto-snapshotting can confuse file-watcher-heavy stacks (some Bazel / Buck setups, some IDE indexers — usually fine, rarely interacts badly), `--colocate` mode has subtle gotchas if teammates do operations on `.git/` that jj doesn't expect (force-push that rewrites a bookmark you have a local descendant of, GC that prunes a commit jj references — `jj git import` reconciles, read the [colocation docs](https://jj-vcs.github.io/jj/latest/git-compatibility/) before adopting on a busy shared repo), pre-commit hooks need adapting (`jj fix` runs configured formatters but no first-class `pre-commit`-framework equivalent yet — run those from CI rather than expecting jj to fire them locally), and pre-1.0 with steady ~monthly releases that still shift the CLI surface between minors (pin in setup docs and review release notes before bumping) | [`jj`](clis/jj/) |
| Want **a `tmux`-class terminal multiplexer with the onboarding cliff filed off — visible status + tab bar by default, the bottom strip lists every keybind for the current mode (`NORMAL` / `PANE` / `TAB` / `RESIZE` / `SCROLL` / `SESSION` / `LOCKED`) so workflows are discoverable not memorized, layouts as declarative KDL files diffable in git, floating panes on a hotkey, and a WebAssembly plugin runtime where built-in widgets (status bar, tab bar, session manager) are themselves wasm modules and third-party plugins drop in as one `*.wasm` file** — `zellij` (zellij-org, MIT, v0.44.1) is the Rust-implemented multiplexer for operators who want `tmux`'s session-survives-the-SSH-drop durability without `tmux`'s 200-line `.tmux.conf` ramp: `zellij` with no args attaches the default session (or creates one), `zellij --layout dev` boots a named layout, `zellij attach <name>` reattaches a detached session, panes split with `Ctrl+P` then `n` / `d` / `r`, floating panes toggle with `Alt+f`, the scrollback gets its own mode (`Ctrl+S`) with regex search and `Ctrl+S e` opens the scrollback in `$EDITOR`, `Ctrl+G` lock-mode passes every key through to the inner program for `vim` / `helix` keymap conflicts, and KDL layout files (`~/.config/zellij/layouts/*.kdl`) declare panes / tabs / running commands declaratively so a multi-pane workspace boots from one `zellij --layout llm-dev` command (the `tmuxinator` equivalent is more fragile and not first-class);
