- [amber](clis/amber/) — code search-and-replace tool in Rust that pairs a `ripgrep`-class recursive search front (`ambs <pattern>`) with a transactional batch-rewrite front (`ambr <pattern> <replacement>`) that opens every candidate hit in `$EDITOR` as one combined diff buffer so you can delete or edit individual hunks before the rewrite is committed in a single pass — multibyte text (UTF-8 / UTF-16 LE/BE / EUC-JP / Shift_JIS) auto-detected per file, `.gitignore` respected by default, regex with backrefs in the replacement, parallel across cores. Pick over `sed -i` (no preview, easy to corrupt files on a bad regex) and over `rg --replace` (prints rewritten text to stdout but doesn't apply) when the workload is "project-wide rename whose regex matches the things I want plus a handful of false positives, and I'd rather scroll the diff once and prune than build the perfect exclusion regex." Skip for AST-level refactors (use [`comby`](clis/comby/) / [`ast-grep`](clis/ast-grep/)), single-file scripted edits (use [`sd`](clis/sd/) / `sed`), or one-shot stdin transforms (use `rg --replace` piped to a file).
- [t-rec](clis/t-rec/) — terminal recorder that emits an animated GIF directly from native screen-capture APIs (X11 / Wayland / Quartz) so the recording is the *real* terminal including font ligatures, true colour, kitty graphics protocol, and sixel images — none of which survive `asciinema`'s re-execution-in-JS playback path. Default mode emits one frame per `Enter` press for a "natural typing cadence" GIF that's paced like a real demo, and an idle-frame compactor drops duplicate frames between commands so a 60-second session with 8 seconds of typing produces a 12-second GIF. Pick over [`asciinema`](clis/asciinema/) (records typed keystrokes, not pixels — drops GPU-only features) when the visual output matters; pick over [`vhs`](clis/vhs/) when you want to record an *interactive* session, not author a deterministic `.tape` file in advance; pick over `terminalizer` when 6 MB single binary beats 300 MB Electron install. Skip for fully-reproducible scripted recordings (use `vhs`), for cast files that diff cleanly in PRs (use `asciinema`), or for full-desktop screen recording (use OBS).
- [gifski](clis/gifski/) — `pngquant`-author's GIF encoder that computes a per-frame neural-quantised 256-colour palette with temporal dithering, producing GIFs that are 2-3× sharper than `ffmpeg -vf "palettegen,paletteuse"` at the same byte budget — the last-mile encoder paired with [`vhs`](clis/vhs/) / [`agg`](clis/agg/) / [`asciinema`](clis/asciinema/) / [`t-rec`](clis/t-rec/) when their built-in encoders blow your README's image-size budget. Pick over `ffmpeg -f gif` when palette quality at small sizes matters (anti-aliased terminal text loves this); pick over `gifsicle` when you're encoding from raw frames or video, not optimising an existing GIF. Skip when AGPL-3.0 is incompatible downstream (use `gifsicle` MIT), when you need WebP / AVIF (use `ffmpeg`), or when you just want lossless GIF re-optimisation (`gifsicle -O3`).
- [parallel](clis/parallel/) — Ole Tange's GNU `parallel`, a perl one-binary `xargs`-on-steroids whose load-bearing features the modern Rust re-imaginings still don't ship together: `--joblog jobs.log` + `--resume-failed` makes a 4-hour batch into a "rerun until green" loop, `--sshlogin host1,host2` fans the same job stream out to remote machines with `--transferfile` / `--return` for I/O staging, and `{1}` / `{1.}` / `{1/}` / `{= perl-expr =}` template substitution makes "for each pair in this CSV, on three remote hosts, run this command" a one-liner. Pick over `xargs -P` when you need resumability, ordered output, or remote hosts; pick over [`pueue`](clis/pueue/) / [`task-spooler`](clis/task-spooler/) when the workload is "10k jobs across 5 hosts then exit" rather than a long-lived personal queue. Skip when `xargs -P` covers it, when a real scheduler (Slurm / Nomad / k8s Jobs / GitHub Actions matrix) already owns retries, or when you need a queue-shaped TUI (use `pueue`).
- [pyenv](clis/pyenv/) — the boring, decade-stable per-shell Python switcher that puts a `shims/` directory at the front of `PATH` and resolves to the right CPython by walking `.python-version` files up from `$PWD` — every tool that finds Python through `PATH` (your editor, your test runner, your `Makefile`, a `#!/usr/bin/env python` shebang) sees the same active version with no per-tool config. Builds CPython (or PyPy / GraalPy / etc.) from source via `python-build` so any custom `--enable-optimizations` / `--with-openssl=...` is reachable. Pick when you need the source-build escape hatch or you already have `.python-version` files committed across repos; pick over [`asdf`](https://asdf-vm.com/) when Python is the only runtime and you want the focused, decade-tested tool. Skip when you're greenfield-Python and [`uv`](clis/uv/) covers it (prebuilt python-build-standalone in seconds beats `pyenv install`'s 3-10 min compile), when [`mise`](clis/mise/) already manages 3+ runtimes for you, or when you're on Windows native (use `pyenv-win` fork or `uv`).
- [frawk](clis/frawk/) — JIT-compiled `awk` (Cranelift or LLVM backend) with a parallel chunked SIMD CSV / TSV / regex-split parser — adds first-class `-i csv` / `-i tsv` input modes that handle quoted fields and embedded newlines correctly (a long-standing footgun for `awk -F,` on real CSV), matching `-o csv` / `-o tsv` quoted-output modes, and `-pr` parallel mode that runs the per-record block on N threads merging per-chunk `END` aggregates; explicit `int(x)` / `hex(x)` typing instead of awk's surprising implicit promotion. Pick over `mawk` / `gawk` when you have a multi-GB CSV / TSV / log file, an existing awk-shaped script, and the awk runtime itself is the bottleneck (5-20× speedups are typical); pick over [`mlr`](clis/mlr/) when you're already thinking in awk syntax (mlr is verb-pipeline over named columns — different mental model); pick over [`qsv`](clis/qsv/) when the per-record logic is a script not a verb. Skip for the last 5% of gawk's surface (gensub edge cases, network I/O, `mktime`), for non-tabular data (use [`jq`](clis/jq/) / [`dasel`](clis/dasel/) / [`mlr`](clis/mlr/)), or when the dataset already fits in [`duckdb`](clis/duckdb/) / pandas.

## Previous rotation (+3)

- [caligula](clis/caligula/) — user-friendly TUI disk imager in Rust that replaces `dd if=image.iso of=/dev/sdX` with an interactive picker that lists removable block devices (and refuses to clobber the disk you're booted from), validates the source against `*.sha256` / `*.b3` sidecars or `--hash` BLAKE3/MD5/SHA1/SHA256, decompresses `.xz` / `.gz` / `.bz2` / `.zst` / `.lz4` images on the fly so you don't pre-extract a 4 GB ISO, shows real throughput + ETA, and post-write reads the device back to byte-verify the flash succeeded — single static binary, no GUI toolkit, runs over SSH on a headless box. Pick over raw `dd` when you flash USB installers / SD cards regularly and want `dd`-class speed without `dd`-class destruction risk; pick over balenaEtcher when you don't have or want an Electron desktop session; pick over Rufus when you're not on Windows. Skip for Windows-native flashing (use Rufus), partitioning (use `parted` / `gparted`), or live-system cloning (use `clonezilla`).
- [oils](clis/oils/) — POSIX shell with an upgrade path: ships `osh` (near-100% bash-compatible runtime that runs your existing `.bashrc` / autotools output / `set -euo pipefail` pipelines unchanged but with a real parser and cleaner error messages) and `ysh` (a from-scratch shell language with typed values `Int` / `Str` / `List` / `Dict`, JSON / J8-Notation as first-class values, expression-vs-command modes, exceptions instead of `$?` checking, and garbage collection) from one binary, with `shopt --set ysh:upgrade` letting one codebase migrate file-by-file. Pick over bash / zsh / dash when you want bash-compat *today* + a typed shell language *tomorrow* without rewriting; pick over [`fish-shell`](clis/fish-shell/) when you need POSIX-compat for build scripts; pick over [`nushell`](clis/nushell/) when the workload is `make`-style command orchestration rather than tabular data wrangling. Skip as a day-one interactive login shell (interactive UX still trails fish/zsh+plugins) and for sandboxed embedded scripting (use Lua, Starlark, or [`pkl`](clis/pkl/)).
- [minisign](clis/minisign/) — dead-simple Ed25519 file signing in a ~300 KB binary: one key pair (`minisign.pub` + encrypted `minisign.key`), one signature file (`<file>.minisig`) per artifact, no keyrings, no web of trust, no certificate chains, no agent daemon — plus a signed *trusted comment* slot so release notes / build metadata can't be tampered with separately from the file, plus a pre-hashed mode (`-H`) that signs the BLAKE2b digest so verification of multi-GB tarballs doesn't re-stream the whole file. Wire-compatible with OpenBSD `signify`. Used in production by Zig releases, Tor Browser, libsodium, dnscrypt-proxy, and WireGuard's Windows installer. Pick over `gpg --sign` when you don't want the OpenPGP keyring / web-of-trust / subkey complexity surface that's the reason most projects ship `sha256sums.txt` instead of signed releases; pick over [`cosign`](clis/cosign/) when you're signing tarballs / binaries / firmware rather than OCI artifacts; pick over [`age`](clis/age/) / [`rage`](clis/rage/) when you need *signing*, not encryption. Skip when you need OpenPGP interop with distro maintainers (use `gpg`) or transparency-log-backed supply-chain provenance (use [`cosign`](clis/cosign/) + [`rekor`](clis/rekor/)).

## Two rotations back (+3)

- [traefik](clis/traefik/) — cloud-native HTTP/TCP/UDP reverse proxy that **discovers its own routes** from Docker labels, Kubernetes IngressRoute / Gateway API CRDs, Consul / Nomad / ECS catalogs, or a watched YAML/TOML directory — every service publishes `traefik.http.routers.api.rule=Host('api.example.com')` and traefik materialises the router → middleware → service graph within sub-second of the service appearing, with built-in ACME (TLS / HTTP / DNS-01 challenges, wildcard certs), an HTTP/3 entrypoint behind a one-line opt-in, a composable middleware library (rateLimit / forwardAuth / circuitBreaker / stripPrefix / compress / IPWhiteList), Prometheus + OTLP observability out of the box, and a live `/dashboard/` view of the routing graph for debugging — pick over [`caddy`](clis/caddy/) when the topology is dynamic (containers come and go faster than you want to edit Caddyfile) and over nginx / HAProxy when "auto-discovery from the runtime" beats raw L7 throughput, complementary to [`consul`](clis/consul/) / [`nomad`](clis/nomad/) / [`podman`](clis/podman/) / [`nerdctl`](clis/nerdctl/) (whose sockets / catalogs traefik consumes directly as providers)
- [kitty](clis/kitty/) — GPU-accelerated terminal emulator that treats the terminal as a programmable surface: native tabs and split layouts without tmux (`Ctrl+Shift+T` / `Ctrl+Shift+Enter`), the **kitty graphics protocol** for in-band PNG / RGBA image transfer (consumed by `matplotlib`, `chafa --format=kitty`, `nvim` image plugins, `mpv --vo=kitty`, `euporie`), the **kitty keyboard protocol** that disambiguates Ctrl-i vs Tab and reports modifier press / release events for serious modal editors, OSC 8 hyperlinks rendered as real clickable spans, a remote-control socket (`kitty @ launch --type=tab nvim`) scripts can drive from inside the session, and a **kittens** plugin model that ships transparent-config `kitten ssh` (mirrors local zshrc + nvim to the remote box), `kitten icat` (inline images), `kitten diff` (image-aware side-by-side), and `kitten hyperlinked-grep` (rg with clickable file:line: links) — pick over [`wezterm`](clis/wezterm/) when the kitty graphics protocol's broader TUI support matters more than wezterm's Lua-config flexibility, complementary to [`vhs`](clis/vhs/) / [`asciinema`](clis/asciinema/) / [`agg`](clis/agg/) for recording sessions and to tmux for remote persistence
- [taskwarrior](clis/taskwarrior/) — structured, filter-first task database for the command line: typed attributes (`project`, `tags`, `due`, `wait`, `recur`, `depends`, plus user-defined `uda.*`), one uniform filter grammar across every verb (`task project:work and +urgent and due.before:eow list / count / done / modify / annotate / burndown.daily / export`), recurring tasks with proper instance semantics (`recur:weekly due:fri` materialises one instance per week), dependent tasks hidden until the blocker closes, deferred tasks invisible until a date, per-context implicit filters, a hook system for shell-script integrations (Slack on add, Prometheus metrics, iCal sync), and the v3 **Taskchampion** Rust storage + sync layer giving you a SQLite-backed replica plus end-to-end encrypted multi-device sync against either a self-hosted `taskwarrior-sync-server` or an S3 / GCS bucket — pick over a Markdown / `todo.txt` checklist once you cross ~50 active tasks across multiple projects and need to query "what's due this week, untagged, in any project but @home" as a one-line invocation; a natural sink for LLM agents' "flag-for-followup" tool calls (one `task add` with `+from-agent` tag) so an agent's ephemera becomes a queryable, filter-friendly database the next agent run reads back via `task export +from-agent and status:pending`

## Three rotations back (+3)

- [podman](clis/podman/) — daemonless, rootless-by-default, Docker-CLI-compatible OCI container engine that emits Kubernetes YAML as its native serialisation: `podman generate kube` produces a manifest `kubectl apply -f` accepts, and `podman play kube` replays one locally; pods are a first-class CLI primitive, the storage / image / signature stacks are shared with `buildah` / `skopeo`, and `podman machine` brings the same engine to macOS / Windows via a managed Linux VM — pick over [`colima`](clis/colima/) / [`nerdctl`](clis/nerdctl/) when you want the rootless Docker-CLI compat layer + `play kube` workflow + the exact engine RHEL / Fedora ship in production, over Docker when "no root daemon, no docker.sock to harden" is the actual ask
- [nomad](clis/nomad/) — single-binary multi-driver workload scheduler (containers + raw binaries + JVM jars + QEMU VMs + WASM under one HCL job spec), Raft control plane that fits on three 2 GB servers, native batch / system / service / parameterised / periodic job types, canary / blue-green / rolling updates with auto-revert, and Consul-Connect service mesh as a one-line `connect {}` block — pick over Kubernetes when the team / footprint / mixed-driver story makes the kube CRD galaxy the wrong size, complementary to [`consul`](clis/consul/) (service discovery + mesh) and [`tofu`](clis/tofu/) (host provisioning) since Nomad is just the scheduler core
- [ugrep](clis/ugrep/) — the "grep that does everything": every grep dialect (BRE / ERE / PCRE2 / fuzzy `-Z` / hex `-X`) plus recursive search inside compressed archives (`.gz` / `.xz` / `.zst` / `.zip` / `.7z` / `.tar`) **and** PDF / DOCX / XLSX (via user-defined `--filter` extractors) without unpacking, plus a real interactive TUI (`ug --query`) with live regex preview and file navigation, plus structured `--json` / `--xml` / `--csv` output — pick over [`ripgrep`](clis/ripgrep/) when you need archive descent, fuzzy match, or PCRE2 recursion (rg remains faster on the pure source-tree case), complementary to [`ast-grep`](clis/ast-grep/) / [`comby`](clis/comby/) (AST-level) and [`fd`](clis/fd/) (file finder) in the lexical / structural / file-name layers of the search story

## Rotation before that (+3)

- [mlr](clis/mlr/) — single-binary stream processor for **name-indexed tabular data** (CSV / TSV / JSON-Lines / Parquet / DKVP / pretty-print / fixed-width) with the verb-pipeline ergonomics of `awk` + `cut` + `join` + `sort` + `stats1` combined; v6's typed expression DSL (`mlr put '$margin=($price-$cost)/$price'`), streaming group-by (`mlr stats1 -a sum,mean,p99 -f latency -g region huge.csv`), and short-flag format conversion (`--c2j`, `--j2c`, `--iparquet --ojsonl`) make it the right shell tool whenever the data has named columns and pandas / DuckDB would be overkill — pick over [`xsv`](clis/xsv/) when you need format conversion or a real expression language, over [`jq`](clis/jq/) when records are streamed and tabular rather than a single JSON document, over [`duckdb`](clis/duckdb/) when the workload is pipeline-shaped rather than relational
- [valkey](clis/valkey/) — vendor-neutral, fully-BSD-3-licensed, Linux-Foundation-stewarded continuation of the Redis 7.2 codebase: same RESP3 wire protocol, same data structures (strings / hashes / lists / sets / sorted sets / streams / hyperloglog / bitmap / geo / vector via `valkey-search`), same RDB+AOF persistence, same Sentinel + Cluster topology — drop-in for every existing Redis client SDK — plus multi-threaded async I/O on by default, dual-channel replication that decouples snapshot from command stream, RDMA support, and Hash Field TTL; the right answer when you want Redis ergonomics under a license distros and clouds can ship without RSALv2 / SSPLv1 friction (microsecond-latency cache, session store, rate-limiter, queue, pub/sub bus, LLM-response cache keyed on prompt hash, embedding-cache backend)
- [borg](clis/borg/) — single-binary deduplicating, encrypted, append-only **client-side backup over SSH**: content-defined Buzhash chunker means a daily snapshot of a 200 GB working tree typically writes a few hundred MB of new chunks, AES/HMAC-SHA-256 (or BLAKE2b) authenticated encryption is on by default so the storage host never sees plaintext, `borg mount` exposes any archive as a FUSE filesystem for random-access restore, and `borg prune --keep-daily 7 --keep-weekly 4` enforces retention without rewriting chunks; pick over [`restic`](clis/restic/) when the target is an SSH host (not S3 / B2 / GCS) and you want the more mature FUSE mount, over [`kopia`](clis/kopia/) when CLI-only and one-writer-per-repo is the right shape, over [`rclone`](clis/rclone/) when you need *snapshots and dedup* rather than just a mirrored sync — the natural backup engine for LLM project trees full of edit-in-place vector indexes, fine-tune checkpoints, and agent traces

## Latest additions (+3)

- [kubo](clis/kubo/) — reference Go implementation of **IPFS** in one `ipfs` binary that is both daemon and CLI: `ipfs add -r ./site` content-addresses every file (CID = sha256 of bytes — same input anywhere on Earth, same hash), `ipfs daemon` joins the public DHT swarm so any peer holding the CID can serve it, `ipfs name publish <cid>` signs a stable IPNS pointer under your node's libp2p identity so consumers fetch `/ipns/<peer-id>` and always get the latest version, and the local HTTP API on `127.0.0.1:5001/api/v0/` exposes every CLI verb to any language; pick over [`rclone`](clis/rclone/) / [`s5cmd`](clis/s5cmd/) when the address must survive the storage backend, over [`borgbackup`](clis/borgbackup/) / [`duplicacy`](clis/duplicacy/) when the workload is *public sharing* not *private encrypted snapshots*, and over a hand-rolled HTTP+sha256 cache when you want global dedup + free CDN-like behaviour + signed mutable pointers without standing up Cloudflare or a signing service; not for ACL'd private buckets (use the cloud's CLI), not when "please forget this content" is a hard requirement (content-addressed networks have no unpublish), and not when the DHT bandwidth overhead is unacceptable on the host (MIT OR Apache-2.0, v0.41.0)
- [jaeger](clis/jaeger/) — CNCF-graduated **distributed-tracing platform** as a single Go binary that is now built on the OpenTelemetry Collector: `jaeger --config config.yaml` ingests OTLP spans on gRPC :4317 and HTTP :4318, runs them through standard OTel processors (batch, sampling, redaction), stores them in in-memory / Badger / Cassandra / Elasticsearch / OpenSearch / ClickHouse, and serves the React query UI + JSON API on :16686 — `docker run -p 16686:16686 -p 4317:4317 jaegertracing/jaeger:2.17.0` is a complete tracing stack with no extra moving parts; pick over hosted SaaS APMs when traces must stay inside your perimeter (regulated data, on-prem, air-gapped), over [`vector`](clis/vector/) when you need a tracing *backend* with a query UI not just a routing pipeline, and over [`otel-cli`](clis/otel-cli/) when you need the receiver-side counterpart to a span emitter; not for unified metrics+logs+traces (use SigNoz / Grafana LGTM), not when the team already runs Tempo, and not when long-term retention without managing Cassandra / Elasticsearch is the dealbreaker (Apache-2.0, v2.17.0)
- [witness](clis/witness/) — CNCF-sandbox **in-toto attestation wrapper**: `witness run -s build -k key -- make release` runs your build exactly as before but sidecars it with attestors (`git`, `commandrun`, `environment`, `material`, `product`, `oci`, `slsa`, `sbom`, `vex`, AWS / GCP IID, Sigstore Fulcio) that record the command, the input + output file hashes, the git commit, and the runtime environment, then signs the resulting in-toto Statement with cosign / KMS / PKCS#11 / file keys; downstream `witness verify -p policy.signed.json -k pub.key -f artifact.tar.gz` walks a signed policy declaring which steps must have run, in which order, by which key, with which constraints, and exits non-zero on a violation — a verifier in CI / admission controller / release pipeline can mechanically reject builds that bypassed `test` or were signed by an unauthorised key; pick over hand-rolled `sha256sum + gpg` for a typed signed record of *how* the artifact was built, over [`cosign`](clis/cosign/) when the question is "what process produced this?" (they compose), over [`slsa-verifier`](clis/slsa-verifier/) when you need to *author* attestations instead of only verify SLSA-generator output, and over [`syft`](clis/syft/) when the need is provenance not an SBOM (witness can carry an SBOM as one attestor); not when "sign this binary" is the entire ask (use cosign), not when Tekton Chains or GitHub's built-in attestation already covers the trust chain (Apache-2.0, v0.11.0)

## Earlier rotation (+3)

- [carapace](clis/carapace/) — single Go binary that ships rich argument completers for ~1100 commands and emits them as native completion scripts for bash / zsh / fish / nushell / elvish / oil / xonsh / tcsh / PowerShell, so the *same* spec gives you flag descriptions on the right, dynamic value enums (`kubectl get pods -n <TAB>` lists live namespaces), and subcommand trees in whichever shell you happen to be in; `CARAPACE_BRIDGES=zsh,fish,bash` falls back to native completion for unsupported commands so installing it strictly *increases* coverage — pick when you switch shells (or want fish-grade completion in zsh / nushell) and don't want to maintain three separate `_mytool` files for your in-house CLIs
- [yt-dlp](clis/yt-dlp/) — actively-maintained `youtube-dl` successor: one CLI extracts audio / video / subtitles / thumbnails / metadata from ~1800+ sites with a uniform format-selection grammar (`-f "bestvideo[height<=1080]+bestaudio/best"`) and a uniform `--print-json` metadata schema; native SponsorBlock + chapter parsing, plugin extractors for the long tail, monthly releases that catch site-side player changes — the canonical "URL → audio file" primitive at the front of any `yt-dlp -x --audio-format wav | whisper.cpp` transcription pipeline for an LLM agent
- [d2](clis/d2/) — text-to-diagram compiler with auto-layout that does not fight you: declarative language (`a -> b: label`, containers, classes, imports), three layout engines (Dagre / ELK bundled, TALA paid), `d2 fmt` canonicalizes whitespace gofmt-style, `d2 --watch` re-renders to the browser in <500 ms; outputs SVG / PNG / PDF for embedding in mdBook / Hugo / Docusaurus — pick over Mermaid when you need real auto-layout on >20 nodes and over PlantUML when you don't need UML formalism

## Latest additions (+3)

- [ripgrep-all](clis/ripgrep-all/) — `rga`, a Rust wrapper around [`ripgrep`](https://github.com/BurntSushi/ripgrep) that transparently extracts text from PDFs, EPUB / MOBI e-books, Office docs (`.docx` / `.odt` / `.pptx` / `.xlsx`), zip / tar / gz / xz / 7z archives, sqlite databases, OCR'd images (via `tesseract`), and audio / video metadata (via `ffprobe`) before running the same `rg <pattern>` you already know — and caches the extracted text on disk keyed by content hash so the second `rga "fourier transform" ~/papers/` over 400 PDFs is instant; pick when "I know the phrase is in *one* of these binary blobs but `rg` returns zero hits because they're not text"; not for grepping plain source trees (use `rg`), and AGPL-3.0 means embedding `rga` in a SaaS without source disclosure breaches the license — for laptops / CI it's a non-issue (AGPL-3.0, v0.10.10)
- [jrnl](clis/jrnl/) — zero-friction command-line journal: `jrnl yesterday at 3pm: shipped the migration @work` is one shell line and the entry is timestamped, written to a plain text file (or AES-256 encrypted blob), tagged, and queryable; supports multiple named journals (`jrnl work`, `jrnl personal`), date-range filters (`-from "last week"`), tag faceting (`@meeting`), JSON / Markdown / YAML / calendar-heatmap export, and an `$EDITOR` round-trip for the longer entries — pick over Obsidian / Notion when the workflow is "type a thought, forget about it, find it again six months from now by date / tag / phrase," and over a Markdown-file-in-git when you want auto-timestamping + date-range query + optional encryption without writing any glue; not a knowledge graph (no backlinks, no graph view) (GPL-3.0, v4.2)
- [icdiff](clis/icdiff/) — single-Python-script side-by-side colored diff with **intra-line character highlighting** (the changed *characters* inside a changed line are bolded, so a one-character typo doesn't look like a whole-line rewrite), full Unicode-width handling for CJK / emoji, auto-detected terminal width, and a `git-icdiff` wrapper that drops in as a `git difftool` so `git icdiff HEAD~3 -- src/` becomes the default review view; pick over GNU `diff -y` for the inline character grouping and the Unicode width handling, and pick alongside [`delta`](clis/delta/) (which prettifies *unified* `git diff` output) when the question is "let me see these two files next to each other" rather than "make `git diff` pretty" — they compose; not for three-way merge (use [`mergiraf`](clis/mergiraf/) / `vimdiff`) or hex / binary diff (use `vbindiff`) (PSF-2.0, release-2.0.10)

## Previously latest additions (+3)

- [task-spooler](clis/task-spooler/) — single-user, single-host **serial / N-slot job queue** in one C binary (`tsp` on Debian, `ts` on Homebrew): `tsp <cmd>` enqueues, the per-user spooler daemonises itself on first use over a Unix-domain socket (no `systemd` unit, no root, no config file), runs jobs one-at-a-time in submission order with stdout / stderr captured to per-job temp files you can `tsp -c <id>` (cat finished output) / `tsp -t <id>` (tail running) / `tsp -W <id>` (block until done, exit with its code), bumps to N parallel slots with `tsp -S <n>`, and the `-G <gpus>` / `-d` (depend on previous) / `-m <addr>` (email on done) / `-L <label>` flags cover "queue 40 GPU training runs serially with one GPU each, chain an eval after each, email me" without writing a wrapper; pick over `at` / `batch` (system-wide, root-owned, no live tail), over GNU `parallel` (one-shot fan-out across a finite input — task-spooler is a long-lived queue accepting new jobs over its lifetime), and over [`pueue`](clis/pueue/) when the strictly-simpler "one C binary, no daemon to manage, predates pueue by 15 years, installs on systems where shipping a Rust toolchain is not on the table" shape is the right tool — not for multi-node fan-out (use Slurm / Ray / Nomad) or reproducible declarative pipelines (use Snakemake / Nextflow) (GPL-2.0, v2.0.0)
- [plow](clis/plow/) — single-Go-binary **HTTP load generator with simultaneous live terminal table + self-served web-UI charts**: hammers a URL at fixed concurrency (`-c`) or fixed RPS (`-q`) for `-d <duration>` or `-n <count>`, computes a real per-status-code latency histogram (p50 / p75 / p90 / p95 / p99 / p99.9 + min / mean / max) that redraws live in the terminal **and** ships the same data to a built-in `--listen :18888` web UI with sparkline charts of QPS / latency / status mix so a coworker can watch from a browser without screen-share; covers `POST` with body from string / file / `-d @body.json`, mTLS via `--cert` / `--key`, `--http2`, `--insecure`, and per-request body templating from CSV / JSONL (`-B body.tmpl`) so a 100k-RPS test isn't pounding the same payload, with `-o json` final-summary output suitable for diffing across runs as a CI perf gate; pick over `ab` (single-threaded view of multi-threaded run, no live UI), over `wrk` / `wrk2` (excellent throughput numbers but Lua scripting + no live web UI + no per-status grouping), over [`oha`](clis/oha/) specifically when the audience is more than one person — the web-UI-served-on-a-port shape is the differentiator — and over `vegeta` when *during-the-run* visibility matters more than file-in / file-out batch processing; not for multi-step user journeys with sessions (use k6 / Locust) or distributed load from many generator nodes (Apache-2.0, v1.4.0)
- [micro](clis/micro/) — single-static-Go-binary **terminal text editor with desktop-keyboard muscle-memory built in**: Ctrl-S saves, Ctrl-C / Ctrl-V are the system clipboard, Ctrl-Z undoes, Ctrl-F finds, Ctrl-D adds the next occurrence to a multi-cursor selection à la VS Code / Sublime — over a true-color 256-color TUI with mouse drag-select, syntax highlighting for ~140 languages out of the box, horizontal / vertical pane splits (`Ctrl-E vsplit`), an integrated terminal pane (`Ctrl-E term`) for running tests next to the code, JSON `~/.config/micro/settings.json` config (no new DSL), and a Lua plugin manager (`micro -plugin install lsp filemanager linter`) for opt-in LSP / linting / sidebar; the whole thing is ~12 MB with zero runtime deps, drops onto a fresh Alpine box with one `apk add micro` and onto a busybox container with `curl https://getmic.ro | bash` — pick when SSH'd into a teammate's debug host, you have to edit one config file, and you do not want to deliver a 30-minute vim tutorial first (Ctrl-S means *Save*, full stop, no `:` and no `Esc`); not the right pick as a primary IDE on a 200k-LOC monorepo (use neovim / [`helix`](clis/helix/) / VS Code with real LSP setup) or for `vimdiff`-class 3-way merges (use [`mergiraf`](clis/mergiraf/)) (MIT, v2.0.15)

## Previously latest additions (+3)

- [pdfcpu](clis/pdfcpu/) — pure-Go PDF processor + validator + form filler in one Apache-2.0 single static binary; `pdfcpu validate` is a real PDF-1.7 / ISO 32000-1 conformance gate suitable for CI, `pdfcpu optimize` linearises and dedupes streams (often 30–60 % smaller), and `watermark` / `nup` / `merge` / `split` / `encrypt` / `form export` cover the day-to-day batch transforms without dragging Ghostscript / Poppler / Java native deps into a distroless container — pick when the ask is "PDF surgery in a 30 MB scratch image as part of a Go pipeline", not for high-fidelity rasterisation (use `mutool` / Ghostscript) or OCR (pair with `tesseract` / [`marker`](clis/marker/)) (Apache-2.0, v0.11.0)
- [typst](clis/typst/) — modern Rust-native typesetting system that compiles `.typ` source to PDF / PNG / SVG with sub-second incremental builds and `typst watch` live preview; the styling language is a real type-checked expression language (`#let`, `#show`, `#set`) instead of TeX macro expansion, the math typesetter approaches LaTeX quality on common formulas, and the `@preview/...` package ecosystem covers CV / thesis / slides / cetz drawing — pick when authoring greenfield documents (papers, theses, generated reports in CI) and you want LaTeX-class output without a multi-GB TeX Live install, not when you must collaborate on existing LaTeX manuscripts or when the venue mandates a specific journal `.cls` (Apache-2.0, v0.13.1)
- [goaccess](clis/goaccess/) — single-C-binary real-time web log analyzer that turns Apache / Nginx / Caddy JSON / CloudFront / ELB / S3 / traefik / Squid access logs into either an `ncurses` dashboard, a self-updating HTML report driven over WebSocket, or JSON / CSV for downstream pipelines — visitors / requests / 404s / referrers / browsers / GeoIP-mapped hosts / status codes computed in-process against a custom in-memory hash store (or a persistent on-disk store with `--persist` for incremental updates); pick when you have access logs on disk and need a 30-second answer to "where is this traffic spike coming from" or a permanent NOC dashboard, not when the ask is application-level metrics (Prometheus + Grafana) or full-text log search (Loki / [`lnav`](clis/lnav/)) (MIT, v1.9.4)

## Previously added (+3)

- [wtfutil](clis/wtfutil/) — single-Go-binary personal terminal dashboard: a YAML grid (`~/.config/wtf/config.yml`) tiles widgets — multi-timezone clocks, GitHub / GitLab / Gitea PR queues, Jira / Trello / Asana / GitHub Issues backlogs, Google + ICS calendars, CI status (CircleCI, Jenkins, Gitlab CI), Datadog / Grafana / New Relic / Prometheus / Zabbix alerts, AWS / GCP / DigitalOcean inventory, weather, RSS, OpsGenie, PagerDuty, plus arbitrary `cmdrunner` widgets that just shell out and render stdout — onto one full-screen TUI with per-widget refresh intervals; the right pick when the morning-standup ask is "one terminal pane that shows my PRs + my Jira + my on-call + my calendar + the build status I care about, side by side, refreshing on its own, over SSH" rather than a browser fan of dashboards or a single-domain TUI like [`gh-dash`](clis/gh-dash/) / [`k9s`](clis/k9s/) / [`btop`](clis/btop/) (MPL-2.0, v0.49.1)
- [aerc](clis/aerc/) — modern terminal email client in Go covering IMAP / JMAP / Maildir / notmuch / mbox accounts behind one vi-modal three-pane TUI, with first-class `git send-email` patch review (`:pipe -m | git am -3`, `:patch new`, `:patch-apply`) that round-trips proper `git-format-patch` series — the reason it has the following it does inside kernel-style mailing-list communities; pick over [`himalaya`](clis/himalaya/) (CLI-shaped, scripts cleanly but no persistent UI) when the workload is a high-volume mailing list or you want IMAP + JMAP + notmuch behind one consistent UI, and over neomutt when you want JMAP and patch review without authoring a 600-line muttrc — but never for one-shot scripted "send mail from CI" jobs (use `himalaya` / `mailx` / `msmtp`) (MIT, 0.21.0)
- [newsboat](clis/newsboat/) — synchronous personal RSS / Atom reader: stacked feed-list / article-list / article-body TUI with vi keys over a local SQLite cache, tag-grouped feeds, SQL-shaped query feeds (`"unread == \"yes\" and age < 7"`), OPML import / export, sync against self-hosted Tiny Tiny RSS / Newsblur / Miniflux / FreshRSS / Inoreader / FeedHQ / Owncloud-News, podcast enclosures via the bundled `podboat`, macros to pipe the open article into `$BROWSER` / `pandoc` / Pocket; pick when "one terminal pane, vi keys, 200 feeds, mark them read locally, batch-open URLs over SSH" is the actual ask — not for podcast-first workflows or full-text search across years of archives (MIT, r2.43)

## Previously added (+3)

- [wasmer](clis/wasmer/) — universal WebAssembly runtime with pluggable Cranelift / LLVM / Singlepass backends and the WASIX extension that adds threads / sockets / fork-exec on top of stock WASI; pairs a `wasmer run` execution path with `wasmer publish` to a public package registry so a `.wasm` artifact can be installed and run by name (`wasmer run wasmer/python -- -c ...`) the way npm distributes Node packages — pick over [`wasmtime`](clis/wasmtime/) when you want WASIX (richer POSIX surface) and the registry-as-distribution model rather than wasmtime's stricter upstream-WASI focus
- [jj](clis/jj/) — git-compatible VCS frontend (Jujutsu) where the working copy is itself a mutable revision auto-amended on every command, branches are anonymous by default, conflicts are first-class objects stored *inside* commits instead of blocking the operation, and every operation is reversible via `jj op log` + `jj undo`; backs onto a real `.git` directory so `git push` / `git fetch` / GitHub remain usable on the same checkout — the right pick when stacked-PR rewriting (`jj split` / `jj squash` / `jj rebase -d`) and "never get stuck mid-rebase" matter more than muscle-memory continuity with `git`
- [process-compose](clis/process-compose/) — Docker-Compose-shaped orchestrator for plain OS processes: `process-compose.yaml` declares processes with `command` / `depends_on` / `readiness_probe` / `liveness_probe` / restart policies, and `process-compose up` runs the supervised tree under a curses TUI with live logs and per-process start/stop/restart; no container runtime, no daemon — the gap-filler between a `Procfile` + `foreman` and spinning up `docker compose` for a local dev stack on macOS where the container layer is the slowest part of the inner loop

## Previously added (+3)

- [kcat](clis/kcat/) — non-JVM, single-binary Kafka producer/consumer/metadata client built on `librdkafka`; `-f` format strings + `-Z` null-payload distinction + libserdes-backed Avro/JSON-Schema/Protobuf decode make it the right shell-pipeline tool for Kafka, Redpanda, MSK, and any Kafka-protocol broker — orders of magnitude faster cold start than the bundled `kafka-console-*.sh` JVM scripts
- [natscli](clis/natscli/) — official CLI for NATS covering core pub/sub, request/reply, JetStream persistent streams + durable consumers, KV store, object store, decentralized JWT/NKey account management, and built-in `nats bench` latency/throughput harness in one Go binary; `nats context` saves named credential bundles for clean dev/staging/prod switching
- [treefmt](clis/treefmt/) — polyglot formatter orchestrator: one `treefmt.toml` maps globs to underlying formatters (`gofmt`, `rustfmt`, `prettier`, `shfmt`, `nixpkgs-fmt`, `taplo`, ...), runs each in parallel on its file batch, content-hashes results into a per-project cache so reruns are sub-second incremental, and `--fail-on-change` makes it a one-line CI gate

## Previously added (+3)

- [ko](clis/ko/) — daemonless container builder for Go services: `ko build ./cmd/server` cross-compiles, layers on a distroless base, and pushes to the registry without a Dockerfile or a Docker daemon; `ko apply -f config/` rewrites `image: ko://...` references in Kubernetes manifests with the freshly-pushed digest before piping to `kubectl apply` — reproducible byte-identical images across CI hosts, automatic SBOMs, and the Dockerfile-authoring layer deleted from the workflow for Go-only services
- [regctl](clis/regctl/) — daemonless OCI registry client (regclient project) covering the same ground as `crane` and `skopeo` with first-class OCI 1.1 referrers (Sigstore signatures / attestations / SBOMs attached to a digest), `image copy` that mirrors between registries without local pull, `image mod` for non-destructive entrypoint / timestamp / layer edits that emit a new digest without rebuilding, and a sibling `regsync` daemon for declarative YAML-driven mirror policies — the right pick when OCI 1.1 referrer support and air-gap mirroring matter
- [cue](clis/cue/) — typed configuration language that unifies schema, constraint, and data: `cue vet data.yaml schema.cue` validates JSON / YAML against typed CUE schemas, `cue export` renders to JSON / YAML / TOML, and the unification operator merges configurations from multiple sources with refuse-to-merge semantics so cross-team override drift surfaces at vet time, not in production; the substrate underneath [`timoni`](clis/timoni/) and a credible replacement for the "JSON Schema for validation + Helm templates for generation + YAML anchors for reuse" stack

## Previously added (+3)

- [osquery](clis/osquery/) — exposes the OS as a relational database (processes, listening sockets, kernel modules, installed packages, file hashes, scheduled tasks — hundreds of tables) so fleet introspection becomes one `SELECT` instead of per-OS shell scripts; `osqueryi` for ad-hoc, `osqueryd` for scheduled query packs that diff host state and ship results to a logger pipeline (Fluent Bit, Vector) — defensive endpoint observability, no payload execution
- [rekor](clis/rekor/) — Sigstore transparency-log CLI: uploads, queries, and verifies signatures / attestations / in-toto statements / SBOMs against the public `rekor.sigstore.dev` Merkle log (or a self-hosted instance) so every `cosign` / `gitsign` signature lands in a tamper-evident, timestamped, publicly auditable record — closes the supply-chain loop a leaked Fulcio cert would otherwise leave open forever
- [timoni](clis/timoni/) — Helm-without-text-templating: modules are written in CUE with a typed `#Config` schema (errors surface at `mod build` time, not at `kubectl apply` time), distributed as OCI artifacts in any standard registry, signed with cosign, reconciled to the cluster via server-side apply with bundle-level prune semantics — the package manager for teams already running OCI-distributed everything

## Previously added (+3)

- [kubectl-tree](clis/kubectl-tree/) — `kubectl` plugin that walks `metadata.ownerReferences` downward from any root object (Deployment, Helm release Secret, Argo `Application`, Crossplane composite, custom CR) and renders the controller-spawned subtree as ASCII; CRD-aware via API discovery, `--show-images` decorates Pods, `-A` widens across namespaces — owner-graph snapshot, not a selector-graph or live-watch tool
- [kubespy](clis/kubespy/) — single-object live watcher with three lenses (`trace` for human-readable transitions, `changes` for RFC-6902 JSON-Patch diffs per tick, `status` for `.status`-subtree-only renders); the right tool when an object is *changing* and you need the trajectory rather than the final snapshot — read-only, dormant upstream since 2024 but still the canonical "watch this CR walk through its phases" probe
- [kubefwd](clis/kubefwd/) — bulk port-forward + `/etc/hosts` DNS bridging; `sudo -E kubefwd services -n <ns>` forwards every Service in one or more namespaces and writes hosts entries so locally-running code resolves `postgres`, `redis`, `api.dev` exactly as it would from inside a Pod, then reaps the entries on shutdown — laptop-as-Pod for integration tests against an existing cluster

## Previously added (+3)

- [conftest](clis/conftest/) — OPA Rego policy test runner for Kubernetes / Terraform / Dockerfile / Helm / arbitrary YAML/JSON/HCL; one binary, no server, policies live in `policy/*.rego` next to the code they govern, exits non-zero in CI when a `deny` rule fires — policy-as-code unit tests, not an admission controller
- [checkov](clis/checkov/) — multi-framework IaC misconfiguration scanner (Terraform / CloudFormation / Kubernetes / Helm / Dockerfile / GitHub Actions / ARM / Bicep / Serverless) with ~1000 built-in policies mapped to CIS / NIST / PCI / HIPAA, SARIF + baseline workflow for adopting on existing repos — broad batteries-included IaC scanning
- [glasskube](clis/glasskube/) — Kubernetes package manager treating each install as a `Package` CRD reconciled by an in-cluster operator (transitive dependency resolution, `glasskube update`, local web UI via `glasskube serve`); `apt`/`brew`-style ergonomics for cluster add-ons — Helm alternative for teams that want operator-driven app lifecycle

## Previously added (+3)

- [skaffold](clis/skaffold/) — inner-loop build/deploy orchestrator for Kubernetes; one `skaffold.yaml` watches source, rebuilds container images, side-loads or pushes them, and reapplies Helm / Kustomize / raw manifests with port-forward + log-tail wired through `dev` mode — the edit-save-see-it-running loop for "my code only makes sense inside a cluster"
- [flipt](clis/flipt/) — self-hosted feature-flag + dynamic-config server (GPL-3.0) shipped as one Go binary with HTTP/gRPC + OpenFeature/OFREP APIs; v2 stores flags as YAML in a Git repo so flag changes go through the same PR review as code — feature flags without a SaaS tether
- [litestream](clis/litestream/) — streaming WAL replication for SQLite to S3 / GCS / Azure Blob / SFTP with second-level RPO and point-in-time restore; no fork of SQLite, no app library, the same binary handles backup and restore — durable single-node SQLite for ephemeral compute

## Latest additions (+3)

- [kube-linter](clis/kube-linter/) — static analyzer for Kubernetes YAML and rendered Helm charts (Go, Apache-2.0, v0.8.3) by stackrox: walks a directory of manifests against ~50 built-in production-readiness + security checks (missing `livenessProbe` / `readinessProbe`, `runAsNonRoot: false`, `privileged: true`, `hostNetwork`, missing CPU/memory requests, latest-tag images, default ServiceAccount mounted, dangling NetworkPolicy selectors, deprecated APIs) and emits SARIF / JSON / plain text suitable for CI gating; no API server round-trip and no admission webhook so it finishes in a couple seconds even for hundreds of manifests, custom checks are pure YAML (`templates: [run-as-non-root]` matched against `objectKinds: [Pod, Deployment, StatefulSet]` with selectors) so a platform team can ship an org-wide policy file without writing Go or Rego, pairs naturally with `helm template ... | kube-linter lint -` for chart authors who want the *rendered* output linted; sits in the gap between [`kubeconform`](clis/kubeconform/) (schema-only, no policy) and runtime admission ([`kyverno`](clis/kyverno/) / OPA Gatekeeper), and complements [`kubescape`](clis/kubescape/) which scans live cluster posture rather than YAML on disk
- [dufs](clis/dufs/) — single-binary HTTP file server with built-in web UI, WebDAV, upload / delete / search, per-path basic-auth ACLs, resumable uploads, and TLS (Rust, dual MIT / Apache-2.0, v0.45.0) by sigoden: `dufs .` answers `GET` / `PUT` / `DELETE` / `PROPFIND` / `MKCOL` on the current directory so you can `curl -T file.bin http://host:5000/` *or* mount the same URL from Finder / Explorer / `davfs2` as a network drive; web UI does folder browsing, multi-select zip download, drag-and-drop upload with progress, in-browser preview for text/images/PDFs/video, and a search box that walks the tree; per-path auth (`-a /admin@user:pass:rw`, `-a /pub@*:r`) lets one binary host a public read share and a private read-write share without a reverse proxy; ~8 MB binary that runs on Linux / macOS / Windows / FreeBSD / Android (Termux) — the right pick when `python -m http.server` is too thin (no uploads, no auth, no WebDAV) but Nextcloud / Filebrowser is overkill, and a clean WebDAV target for `rclone`, iOS Files.app, or self-hosted Joplin / Obsidian sync
- [kaf](clis/kaf/) — modern single-binary Kafka CLI (Go, Apache-2.0, v0.2.14) by birdayz: `kcat`-class producer / consumer / metadata client with first-class support for *named clusters* (`kaf config add-cluster prod --brokers ... --tls --schema-registry-url ...`, then `kaf config use-cluster prod` the way `kubectl` switches contexts), Schema-Registry-aware decode of Confluent-framed Avro / Protobuf / JSON-Schema, consumer-group inspection + offset reset by timestamp or by partition, topic create / alter / delete, and human-friendly TTY output instead of JVM `kafka-console-*.sh` startup latency; the right pick when you move between several clusters per day and want named contexts baked into the CLI, or when topics carry registry-framed payloads that you need decoded inline; pick [`kcat`](clis/kcat/) instead when you want `librdkafka`-grade format-string ergonomics (`-f '%T %k %s\n'`) for one-cluster shell pipelines, and reach for `cruise-control` / the bundled `kafka-*.sh` scripts for broker-side admin (rebalance plans, JBOD, KRaft metadata surgery) that kaf intentionally does not cover

## Latest additions (+3)

- [quickwit](clis/quickwit/) — cloud-native distributed search engine for logs and traces (Rust, Apache-2.0, v0.8.2) by quickwit-oss: indexer writes immutable `tantivy` splits straight to S3 / GCS / Azure Blob, a metastore (Postgres or a file in the same bucket) tracks split metadata, and stateless searcher pods load only the byte ranges a query needs — so storage and query scale independently and the bill is roughly "S3 storage cost of the raw logs"; serves an Elasticsearch-compatible `_search` API and Jaeger-compatible OTLP trace ingestion from the same binary, which collapses two stateful clusters (ES + Cassandra-backed Jaeger) into one stateless deployment in front of object storage; sits in the gap between Loki-class label-only stores (cheap, but you can't full-text-search a stack trace) and hot-replica Elasticsearch (full-text, but the bill assumes everything stays warm); pair with [`vector`](clis/vector/) or `fluent-bit` as the shipper, skip when freshness must be sub-second (commit cadence is tens of seconds by design) or when `ripgrep` over a rotated log dir on one box is still answering every question
- [sqruff](clis/sqruff/) — Rust SQL linter + auto-formatter, port of the sqlfluff rule catalog (Apache-2.0, v0.38.0) by quarylabs: same rule philosophy as [`sqlfluff`](clis/sqlfluff/) (keyword casing, comma placement, ambiguous joins, JINJA-aware templating, alias requirements, ten dialects covering BigQuery / ClickHouse / Databricks / DuckDB / Postgres / Redshift / Snowflake / SQLite / Spark / T-SQL) but rewritten on a hand-built grammar so cold start drops from "Python import + plugin discovery" to ~10ms and a `--fix` pass on a few hundred files finishes inside one CI second; `.sqruff` config is a TOML superset overlapping enough with sqlfluff's `.sqlfluff` rule IDs that a team can migrate file-by-file rather than big-bang; the right pick for polyglot warehouses (BigQuery + Snowflake + DuckDB in the same repo) where you already enjoy the `ruff` "Rust-fast linter that auto-fixes" experience for Python and want it for SQL — not a replacement for `sqlmesh` / `dbt` `defer` (no catalog-aware lineage, no type checking) and stay on `sqlfluff` for any rule sqruff hasn't ported yet
- [pyrefly](clis/pyrefly/) — fast static type checker for Python in Rust by Meta (MIT, 0.63.1): from-scratch bidirectional inference on top of standard PEP 484 / 526 / 604 / 695 annotations, persistent incremental state so a recheck after a 10-line edit on a 1k-file project is tens of milliseconds rather than `mypy --cache-fine-grained` cold-start seconds, and the same binary doubles as a language server (`pyrefly lsp --stdio`) that VS Code / Neovim / Helix can drive — collapses the long-standing Pyright-Node-vs-mypy-Python split into one Rust binary; orthogonal to [`ruff`](clis/ruff/) (lints + formats lexically; doesn't reason about types) and to [`ty`](clis/ty/) (Astral's competing in-development Rust type checker — both are alpha-grade and racing to mypy/Pyright parity, pick whichever passes on your code today); skip when you depend on a `mypy` plugin (SQLAlchemy, Django stubs, attrs narrowing) since pyrefly does not implement the mypy plugin protocol, or when an external contract demands Pyright-byte-identical diagnostics

## Latest additions (+3)

- [logdy](clis/logdy/) — local-first realtime log viewer with a built-in web UI (Go, Apache-2.0, v0.17.1) by logdyhq: pipe stdin in (or point at one or more files), it spawns a `localhost:8080` HTTP server with a single-page UI that streams new lines over a WebSocket, parses each line as JSON when possible, lets you write inline TypeScript snippets to extract / rename / colorize fields, and gives you persistent filters, multi-tail merging, and follow-mode toggling that `tail -f | grep` and `less +F` cannot — sits in the gap between "I'll just `tail`" (no structure, no scrollback search without losing the live tail) and Loki/Grafana (great, but not for one-off debug); compare to `frontail` (older, unmaintained, no JSON parsing) or `lnav` (TUI, not shareable over an SSH tunnel); skip when you've already adopted Vector → Loki → Grafana or when the binary's ~45 MB embedded UI bothers you for a tiny container
- [hexpatch](clis/hexpatch/) — TUI binary patcher and hex editor that knows what it's looking at (Rust, MIT, v1.12.6) by Etto48: opens any file as hex, decodes it as ELF / Mach-O / PE when it recognizes the magic, shows Capstone disassembly side-by-side with the raw bytes, and lets you rewrite an instruction as assembly (`mov rax, 0`) which Keystone re-assembles to bytes and writes back **in place** — the thing GUI hex editors like `hexedit` / `bvi` / `heh` make you do by hand. Speaks x86 / x86-64 / aarch64 / arm out of the box, parses headers so you navigate by section / symbol instead of byte offset, ships an SSH/SFTP mode so you can patch a binary on a remote box without scping it back and forth (CTF boxes, embedded targets, "the bug only repros on staging"), Lua scripting for custom keybindings — fills the niche of Ghidra's "Patch Instruction" or Binary Ninja's "Make Code" as a 24 MB CLI; not a replacement for radare2 / Ghidra for actual analysis, and the in-memory model isn't suited to disk-image-sized files
- [f2](clis/f2/) — cross-platform safe-by-default batch renamer (Go, MIT, v2.2.2) by Ayooluwa Isaiah: every rename is a dry-run preview until you re-run with `-x`, conflicts (case-fold collisions on Windows / macOS HFS+, identical targets, target-overwrites-source) are caught and refused with a structured report, persistent undo via backup file in `$XDG_DATA_HOME/f2` survives a reboot, and the replacement template language references regex capture groups, EXIF tags from photos, ID3 tags from audio, MIME type, mtime, sequential indices with width specifiers, random tokens — replaces `mmv`, the Perl `rename`, the util-linux `rename`, `vimv`, `qmv` and most one-off `for f in *; do mv ...` shell loops for the "I have 4000 photos to reorganize by EXIF date taken" case; v2.x split the rename engine into a reusable Go library; skip when the rename is genuinely one-shot (`mv old new` is shorter) or when you'd rather edit names in `$EDITOR` (`vidir` from moreutils)

## Previously added (+3)

- [csvtk](clis/csvtk/) — cross-platform CSV / TSV swiss-army knife (Go, MIT, v0.37.0): ~30 subcommands (`cut`, `filter2`, `freq`, `join`, `mutate2`, `pretty`, `summary`, `plot`, `csv2json`, ...) with named-column addressing (`-f sample,vaf` instead of `-f 1,4`) so pipelines survive upstream column reorders, native LZ4 read/write since v0.37.0, ships from a single static binary, sits between `xsv`/`qsv` (fast but limited) and pandas (full but you wrote a script) — reach for it when an ad-hoc TSV question wants an answer in one pipe rather than a notebook
- [peaclock](clis/peaclock/) — responsive terminal clock / timer / stopwatch (C++17, MIT, v0.4.3): three modes (clock / timer / stopwatch) × three views (ASCII / digital / binary), auto-resizes to fill the terminal with optional aspect-ratio lock, 24-bit color + truecolor "party mode", custom date strings + locale + timezone overrides, `timer-exec "say break time"` shell hook on timer completion, live `:`-prompt for runtime reconfiguration with fuzzy command history and `mkconfig!` to dump current state back to `~/.peaclock/config` — the rare TUI clock that earns the "spare-monitor display" slot because it actually behaves like a desktop widget
- [anew](clis/anew/) — append-unique line filter (Go, MIT, v0.1.1) by tomnomnom: reads stdin, appends only first-time lines to a target file, *and* echoes those new lines to stdout — the missing primitive between `tee -a` (keeps duplicates) and `sort -u` (loses order, rereads the world); two flags total (`-d` dry-run, `-q` quiet), composes with every other line-oriented Unix tool to give you incremental pipelines for free ("only process what's new since last run") — incremental crawls, append-only changelogs, watching logs for previously-unseen error signatures, building corpora from streamed inputs

## Previously added (+3)

- [beets](clis/beets/) — music library tagger and organizer (Python, MIT): point it at a tree of audio files, identify each release against MusicBrainz / Discogs / Spotify, rewrite tags + filenames into a configurable canonical layout, store everything in a queryable SQLite library, plugins are 20-line Python files in `~/.config/beets/beetsplug/` — the only mature scriptable Unix tool that turns a 30 GB pile of inconsistently-tagged audio into a database-backed collection downstream tools (HTTP serve, embeddings, audio-ML datasets) can rely on for stable identifiers and consistent paths
- [gifsicle](clis/gifsicle/) — Eddie Kohler's canonical GIF manipulator (C, GPL-2.0): combine PNG frames into an animation (`--delay --loopcount`), explode an animation back to frames, crop / resize / dither / remap colors, strip metadata, and `-O3` losslessly optimize an existing animation by detecting inter-frame redundancy and shipping each frame as a minimal partial-frame disposal patch (often 30-70 % smaller, no visible quality loss); the post-processing tail of every screen-recording / terminal-cast / model-output GIF pipeline (vhs / t-rec / asciinema-to-GIF / ffmpeg-rendered training curves), in every distro repo, ~150 KB binary, flag set stable across decades — install once and never think about it again
- [vnstat](clis/vnstat/) — lightweight network-traffic counter (C, GPL-2.0): polls per-interface RX / TX byte counters from `/proc/net/dev` (Linux) / `getifaddrs` (BSD/macOS) on a 5-min cadence and rolls up into a tiny on-disk database with hourly / daily / monthly / yearly / top-day views, no packet capture and no root unlike `iftop` / `nethogs` / `bandwhich`, so CPU is essentially zero and a low-power VPS over SSH is fine; `vnstat --json m` emits month rollups as JSON for `jq`, v2.13 added `--db` for ad-hoc database queries and exit-status differentiation in `--alert` mode (cron can distinguish "threshold crossed" from "vnstat errored") — the long-horizon counter `bandwhich` (real-time per-process) and `iperf3` (active throughput tests) cannot give you, and the right answer for "how much egress did this box actually move last month?"

## Previously added (+3)

- [sttr](clis/sttr/) — single-Go-binary string-transform multitool: ~40 sub-commands covering base32/base64 encode/decode, URL escape, MD5/SHA-1/SHA-256/SHA-512, JWT decode, JSON↔YAML, camelCase ↔ snake_case ↔ kebab-case ↔ PascalCase, slugify, ROT13/Caesar/Morse, UUID generate, Bcrypt, line sort/dedupe, Markdown→HTML, ASCII QR emit; pipe-friendly (`echo hunter2 | sttr md5 | sttr base64-encode`) plus an interactive picker (`sttr i`) for when you forget the exact transform name — the right pick when your shell already has 12 muscle-memory invocations of `openssl` / `xxd` / `base64` / `uuidgen` / `qrencode` / `python -c` and you'd rather collapse them into one binary; not a crypto library and not for hashing multi-GB files (use `b3sum` / `sha256sum` directly) (MIT, v0.2.30)
- [gh-poi](clis/gh-poi/) — `gh` CLI extension that walks every local branch, asks GitHub whether the associated PR is `merged` or `closed`, classifies each as deletable / not / unknown, previews a colored tree with PR numbers + states, then runs `git branch -D` on the safe subset; correctly reaps **squash-merged** branches that `git branch --merged` lies about (because the squash creates a different commit hash on `main`), and v0.17.0's `--scan deep` walks PRs from all configured remotes (fork + upstream) so the "I PR'd from my fork, then forgot the local branch" case stops accumulating — pairs with [`git-branchless`](clis/git-branchless/) for non-PR cleanup; not a fit for non-GitHub remotes (no GitLab / Gitea PR API) (MIT, v0.17.0)
- [spr](clis/spr/) — Rust stacked-PR tool that maps **each local commit** on your feature branch to a separate GitHub PR (one commit ↔ one PR, with `spr/<author>/<branch>/<n>` head refs), injecting a machine-readable trailer into each commit message so the next `spr diff` can find and update the right PR; `spr land` merges the bottom of the stack and rebases the rest cleanly, `spr amend` targets a specific commit for `git commit --fixup`-style edits without rewriting the whole branch, GitHub device-flow auth in v1.3.7 — the right pick when your team has agreed reviewable units are *commits* not *branches*; pick [`git-spice`](clis/git-spice/) instead for branch-per-change stacks, [`jj`](clis/jj/) for the same problem at the VCS layer, [`git-machete`](clis/git-machete/) for visualizing/restacking already-pushed branch stacks (MIT, v1.3.7)

## Previously added (+3)

- [atlas](clis/atlas/) — schema-as-code for Postgres / MySQL / MariaDB / SQLite / SQL Server / ClickHouse / Redshift / TiDB; declare the desired schema in HCL or SQL, `atlas migrate diff` computes the migration against an ephemeral *dev database* (`docker://postgres/16/dev`) so the plan is validated before it ships, `atlas migrate lint` catches destructive ops + missing concurrent-index hints + backward-incompatibility against the previous migration, `atlas schema apply` does declarative reconcile when versioned migrations are overkill — the cross-engine diff engine the migration space was missing
- [gitsign](clis/gitsign/) — keyless git commit / tag signing via Sigstore: drops in as `gpg.x509.program`, OIDC identity (Google / GitHub / GitLab / your issuer) → ephemeral Fulcio cert → signature → Rekor transparency log → throw the key away; verification policy becomes "signed by `*@orgname` from `accounts.orgname.com`" instead of a wiki page of GPG fingerprints, and the same identity composes with [`cosign`](clis/cosign/) on container images and [`oras`](clis/oras/) on arbitrary artifacts — kill the long-lived signing key
- [nb](clis/nb/) — single-Bash-script notes / bookmarks / tasks CLI where every notebook is a git repo (own remote, own optional AES-256 encryption, own sync cadence), every change is a commit, every bookmark is a fetched + archived single-file HTML snapshot so link rot stops mattering, and the storage is plain files you can `cat` + `grep` + `git log` without the tool installed; ships a TUI (`nb browse`), a web server (`nb browse --serve`), tasks, tags, and search — the middle ground between Notion-class all-in-ones and "just put Markdown in a git repo"

## Previously added (+3)

- [oras](clis/oras/) — push / pull arbitrary artifacts (SBOMs, model weights, prompt bundles, WASM modules, plain tarballs) to any OCI-compliant registry, addressed by digest, mirrored by the same registry replication, gated by the same auth — turn `your-registry.example.com` into a generic content-addressable artifact store; the upstream the rest of the OCI artifact ecosystem (notation, cosign attach, in-toto attestations) standardized around — OCI registry as artifact storage
- [kubeshark](clis/kubeshark/) — eBPF + libpcap per-node tap that reconstructs L7 traffic (HTTP/1, HTTP/2, gRPC, AMQP, Kafka, Redis, DNS) flowing between pods into a filterable, replayable, diff-able session view; zero code changes, zero sidecar, zero mesh — drop in, see what services actually say to each other, tear back down — Wireshark for clusters
- [infracost](clis/infracost/) — reads Terraform / OpenTofu plans and produces a cloud-bill estimate for what would be created / modified / destroyed across AWS, Azure, GCP; `infracost diff` puts a dollar number on every PR before merge so the change that quietly turns a $40/month NAT into a $4,000/month one fails review instead of fails the next month's invoice — FinOps as a per-PR signal, not a quarterly retro

## Previously added (+3)

- [natscli](clis/natscli/) — official NATS CLI: pub/sub on the wire, JetStream stream + consumer + KV + object-store admin as subjects, `nats bench` coordinated load tests with latency histograms, multi-environment contexts (`nats context add prod --creds=…`); the operational counterpart to `nats-server` you reach for whenever the answer is not "write a Go program" — operate / debug / benchmark NATS without client code
- [pgroll](clis/pgroll/) — zero-downtime Postgres schema migrations via expand / contract: each migration creates a new view alongside the old with triggers keeping the underlying table in sync, so old + new app versions coexist during deploy and a destructive change (drop column, rename table, tighten constraint, swap type) ships without a maintenance window or N+1 / N / N-1 application shim; reversible until `pgroll complete` — destructive Postgres migrations as feature flags, not one-way doors
- [himalaya](clis/himalaya/) — modern scriptable email CLI / TUI: one Rust binary speaks IMAP / Maildir / Notmuch / JMAP for read and SMTP / Sendmail for send (backend is a config choice), `-o json` everywhere makes envelopes pipe into `jq`, OAuth2 + PKCE for major providers with refresh tokens in the system keyring, three-pane vim-key TUI built on the same JSON library so behavior cannot drift; replaces the `mbsync` + `notmuch` + `neomutt` + `msmtp` + custom-config stack for everyone who wants email in `tmux` without a weekend to assemble it — terminal email without the 1996 ergonomics tax

## Previously added (+3)

- [kubeconform](clis/kubeconform/) — fast, offline Kubernetes manifest schema validator (kubeval successor); parallel by default, CRD-aware via `-schema-location` templates, drops into pre-commit / CI without the validation-step tax — schema validation, not policy
- [popeye](clis/popeye/) — read-only Kubernetes cluster sanitizer that grades each namespace and resource kind (dangling Services, missing limits, unused ConfigMaps, RBAC sprawl) with HTML / JUnit output for recurring report-card cron — drift visibility week-over-week
- [dockle](clis/dockle/) — container image linter for CIS Docker Benchmark + best-practice checks (root user, missing `HEALTHCHECK`, leaked `.npmrc` / `id_rsa`); complements (does not replace) a CVE scanner — image hygiene, not vulnerabilities

## Previously added

- [pulumi](clis/pulumi/) — IaC engine where AWS / Azure / GCP / Kubernetes resources are declared in TypeScript / Python / Go / .NET against a stateful resource graph with `preview` / `up` / `import` lifecycle — IaC in real languages
- [flux](clis/flux/) — CNCF GitOps controller suite for Kubernetes; `GitRepository` / `OCIRepository` sources reconciled by `Kustomization` / `HelmRelease` controllers with image-automation closing the registry → manifest loop — pull-based GitOps without a central CD server
- [kyverno](clis/kyverno/) — CNCF policy engine where `ClusterPolicy` CRDs in plain YAML do admission validation / mutation / generation / image-signature verification with no Rego, runnable offline as a CI gate — Kubernetes-native policy without a separate DSL

## Earlier rotation

- [pandoc](clis/pandoc/) — Haskell universal document converter with ~40 readers and a larger set of writers, Lua filters operating on the parsed AST, and built-in CSL `--citeproc`, GPL-2.0-or-later — author once, emit Markdown / PDF / docx / EPUB / slide deck from one source
- [aria2](clis/aria2/) — multi-protocol (HTTP/FTP/SFTP/BitTorrent/Metalink) segmented downloader with multi-source mirror splitting and a JSON-RPC control surface, GPL-2.0-or-later with OpenSSL exception — saturate the link when bytes-per-second is the constraint
- [smassh](clis/smassh/) — local-first Textual TUI typing trainer with custom corpora and JSONL result history that never leaves the machine, MIT — keyboard speed practice without a SaaS account
- [kubescape](clis/kubescape/) — OPA-backed Kubernetes / IaC posture scanner mapped to NSA / MITRE / CIS controls with SARIF + JUnit gating — compliance-shaped K8s security
- [devbox](clis/devbox/) — per-project hermetic dev shells backed by Nix without writing Nix; one `devbox.json` reproduces across macOS / Linux / CI — reproducible dev environments
 - [git-bug](clis/git-bug/) — distributed bug tracker stored as git refs, sync over `git push` / `git pull`, optional bridges to GitHub / GitLab / Jira — offline-first issue tracking
 - [colima](clis/colima/) — Docker-socket-compatible local container runtime for macOS / Linux on top of Lima, with optional bundled k3s and Apple Silicon Rosetta amd64 emulation, no desktop app — container runtime VM manager
 - [melange](clis/melange/) — declarative apk package builder with hermetic sandbox + signed apks + SBOM / SLSA provenance, the upstream pair to [`apko`](clis/apko/) for distroless OCI images — supply-chain-first package builder
 - [sapling](clis/sapling/) — git-compatible scalable source control (`sl`) with stack-first smartlog, `absorb`, and transactional `undo` across rebases / amends, the [`jj`](clis/jj/) sibling shipped from a giant-monorepo lineage — modern git-interop VCS
 - [talisman](clis/talisman/) — pre-commit / pre-push git hook that scans the staged diff for credential-shaped strings, high-entropy blobs, and SSH / cloud key headers, refusing the commit before the secret enters `.git/objects` — developer-boundary secret guard
 - [yamlfmt](clis/yamlfmt/) — opinionated Go YAML formatter with a real config file, a `-lint` mode for CI gating, and a `kyaml` formatter for the new Kubernetes encoding — YAML pretty-print + lint
 - [pkgx](clis/pkgx/) — invocation-scoped package runner that fetches a pinned tool version into `~/.pkgx`, wires PATH for that one command, and never mutates `/usr/local` or installs a shell shim — on-demand binary fetcher
 - [kubectl-neat](clis/kubectl-neat/) — `kubectl` plugin that strips server-generated, default, and runtime fields (`status`, `managedFields`, `uid`, `resourceVersion`, `last-applied-configuration`, schema defaults) from `kubectl get -o yaml` so the output is a manifest you can read, diff against gitops, or re-apply, Apache-2.0, v2.0.4 — round-trip cluster YAML to readable manifests
 - [toot](clis/toot/) — Python CLI + TUI for Mastodon-compatible servers (Mastodon / GoToSocial / Pleroma) covering the full read+write surface (`toot post`, `toot timeline`, `toot search --json`, `toot tui`) with first-class multi-account OAuth and JSON output on every read verb so the fediverse becomes a scriptable automation primitive, GPL-3.0, 0.52.1 — keyboard-driven Mastodon client + automation primitive
 - [otel-desktop-viewer](clis/otel-desktop-viewer/) — single Go binary that opens the standard OTLP gRPC (`:4317`) and HTTP (`:4318`) ports, stores received traces in memory, and serves a flame-graph web UI on `localhost:8000` so verifying "did my new OTel SDK call actually emit the span I expected" is one command instead of standing up the Collector + Jaeger + docker-compose, Apache-2.0, v0.2.5 — local OTLP receiver + viewer for instrumentation devloop
 - [pgcat](clis/pgcat/) — Rust Postgres pooler + proxy that speaks the wire protocol (drop-in for `pgbouncer`-shaped configs) and adds primary/replica routing via SQL parsing, sharding by configured key, mirroring of writes to a second cluster for zero-downtime migrations, hot-reload of `pgcat.toml` on `SIGHUP`, and Prometheus metrics on `:9930`, MIT, v1.2.0 — sharded read/write-split Postgres pooler
 - [k0sctl](clis/k0sctl/) — single Go binary that reads a YAML cluster spec (controllers, workers, SSH targets, pinned k0s version) and idempotently `apply`s / upgrades / `reset`s / `backup`s a [k0s](https://k0sproject.io) Kubernetes cluster over plain SSH (or WinRM for Windows workers) without leaving an agent on the hosts, Apache-2.0 (docs CC-BY-SA-4.0), v0.30.0 — declarative SSH-driven k0s cluster bootstrapper
  - [falco](clis/falco/) — CNCF graduated runtime-security engine that hooks the kernel via module / eBPF / modern_bpf, streams syscalls + Kubernetes audit events into a YAML rules DSL (`condition:` / `output:` / `priority:`), and emits matching alerts to stdout / syslog / gRPC / `falcosidekick` for Slack-PagerDuty-Loki-Prometheus fan-out, defensive only (detection, not exploits) — fills the gap that `trivy` / `grype` / `syft` cannot (they scan known CVEs in images; falco watches what the running process actually does), Apache-2.0, 0.43.1 — kernel-level runtime detection for hosts + k8s nodes
     - [cargo-mutants](clis/cargo-mutants/) — Rust mutation-testing harness: rewrites your code with small deliberately-broken edits (replace `+` with `-`, flip booleans, return `Default::default()`, drop `?`) and reruns `cargo test`, listing every mutant that *survived* — i.e. every change the test suite did not catch — as a deterministic file:line + diff feed for a CI gate, with `--file` / `--exclude-re` / `--json` / `--list` to scope and shape the run, MIT, v27.0.0 — mutation testing that catches assertion-free agent-written tests
   - [autorestic](clis/autorestic/) — Go wrapper that turns `restic` from a procedural CLI into a single declarative `.autorestic.yml` describing every backup *location* (path + pre/post hooks + cron + forget policy) and every *backend* (S3 / B2 / Backblaze / SFTP / local / any rclone target), then `autorestic backup -a` / `autorestic forget -a --prune` / `autorestic cron` execute the whole plan in parallel with the repository password generated and stored once instead of pasted into shell history, Apache-2.0, v1.8.3 — declarative multi-backend restic orchestrator
   - [pluto](clis/pluto/) — Go scanner from FairwindsOps that carries an embedded versioned dataset of every deprecated and *removed* Kubernetes API across upstream + add-ons (cert-manager, istio) and four scanners sharing it (`detect-files` for raw manifests, `detect-helm` for installed Helm 3 releases, `detect-api-resources` for live cluster state, `helm template | pluto detect -` for unreleased charts), with `--target-versions k8s=v1.31.0` filtering to "what will the apiserver reject after this upgrade", JSON / SARIF / Markdown output, Apache-2.0, v5.24.0 — Kubernetes deprecated-API scanner for upgrade safety
   - [oxlint](clis/oxlint/) — single-binary Rust JS / TS linter from the `oxc` project that re-implements ~580 of the most-used `eslint` / `typescript-eslint` / `react` / `react-hooks` / `jest` / `unicorn` / `import` / `jsx-a11y` rules and walks files in parallel across all CPU cores, typically 50–100× faster than `eslint` on the same tree (`oxlint .` on a 5000-file repo finishes in under a second), `--format=github` / `--format=json` / `--format=junit` ship in-box for CI, MIT, v1.62.0 — fast ESLint-compatible JS / TS linter
  - [litefs](clis/litefs/) — Go binary that mounts a FUSE filesystem in front of SQLite, captures writes at the VFS layer as `LTX` page-diff files, and ships them to followers over HTTP for one-writer-N-readers replication with optional Consul / static lease primary election and a transparent write-forward proxy on each follower so the application opens `/litefs/app.db` exactly like a local SQLite while gaining hot read replicas + failover, Apache-2.0, v0.5.14 — page-level distributed SQLite via FUSE
  - [marksman](clis/marksman/) — single-binary F# / .NET LSP server for Markdown / CommonMark + Obsidian / Foam / Dendron wiki-link dialects that gives any LSP-aware editor (Neovim, Helix, VS Code, Emacs, Zed) cross-file completion for `[[wiki-links]]`, go-to-definition / find-references / workspace-rename across `.md` files, document and workspace symbol search over headings, and "create the missing note" code actions for broken links, MIT, 2026-02-08 — Markdown-knowledge-graph LSP for any editor
  - [proto](clis/proto/) — Rust polyglot toolchain manager from the moonrepo authors that reads a `.prototools` TOML manifest at the repo root and installs / shims the pinned versions of Node, Deno, Bun, Go, Rust, Python, Ruby, plus any tool added via a sandboxed WASM plugin, so engineers context-switching between Node 18 + Python 3.10 and Node 22 + Bun 1.2 + Python 3.12 repos get the right binary on PATH automatically without `nvm` + `pyenv` + `rbenv` + `gvm` shell hooks fighting each other, MIT, v0.52.4 — sandboxed WASM-plugin polyglot version manager
  - [bkt](clis/bkt/) — Rust subprocess cache that wraps any command (`bkt --ttl=60s -- kubectl get pods`), hashes argv + cwd + a configurable env-var subset, and replays stdout / stderr / exit-code from `$XDG_CACHE_HOME/bkt` until the TTL expires, with `--stale` for stale-while-revalidate and concurrent-call coalescing built in — turns slow upstream commands (cloud APIs, `kubectl`, `aws`, `gh`) into effectively-free reads from inside `tmux` status lines, `starship` prompts, cron pollers, or any "watch this command but don't hammer the upstream" loop, Apache-2.0, v0.8.1 — generic subprocess result cache
  - [aws-vault](clis/aws-vault/) — Go binary that stores long-lived AWS IAM access keys in the OS keychain (macOS Keychain / Windows Credential Manager / GNOME Keyring / KDE Wallet / `pass` / encrypted file) and mints short-lived `sts:GetSessionToken` or `sts:AssumeRole` credentials on demand, injecting them into a single child process's environment (`aws-vault exec prod -- terraform plan`) so plaintext `AKIA...` keys never sit in `~/.aws/credentials`, never leak through process listings, and MFA prompts fire only when an STS session expires instead of on every command, MIT, v7.2.0 — keychain-backed AWS-IAM short-lived-credential broker
